=== PAGE 2 ===
SIKANDER AI — Architecture Document Set · Index   ·   Page 2
1.  How to Read This Set
This set is one architecture described at three depths, plus its proofs and visuals. Read it in this order: the  
Consolidated v7 gives the current top-level shape (seven domains); the Grouping Flowchart shows it on one page;  
the Master v6 is the detailed, locked base where every module is fully specified; the Layer 2 Reference adds the  
court-operations layer and the full dependency matrix; the Interconnection Map visualises the flows; and the  
Completeness Audit proves nothing was lost across versions.
Status labels: LOCKED = finalized, not edited (v6). ADDITIVE = a layer on top of the locked base (Layer 2). CURRENT = 
the present working view (v7). The locked base and the current view describe the same system; the current view  
regroups it, it does not replace it.
2.  Document Register
The combined master PDF binds these in the order below, behind this index.

forensic audit
The six locked decisions
1. No scraper in the app (legal corpus via the Database Connector / BYOD). 2. The Committee Brain is a shared  
platform service, not a module. 3. Court-Compliance reduced to Roster Intelligence; Superior Court Companion and 
AOR Bridge dropped. 4. Finance/Accounting excluded as ERP; matter-level fee notes retained. 5. Opik observability  
self-hosted, never third-party cloud. 6. The 138/159 document taxonomy restored from the Drafting DNA, not  
rebuilt.
Recorded amendment (Layer 4). Decision #1 is amended narrowly: the app MAY fetch official public legislation — 
statutes, ordinances, acts, rules, SROs, notifications — from whitelisted government sources (module L4.9). The 
amendment does NOT extend to reported or unreported judgments (manual, L3.5) or to subscription / paywalled 
databases (BYOD). A deliberate, scoped change, recorded so the decision history stays honest.
=== PAGE 11 ===
SIKANDER AI — Architecture Document Set · Index   ·   Page 11
Recorded removals. By your instruction: (1) the Stamp-duty calculator (was in Property, D4) and (2) the Sindh 
court-fee calculator (was in Limitation, D1) are removed; and (3) the standalone read-only Client Portal is dropped 
— client-facing access is now the Layer 8 WhatsApp self-service agent (L8.1), while Client Reporting (the firm's 
push status reports) is retained. Recorded so the changes are on the record.
Deliberately excluded (still out)
In-app judgment scraper · subscription-database scraping · Superior Court Practice Companion · AOR Bridge · Finance 
& Accounting (ERP) · SaaS/Stripe billing (deferred) · formal outcome/judge prediction (reframed to advisory) ·  
Meilisearch (deferred) · custom model fine-tuning (Drafting DNA superior). Note: the only fetching now permitted  
in-app is the official-legislation statute/SRO source manager (L4.9).

=== PAGE 14 ===
SIKANDER AI — Consolidated Architecture v7   ·   Page 3
1.  In Plain Words — What This Consolidation Does
The system had grown to about fifty separately-named modules. That is a lot to hold in the head, and several of  
those modules were really the same act under different names — eight separate document 'generators', three  
separate 'viewers', a whole cluster of drafting helpers. This edition regroups everything into seven clear domains,  
each being one thing the firm actually does. It is a cleaner map laid over the same system: no feature, action or value 
is removed; every former module survives as a capability inside a domain. Your locked v6 stays exactly as finalized  
underneath — this is a view over it, in keeping with the layering principle.
It also adds the seventh domain you asked for: Trial & Case Strategy, whose centrepiece reads a whole case file and 
explains it back to you.
Before After
5 platform services + 8 Parts (A–H) + Layer 2 — about 49 named units Platform Core + 7 Domains, each holding its capabilities
Similar acts split across many modules One capability per act (e.g. one 159-type Generator, not eight)
Trial work scattered across research, drafting and court modules A dedicated Trial & Case Strategy domain that pulls them together
=== PAGE 15 ===
SIKANDER AI — Consolidated Architecture v7   ·   Page 4
2.  The Seven-Domain Model
Beneath everything sits the Platform Core (unchanged): the AI Brain, Job Engine, Observability (Opik), Notifications, 
and Security & Tenancy. Above it sit the seven domains.
PLATFORM CORE   AI Brain · Job Engine · Observability · Notifications · Security
     |
  D1 Practice & Matter        D2 Intake & Communication
  D3 Documents & Knowledge    D4 Drafting & Generation
  D5 Research & Legal Intel   D6 Court Operations & Roster
  D7 Trial & Case Strategy    (orchestrates D3, D4, D5, D6)
     |
DATA   Firestore · Storage/R2 · Database Connector (BYOD)

4.  The Six Standing Domains (capabilities preserved)
Each domain is summarised with the capabilities it now contains and the old module numbers they came from. Full 
per-module specifications remain in the locked v6 document; this is the domain-level view.
Domain 1 — Practice & Matter
In one line. The operational spine — the firm, its people, its clients, and the matter record everything else hangs 
off.
Capabilities
Capability From (v6 #) What it does
Firm & People 40 Onboarding, 8 roles, per-matter permissions, MFA/security, audit.
Client Registry 1, 3 Client profiles; the client/matter file tree; restricted staff upload.
Matter Lifecycle 2 Auto-numbering, stage flow, diary, timeline, fee notes, updates.
Evidence & Chain-of-Custody 4 Evidence register with an immutable custody trail.
Conflict Check 6 Automatic PBC Rule 85 screening; CLEAR/FLAG; partner waiver.
Limitation & Deadlines 44 Limitation Act 1908 calc; 90/30/7-day cascade. (Sindh court-fee calculator removed by 
decision.)
Client Reporting 43 On-demand/scheduled client status reports.
Primary data. The Matter record is the master object; associate identity/initials and client records feed everything 
downstream.
Domain 2 — Intake & Communication

SIKANDER AI — Consolidated Architecture v7   ·   Page 9
5.  Domain 7 — Trial & Case Strategy (new)
This is the new domain. It is where everything about a trial lives — and, above all, where you can hand the system a 
whole case file and have it understood. It does not replace Research, Drafting or Court Operations; it orchestrates  
them around a single file.
5.1  Trial Case Analyzer — the centrepiece
Purpose. Drop an entire case file into one place — every order, pleading, statement, notice and piece of evidence, 
as one bundle or many documents — and receive a careful, chronological, plainly-explained understanding of the 
case, grounded only in what the file actually says.
Why it earns its place. When a running case is handed over by a client, the first and hardest task is to understand it 
— what it is about, how it started, what has happened, and where the strength and danger lie. Doing that by 
reading a thick file by hand takes hours a partner rarely has. This turns the file itself into the brief.
What you upload, and what it returns
You upload the whole file. The Analyzer reads every document to the last detail (via the Documents domain: OCR,  
split, classify), places each in time, and returns — in order:
1. Case overview — what the case is: nature, parties, forum, and the relief sought.
2. Origin of the controversy — how the dispute began, traced to its earliest document.
3. Chronological narrative — every document and every order in date order, each order summarised with its 
effect and the stage it moved the case to.
4. Current stage — exactly where the matter stands and what is pending.
5. Strong points — the case's genuine strengths, each tied to the document or order that supports it.
6. Weak points — the vulnerabilities and gaps, stated honestly.
7. Assessment and next steps — a qualitative read of the chances, the reliefs still open, and the applications to 
file next.
How the developer builds it
8. Accept a bundle (one large PDF or many files) into a Trial File workspace; run it through the Documents 
domain — OCR, split into constituent documents, classify each (order / pleading / statement / notice / 
evidence).
9. Extract dated events from each document and assemble a single chronological timeline; identify every order 
and its operative effect.
10.Generate the seven-part overview through the AI Brain (Platform), with a strict instruction to use only the 
uploaded documents and to attach, to every factual statement, the document and page it came from.
11.Pass the extracted issues to Research (D5) for authorities, to Arguments (D5) to build the case, to Bench 
Intelligence (D6) for the judge, and offer to auto-create the matter (D1) and draft the next application (D4).
12.Store the overview as a living Trial Brief that updates when new documents are added to the file.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
ingestTrialFile POST Accept the case-file bundle into a Trial workspace
buildChronology internal Extract dated events; assemble the timeline
extractOrders internal Identify each order and its operative effect
generateOverview internal Produce the 7-part overview via the AI Brain, provenance-tagged
assessStrengthsWeaknesses internal Reason strengths/weaknesses from the record (qualitative)
linkToResearch internal Hand issues to Research/Arguments (D5)
createMatterFromFile POST Auto-create the matter (D1) from the file
refreshTrialBrief internal Update the brief when documents are added
Firestore shape
=== PAGE 21 ===
SIKANDER AI — Consolidated Architecture v7   ·   Page 10
// firms/{firmId}/trialFiles/{trialId}
interface TrialBrief {
  matterId?: string; documents: string[]; // constituent docs
  chronology: Array<{date, docId, page, event}>;
  overview: { nature, parties, forum, relief };
  controversyOrigin: { text, sourceDocId, page };
  orders: Array<{date, effect, stage, sourceDocId, page}>;
  strengths: Array<{point, sourceRef}>;
  weaknesses: Array<{point, sourceRef}>;
  assessment: string; nextSteps: string[];
  groundedOnly: true; // no fact without a sourceRef
}
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from Documents & Knowledge (D3) — OCR text, split documents; the uploaded bundle.
Writes to trialFiles (the Trial Brief); optionally a new Matter (D1); the matter timeline.
Calls Platform AI Brain (overview & reasoning); Research/Arguments (D5); Bench Intelligence (D6); Drafting (D4).
Triggered by A case-file upload; adding documents to an existing trial file.
Data required The case-file documents themselves — nothing else is mandatory; a matter link is optional.
Two hard guardrails (non-negotiable). First, grounded-only: every fact and every 'order dated X' in the overview 
must cite the document and page it came from; the Analyzer may never invent procedural history. Second, the 
strengths, weaknesses and 'chances' are qualitative, reasoned advocacy — never a confidence percentage — 
because a false number on a client's live case is worse than none.
Desired output. For a file handed over cold, a partner-readable brief in minutes: what the case is, how it began, 
every order and the current stage, its strong and weak points, and what to do next — each anchored to the 
document it came from.
5.2  The rest of the Trial domain
Around the Analyzer sit the other trial capabilities, each drawing on the standing domains:
Capability What it does Draws on
Trial Strategy Builder Turn the Analyzer's findings into a trial plan: issues to press, order of 
arguments, reliefs to seek, risks to manage.
D5 Research/Arguments; D6 
Bench Intel
Evidence & Witness Mapping Map each fact-in-issue to the evidence and witness that proves it; 
expose gaps.
D1 Evidence; D3 Documents
Cross-Examination Prep Generate cross-examination lines from contradictions in the record and 
the opponent's statements.
D5 Counter-Arguments; the file
Chronology & Brief Generator Export the timeline and brief as a filing-ready or client-ready document. D3 Indexing; D4 Drafting
=== PAGE 22 ===
SIKANDER AI — Consolidated Architecture v7   ·   Page 11
6.  How Trial Orchestrates the Other Domains
Domain 7 is a composer, not a silo. One uploaded file flows outward through the system:
13.Upload the file → Documents (D3) OCR, split and classify every page.
14.Trial Case Analyzer builds the chronology and the seven-part overview, each fact tied to its source.
15.Issues found → Research (D5) returns authorities; Arguments (D5) build the case and the opponent's.
16.The judge → Bench Intelligence (D6) adds advisory tendencies; Roster (D6) shows the next listing.
17.Offer to create the Matter (D1) from the file, so it enters the roster and calendar at once.
18.Draft the next application → Drafting (D4), citation-gated, in the firm's voice.
Because the domains talk through events and shared services, Trial simply subscribes to what it needs — it adds a  
capability as a layer, without any domain beneath it being rewritten.

it, I will raise it with you as a deliberate amendment before making it.

1.  Executive Summary
SIKANDER AI is a comprehensive, AI-powered legal practice-management platform built specifically for Pakistani  
litigation, with particular focus on Karachi High Court and subordinate-court practice. This edition takes the uploaded 
42-module Master Architecture as its base — the richest and most practice-aware feature catalogue in the project  
— and reconciles it against the six decisions locked in this engagement. The result is a system that is both the most  
complete and the most defensible version to date.
Three kinds of change were applied. Modules were added where the base omitted something essential (Conflict of  
Interest Checker, Roster Intelligence System, a named Limitation Engine, and a five-service Platform layer including  
self-hosted AI observability and tenant isolation). Modules were reframed where the base conflicted with a locked  
decision (the Multi-AI Gateway elevated to a shared Committee Brain; the 'Lottery' outcome-prediction reduced to  
advisory Bench Intelligence; the in-app Citation and Statute scrapers reduced to viewers over a Database Connector). 
And in-app scraping was removed outright, in line with the decision that no scraper ships inside the product.

Firestore collections 45+
Supported courts 20+ (roster coverage per court)
AI providers 6 (Claude, GPT-4o, Gemini, Groq, DeepSeek, Perplexity) + 
Whisper/ElevenLabs
Contract types 30+
Legal notice types 25+

4.  Architecture Layers
The system is organised in four layers. Beneath everything sits the Platform Services layer, which every module calls 
but none owns. Above it sit the eight functional Parts (A–H). Data flows through the matter record, which threads  
intake, drafting, research, documents, hearings and client communication into one governed file.
FRONTEND        Flutter (Android / iOS / Web)  +  WhatsApp channel
     |
FUNCTIONAL      Part A Client & Matter   Part B Documents   Part C AI & Drafting
  MODULES       Part D Research          Part E Generators  Part F Intelligence
  (A - H)       Part G Communication     Part H Operations
     |
PLATFORM        P1 Committee Brain   P2 Job Engine   P3 Opik Observability
  SERVICES      P4 Notification Centre   P5 Multi-Tenant Isolation & Privilege
     |
DATA            Firestore  +  Firebase Storage / R2  +  Database Connector (BYOD)
=== PAGE 31 ===
SIKANDER AI — Master Architecture v6.0 (Final Reconciled)   ·   Page 7
5.  Platform Services (shared layer — beneath all modules)
These five services sit beneath every module: callable by all, owned by none. Two are new in this reconciliation (Opik 
observability and tenant isolation); the Committee Brain is the Multi-AI Gateway elevated out of the drafting suite.
Module P1 — Committee Brain (Multi-AI Gateway)
Shared platform service — relocated out of the drafting suite. Callable by all modules, owned by none.
Purpose. The single routing point for every AI call in the system. It selects the optimal model per task type, fails 
over silently when a provider is down, enforces per-firm quota and a kill switch, and logs the cost of every call.
Why it earns its place. If each module called models directly, provider keys would be scattered across the 
codebase with no single place to enforce citation guardrails, no unified cost ledger, and no failover. One 
chokepoint solves all four — and is the one place the observability layer (P3) needs to be instrumented.
Key features
• Routing matrix by task type: drafting/opinion/counter-argument → Claude Opus (fallback GPT-4o); large-
context → Gemini Pro (fallback Claude Sonnet); fast research → Groq Llama (fallback Gemini Flash); 
OCR/vision → GPT-4o Vision; web research → Perplexity; cost-efficient bulk → DeepSeek.
• Silent failover: on primary error the fallback model is called transparently; the caller never sees a provider 
outage.
• Cost governance: per-model cost table, per-user throttling, budget alerts at 70% and 90%, and an owner kill 
switch that disables all AI instantly (or a single model).
• Citation guardrail entry point: no AI output may carry a fabricated or unverified authority; unsupported 
citations are blocked or marked [CITATION REQUIRED].
• Keys held in Secret Manager, never in code.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
committeeBrain onCall Validate quota, route by task type, call provider, log cost
internalAICaller internal Server-side helper with identical routing, no HTTP overhead
setAiKillSwitch onCall Owner disables all AI, or one model, instantly
getAiUsage GET Per-firm spend and token usage over a window
setBudgetAlerts POST Configure 70/90% budget thresholds
Firestore shape
// firms/{firmId}/aiUsageLogs/{logId}
interface AiUsageLog {
  model: string;
  taskType: string;
  tokens: number;
  costUSD: number;
  createdAt: Timestamp;
}
Desired output. A governed AI layer where every call is routed, costed, logged, and citation-guarded — and where 
a single instrumentation point covers every module.
Module P2 — Job Engine
Shared platform service.
Purpose. Every heavy or AI-backed task runs as a tracked Job rather than fire-and-forget: queued → running → 
done/failed, with logs, input/output references, a retry cap, and full audit.
=== PAGE 32 ===
SIKANDER AI — Master Architecture v6.0 (Final Reconciled)   ·   Page 8
Why it earns its place. Traceability, cost control, and the ability to reproduce or audit any AI output require that AI 
actions are recorded, not ephemeral. 'Every AI action is a Job' is a system non-negotiable.
Key features
• Job lifecycle with status, retryCount (max 3), and structured logs.
• Owner/admin job queue view with status and type filters.
• Failed-job retry with guarded maximum before manual intervention.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
createJob onCall Create a queued job of a given type against a matter
getJobStatus onCall Return a job record
getJobQueue onCall Admin: list jobs by status/type
retryFailedJob onCall Requeue a failed job (max 3 retries)
Desired output. A reproducible, auditable record of every background and AI task in the system.
Module P3 — AI Observability & Evaluation (Opik — self-hosted)
NEW — added in this reconciliation. Instrumented at the Committee Brain.
Purpose. Trace, evaluate, and monitor every LLM call the system makes: full prompt/response capture, 
hallucination and RAG metrics, dashboards for feedback scores and token usage, and online-evaluation rules 
applied to live traffic.
Why it earns its place. An AI legal product's gravest failure is a confident wrong answer — above all a fabricated 
citation. The Committee Brain guardrail blocks the obvious cases at generation time; Opik makes the guardrail 
observable and continuous, so silent drift is caught rather than discovered in a courtroom.
Key features
• Tracing: prompt/response capture including nested multi-step chains, via SDK integration in the Cloud 
Functions.
• Evaluation: LLM-as-judge metrics (hallucination; RAG answer-relevance and context-precision) run against 
curated datasets as experiments, wired into CI.
• Production monitoring: dashboards for feedback scores, trace counts, and token usage; online-evaluation 
rules flag issues automatically.
• Deployment is self-hosted (Docker/Helm) — never a third-party cloud — because traces contain privileged, 
client-confidential matter content that must remain inside the firm's environment. Hard security 
requirement, not a preference.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
opikTraceMiddleware internal Wrap every Committee Brain call and emit a trace
runEvalExperiment CI/Python Score a dataset against judge metrics as an experiment
onlineEvalRule internal Apply a judge metric to live traffic and flag failures
Desired output. A continuously monitored AI layer where the citation-safety rule is measured, not merely asserted 
— with no trace data ever leaving the firm.
Module P4 — Notification Centre
Shared platform service.
=== PAGE 33 ===
SIKANDER AI — Master Architecture v6.0 (Final Reconciled)   ·   Page 9
Purpose. System-wide alerting across matter and system events, with channel preferences (in-app, WhatsApp, 
email), delivery status, unread counts, and priority filtering.
Why it earns its place. Every module needs to notify; centralising avoids duplicated, inconsistent alerting and gives 
one place to manage channels and delivery.
Key features
• Unified event bus for hearings, deadlines, tasks, roster changes, and system events.
• Per-user channel preferences and quiet hours.
• Delivery-status tracking and unread counters.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
dispatchNotification internal Route an event to the user's chosen channels
getNotifications GET List a user's notifications with unread counts
markRead POST Mark notifications read
setChannelPrefs POST Configure per-user channel preferences
Desired output. Consistent, auditable alerting the whole platform shares.
Module P5 — Multi-Tenant Isolation & Privilege Protection
NEW / gated — single-firm now, multi-tenant when productised.
Purpose. Firm and matter scoping on every collection, default-deny security rules, and a privilege-protection layer, 
so that one firm's privileged data is invisible to another at the data layer — not merely in application code.
Why it earns its place. The shared 42-module draft carries only two passive fields (subscriptionTier, 
subscriptionExpiry) and no isolation layer. That is safe while the platform is single-firm internal, but the moment a 
second firm's privileged data enters, isolation becomes a hard requirement. This service is specced now and gated: 
dormant for internal use, activated on productisation.
Key features
• Every document carries firmId; matter-scoped documents also carry matterId and a membership list.
• Security rules deny by default and allow only when the caller's custom claims match the firm and the user 
appears in the matter's membership.
• Privilege-protection layer isolating client-confidential content; no cross-tenant read path exists.
• Gating switch: when single-firm, the tenant checks reduce to a single firmId; the subscriptionTier field is the 
only hook that survives from the shared draft and is where billing (deferred) would later attach.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
enforceTenant rules/
middleware Deny any read/write whose firmId claim mismatches
checkMatterMembership middleware Allow matter access only to assigned members
auditPrivilegeAccess internal Log every access to privileged matter content
Desired output. A tenancy boundary enforced in the database, ready to carry more than one firm safely the day 
the firm productises.

7.  PART A — CLIENT & MATTER MANAGEMENT
Module 1 — Client File System
Purpose. Document management organised by client and matter, with the three-folder structure the firm actually 
uses in practice — Orders, Applications, and Other.
Key features
• Client tree: active/closed matters and total documents per client.
• Per-matter folders (Orders, Applications, Other) mirroring the physical file.
• Document actions: in-app view, download, email, WhatsApp send.
• Document sources tagged: Drafted, Uploaded, Scanned, WhatsApp, Email, Court Download.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose

getClientDetails GET Client profile and matters
createClient POST Create a client record
updateClient PUT Update client details

getDocumentProperties GET Document metadata
Firestore shape
// clients/{clientId}
interface Client {
  firmId: string; clientCode: string; // SKD-2025-0124
  name: string; email?: string; phone?: string; cnic?: string;
  category: 'individual'|'corporate'|'government';
  activeMatters: number; closedMatters: number; totalDocuments: number;
  createdAt: Timestamp; updatedAt: Timestamp;
}
Desired output. Every document filed under the correct client and matter, in the firm's own folder logic, reachable 
in two taps.
Module 2 — Case / Matter Management
Purpose. Complete lifecycle management of a legal matter from intake to closure, with auto-numbering, an 
enforced stage flow, a logged timeline, and matter-level fee notes.
Key features
• Auto-numbering (e.g. NHS/2026/CRM/0001) from firm prefix, year, type code and an atomic sequence.
• Enforced stage flow: pre_filing → filed → hearing → reserved → decided → archived, each transition 
logged.
• Hearing dates that write to the calendar and trigger 48h/24h reminders; a day-view matter diary.
• Pending-update tasks (e.g. chase a case number) and matter assignment.
• Matter-level fee notes and disbursement tracking (the retained remnant of finance — full accounting 
excluded as ERP).
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose

Module 4 — Input & Evidence Engine
Purpose. Evidence logging with a proper chain of custody — recording who submitted each item of evidence, 
when, in what condition, and every subsequent handling event.
Why it earns its place. Evidentiary integrity is decisive in criminal, NAB and property matters; a defensible chain of 
custody is something no other lineage captured and is worth its own module.
Key features
• Evidence register per matter: item, source, receipt date, custodian.
• Chain-of-custody log: every transfer and handling event, immutable.
• Links evidence to the immutable original in the Document Vault.
• Condition/annotation notes without altering the original.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose

Module 5 — Client Intake Manager
Purpose. Turn an inbound enquiry — chiefly WhatsApp, but also email — into a structured intake record that can 
be promoted to a client and matter, with AI classification and rule-based routing.
Why it earns its place. New business arrives chaotically across channels; systematic capture and routing converts 
more of it into paying work. The current build reduced this to an unknown-number workspace — the email queue 
and assignment rules are restored here.
Key features
• Universal intake workspace visible to all staff.
• AI classification of the enquiry (practice area, urgency).
• Rule-based assignment routing to the right lawyer.
• Promote-to-client / promote-to-matter workflow.
• Runs a Conflict of Interest check (Module 6) before promotion.

Module 6 — Conflict of Interest Checker
NEW — added in this reconciliation.
Purpose. Automatic conflict screening on every new client and matter: the prospective client and opposing party 
are fuzzy-matched against all existing clients and opposing parties across every matter, returning CLEAR or FLAG.
Why it earns its place. Absent from the shared draft (only a stray 'conflict resolution' bullet). Conflict checking is a 
PBC Rule 85 ethical obligation and existed in the Firebase build; it cannot be omitted from a litigation platform.
Key features
• 80% fuzzy match (Levenshtein / token-set ratio) on client and opposing-party names.
• Screens across all clients, matters and the adverse-party registry.
• CLEAR or FLAG result; a FLAG blocks promotion until resolved.
• Partner waiver with a mandatory written note; immutable trail (PBC Rule 85).
Representative functions

Desired output. A documented, defensible conflict decision before any matter is opened.

Module 10 — Database Connector (BYOD)
Reframed from 'BYO-Storage (External)' — now also the legal-library data source. No scraper ships in the app.
Purpose. The single connection point to external storage and to the firm's external legal database or cloud storage 
(its own, or a licensed citation/statute feed). Viewers read from it; populating it is the firm's own external process.
Why it earns its place. Locked decision: no scraping logic ships in the app. Building a scraper into the product is 
maintenance debt and copyright exposure. The firm owns and controls its corpus; the standalone scraper service 
populates it out-of-band, and this connector is how the app reads it.
Key features
• Connect Google Drive / Dropbox / OneDrive for document mirroring.
• Connect an external legal database / storage for statutes and citations.
• Credential handling and schema mapping to what the viewers (Modules 21, 22, 34) expect.
• No scraping code in the app; ingestion is external and optional.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose

connectLegalDatabase POST Bind an external legal DB/feed
mapSchema POST Map connected schema to viewer expectations
testConnection GET Validate a connection
syncFolder POST Mirror matter folders to external storage
Desired output. A live, firm-controlled data source the viewers read — with all scraping kept outside the product.
Module 11 — Intelligent Document Splitter
Purpose. Auto-split bulk PDFs (e.g. a scanned court file) into individual documents, classify each, and route it to the 
correct matter — sending low-confidence items to a clerk for confirmation.
Key features
• Detects document boundaries in a merged PDF.
• Classifies each split and files it to a matter.
• <70%-confidence classifications routed to human confirmation.
• Feeds the Auto-Clerk Virtual Indexer.

9.  PART C — AI & DRAFTING SUITE
Module 12 — Drafting Factory (Ghost Writer)
Purpose. AI-powered drafting of court-ready Pakistani legal documents from text or voice, following the firm's 
drafting architecture and voice, with a human-in-the-loop pipeline and citation safety.
Why it earns its place. Litigation drafting is the firm's highest-volume, highest-skill output. This module is where 
the Drafting DNA knowledge asset is applied directly — its clause library, opening/closing banks and register 
system are the seed corpus for the firm's voice.
Key features
• Drafting from text or dictation (Whisper), confirmed before drafting.
• Firm or named-partner style, drawn from the Style Learner and Drafting DNA.
• HITL pipeline: facts → strategy outline (advocate approves) → full draft → citation gate → DOCX/PDF 
export.
• Counter-argument mode and a 7-section counter-memo.
• Version history with rollback and an approval lock.
• Citation block: unverified/overruled authorities → [CITATION REQUIRED — PARTNER REVIEW]; court 
provenance footer.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
startDraft POST Begin a draft from facts/dictation
generateOutline internal Produce a strategy outline for approval
approveOutline POST Advocate approves before full draft
generateDraft internal Draft via Committee Brain (Claude Opus)
runCitationGate internal Block/flag unverified authorities
exportDraft POST Export to DOCX/PDF with provenance
rollbackVersion POST Restore a prior version
Desired output. A complete, properly structured pleading in the firm's voice, nothing reaching paper without 
human sign-off.
Module 13 — NXR Wizard (Annexure System)

references in the body.
Why it earns its place. Annexure management is a real, laborious part of Pakistani pleadings; automating it is the 
software expression of the Drafting DNA's annexure-citation protocol.
Key features
• Auto-tag uploaded documents as annexures (A, B, C … or Annex-1, 2, 3).
• Insert and maintain body cross-references as annexures are added/removed.
• Generate the annexure index/schedule for the paper-book.
• Consistent numbering across the whole filing.

12.  PART F — INTELLIGENCE & STRATEGY
Module 31 — Bench Intelligence (advisory)
Reframed from 'Predictive “Lottery” System' — advisory only; outcome prediction removed.
Purpose. Judge and bench intelligence surfaced as analytical context: seniority, prior position, areas of interest, 
writing style, adjournment tendency, and historical outcomes before a bench — presented as advisory signal, 
never as a confident prediction.
Why it earns its place. The shared draft promised 'case outcome prediction'. That is exactly the formal outcome-
prediction the firm decided not to build: Pakistani outcomes turn on facts and advocacy far more than US-style 
statistics, and a hard number risks false confidence. The judge-tendency intelligence is kept; the prediction claim is 
stripped and every output is caveated.
Key features
• Judge profiles: seniority, prior position, areas, writing style, adjournment tendency.
• Bench memory: historical outcomes before a bench, surfaced as context with an explicit over-reliance 
caveat.
• No percentage outcome predictions; qualitative signal only.
• Feeds strategy, not a forecast.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
getJudgeProfile GET Retrieve a judge profile
getBenchMemory GET Historical context before a bench (advisory)
annotateJudgeNote POST Add a partner note to a profile
listBenchTendencies GET Qualitative tendencies with caveats
Desired output. Bench knowledge that sharpens strategy, explicitly framed as context rather than certainty.
Module 32 — 360° Deep Internal Discovery
Purpose. Find the firm's own internal precedents — past drafts, arguments, opinions and research — relevant to a 
new matter, so accumulated work is reused rather than rebuilt.
Why it earns its place. The firm's best asset for a new problem is often its own past work; surfacing it is distinct 
from external research and worth its own module.
Key features
• Semantic search across the firm's own drafts, opinions and research.
• Surfaces reusable clauses, arguments and prior compendia.
• Respects matter membership and privilege boundaries.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
searchInternal GET Find internal precedents for a matter
getSimilarMatters GET Matters with similar issues
reuseArtifact POST Pull a prior draft/argument into a matter
Desired output. The firm's own past work, found and reusable on the next matter.

  clients/{clientId}        clientCode, name, cnic, category, matter counts

SIKANDER AI — Master Architecture v6.0 (Final Reconciled)   ·   Page 38
17.  Security, Isolation & Privilege
Security is enforced in Firestore security rules, not only in application code. The ten system principles hold: no action 
without firmId; no action without matter membership; immutable originals; every AI action a Job; every derived  
output references its source; WhatsApp and app share identical permission checks; low-confidence classification  
requires human confirmation; case-number follow-up is automated; all exports come from immutable originals;  
every significant action is audit-logged.
• Default-deny security rules; firm and matter scoping on every collection.
• Account security depth: MFA, lockout after repeated failures, scoped API keys, session/refresh management 
(Module 40).
• Privilege protection: no cross-tenant read path; privileged-content access is logged (P5).
• AI privilege: Opik traces carry privileged content, so observability is self-hosted within the firm's 
environment — a hard requirement (P3).

21.  Build Sequence & MVP
Each phase depends only on what precedes it; isolation and the matter spine come first because everything assumes 
them, and the AI guardrails precede any module that drafts or researches.
1. Foundation — Auth/claims, Firestore security rules, User & Team Management (40), tenancy/isolation (P5).
2. Matter spine — Client & Matter (1,2), Document Storage (7), Conflict Checker (6).
3. AI layer — Committee Brain (P1) + Job Engine (P2) with guardrails and cost logging, instrumented with self-
hosted Opik (P3) from day one.
4. Document pipeline — Auto-OCR (8), Intelligent Splitter (11), Auto-Clerk Indexer (17).
5. Drafting — Drafting Factory (12) + Style Learner (15) + Document Type Selector (14) + 
Trusted-Source/Citation Gate (33).
6. Research — Living Research Engine (18) on the connected database, then Arguments (19) and Compendium 
(20).
7. Legal library — Database Connector (10) + Citation/Statute/Law-Library Viewers (21, 22, 34).
8. Court intelligence — Roster Intelligence (35) and Limitation Engine (44), one court connector at a time.
9. Channels & service — WhatsApp (36), Email (37), Voice (38), Translator (39), Notification Centre (P4), Client 
Portal/Reporting (43).
10.Hardening & productisation — tenant management, privilege protection, and (deferred) subscription/billing 
on the P5 hook.
Defined MVP
Modules 1, 2, 6, 12, 33 and the Committee Brain working on five real matters, with one end-to-end journey proven: 
WhatsApp intake → conflict check → matter → document upload → Ghost Writer draft → citation gate → partner  
approval → export. Three operational gates must close first: the Flutter front ends wired to this build, the WhatsApp 
webhook registered with Meta, and the AI provider keys loaded.

rule you set: finalized parts are never quietly overwritten as we move forward.

=== PAGE 71 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 2 & Interconnection Reference   ·   Page 4
2.  The Layered / Pluggable Architecture Principle
The overriding design rule for SIKANDER AI is that every new capability is added as a layer, without rewriting the  
code beneath it. A new module is plugged in; it is not stitched into existing modules by editing them. This is what  
keeps finalized work safe as the system grows, and it is enforced by three mechanisms.
2.1  Module Registry (plug-in, don't hard-wire)
Every module registers itself with a central registry at start-up — declaring the events it emits, the events it listens  
for, and the data collections it owns. Modules never import each other directly; they discover one another through 
the registry. Adding a module means registering a new entry, not editing existing ones.
2.2  Event Bus (loose coupling)
Modules communicate by publishing and subscribing to named events (for example court.checkout, roster.updated, 
matter.created), not by calling each other's internals. A new module subscribes to the events it cares about; the  
emitting module neither knows nor changes. This is exactly how Layer 2 attaches: L2.3 listens for the court.checkout 
event that L2.1 emits, and neither had to modify the other.
2.3  Ports & Adapters (stable core, swappable edges)
Each module exposes a stable interface (a port); external services — an AI provider, a court portal, a storage backend 
— attach as adapters behind that port. Swapping a provider or adding a court connector is an adapter change, never 
a core rewrite. The Committee Brain, the Database Connector and the court fetchers are all adapters behind ports.
2.4  What this means in practice
• A new module is a new registry entry + the events it emits/consumes + its own collections. No locked 
module is touched.
• A capability on an existing module (like in/out timing on Court Presence) is added as an extension that 
subscribes to that module's events and writes its own data — the locked module is unchanged.
• Removing or replacing a module is unplugging a registry entry, not unpicking code across the system.
=== PAGE 72 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 2 & Interconnection Reference   ·   Page 5

4.  Layer 2 — Module Specifications (in full)
Each Layer-2 module is specified to the same depth as a v6 module, with an explicit interconnection-and-data block 
so a developer can see exactly what it reads, writes, calls and needs.
L2.1 — Court Presence & Time-Log Extension
Extends locked Module 41 (Court Presence Tracker). v6 module text is unchanged; this adds capability on top.
Purpose. Record not merely that an advocate attended a court, but exactly when they entered and when they left 
— producing a per-visit time-log (in-time, out-time, duration) and a court-attendance report per associate, per day.
Why it earns its place. You need to monitor the full court visit, not a single check-in: when the associate went in 
and when they came out. That in/out record is the basis of a defensible attendance report, of fee justification for 
time spent at court, and of the trigger that asks a departing associate for case updates (L2.3).
How the developer builds it
1. Define a geofence per court (SHC, City Courts, District Courts Karachi 
South/East/West/Central/Malir/Korangi) as a centre point and radius.
2. On the advocate's device, detect geofence entry → write a check-in event (timestamp = in-time); detect 
geofence exit → write a check-out event (timestamp = out-time). Provide a manual override button for 
both, for when GPS is unavailable.
3. Compute duration = out-time − in-time; bind the visit to that day's marked cases for the advocate at that 
court (from L2.2).
4. Generate a per-visit report and a daily court-attendance report; expose both to Client Reporting (43) and to 
matter fee notes (2).
5. Make every in/out record immutable and audit-logged (principle: immutable attendance).
Key features
• Geofenced auto check-in (in-time) and check-out (out-time) per court visit, with manual override.
• Per-visit record: advocate, court, in-time, out-time, duration, cases attended.
• Court-visit report: e.g. 'Adv. SS — City Courts — in 09:42, out 13:15 (3h 33m) — CP 1234/2024, Suit 
88/2025'.
• Daily/period attendance report per associate; firm-wide coverage view.
• Feeds fee justification (time at court) and the client status report.
• On check-out, emits the court.checkout event that L2.3 listens for.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
onGeofenceEnter internal Write in-time check-in for a court visit
onGeofenceExit internal Write out-time check-out; emit court.checkout event
manualCheckInOut POST Manual in/out when GPS unavailable

L2.2 — Associate Roster & Internal Cause-List Sheet
NEW module. Digitises the firm's internal marking sheet; feeds L2.1 and L2.3.
Purpose. Digitise the internal roster the firm already keeps by hand — the sheet on which each associate is marked 
to the cases they are to attend — and make it self-maintaining. Each associate has initials (e.g. SS); when they are 
marked to a matter, those initials appear against that matter on the daily/weekly roster, and when a case switches 
or is re-listed to a new date the initials carry across automatically without re-marking.
Why it earns its place. Today the roster is redrawn by hand every time a cause list moves. By binding the 
associate's initials to the matter (not to a fixed date) and letting the live cause list / roster drive the sheet, the 
timetable rebuilds itself whenever listings change — and the same marking then powers the court-visit update 
alerts (L2.3) and the attendance log (L2.1).
How the developer builds it
6. Add a markedAssociates field on the matter: a list of { advocateId, initials, role }. Marking an associate to a 
matter writes here — this is the single source of truth, bound to the matter, not to a date.
7. Build the roster sheet as a generated view, not a stored table: for a given day/week, read the cause list and 
hearing dates (from Roster Intelligence 35 and Hearing Calendar), and for each listed matter pull its 
markedAssociates initials. The sheet is therefore always current.
8. When Roster Intelligence (35) reports a listing change (case switched / re-listed to a new date or bench), the 
roster view recomputes and the initials appear against the new date automatically — no manual re-
marking. Emit a roster.updated event.
9. Render the sheet by court and by associate (two views of the same data): 'City Courts — 07 Jul: CP 1234 
(SS), Suit 88 (SS), Bail 12 (AK)…' and 'SS — City Courts: CP 1234, Suit 88'.
10.Allow manual override/marking for matters not yet on a cause list, and allow re-assignment (swap initials) 
with an audit trail.
Key features
• Associate initials bound to the matter (markedAssociates), not to a fixed date.
• Self-maintaining roster sheet: initials auto-carry when a case is re-listed or switched (driven by Roster 
Intelligence 35).
• Two synchronized views — by court and by associate — of the same marking.
• Daily and weekly internal cause-list / timetable sheet, exportable and postable.
• Manual marking and re-assignment with an immutable change trail.
• Publishes roster.updated and case.marked events other layers subscribe to.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
markAssociate POST Mark an associate (initials) to a matter
unmarkAssociate POST Remove/parse a marking
getRosterByCourt GET Roster sheet grouped by court for a date/range

SIKANDER AI — Layer 2 & Interconnection Reference   ·   Page 8

getRosterByAssociate GET The cases an associate is marked to
rebuildRoster internal Recompute the sheet on a listing change
exportRosterSheet GET Export the internal cause-list/timetable sheet

Writes to matter.markedAssociates; the generated roster sheet (view); change-trail on re-assignment.
Calls / triggers Emits roster.updated and case.marked events (consumed by L2.1 time-log context and L2.3 alerts).
Triggered by Manual marking; Roster Intelligence (35) listing-change event; new hearing set on a matter (2).
Data required to run For each matter: assigned associates + their initials; the live cause list / hearing dates for the day.
Desired output. An always-current internal roster/timetable sheet where each case shows the initials of the 
associate marked to it, rebuilt automatically whenever listings move.
L2.3 — Marked-Case Court-Visit Alerts & Update Relay
NEW module. Turns a court visit into captured case updates that reach the WhatsApp group and the master matter sheet.
Purpose. When a marked associate is at a court where they have marked cases — and especially as they leave — 
prompt them for the update on each of those cases, capture what they enter, and automatically relay it to the 
firm's WhatsApp group and write it into the master update sheet of each matter.
Why it earns its place. This is the closing of the loop you described: SS is marked in cases at City Courts; when SS 
arrives or leaves, the app beeps — 'you are marked in these two cases, what is the update?' — and the moment SS 
answers, the update posts to the WhatsApp group and lands in each matter's running update sheet, so nothing is 
carried home in someone's head and every matter's file reflects the day's outcome automatically.
How the developer builds it
11.Subscribe to the court.checkout event from L2.1 (and offer an arrival prompt on court.checkin). On the 
event, look up the associate's marked cases at that court for that day (from L2.2).
12.Fire a push notification / beep / WhatsApp message listing exactly those cases: 'You are marked in N cases 
today at [court]: [list]. What is the update on each?'
13.Capture the reply per case — via a quick in-app form or by structured WhatsApp reply (parsed by the 
Committee Brain, P1). Each update is a short status: adjourned / arguments heard / order passed / next 
date, plus free text.
14.On submission, write the update to (a) the matter timeline of each case (Module 2), (b) a consolidated daily 
'Update Sheet' per matter and firm-wide, and (c) post it to the firm WhatsApp group via Module 36.
15.If no update is entered within a window, escalate a reminder (P4) and flag the case as 'update pending' on 
the roster sheet.
Key features
• Departure/arrival prompt naming the exact marked cases at that court.
• Beep / push / WhatsApp channels, per the associate's preference.
• Per-case update capture (structured status + free text), via app or parsed WhatsApp reply.
• Automatic relay to the firm WhatsApp group and to each matter's master update sheet.
=== PAGE 76 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 2 & Interconnection Reference   ·   Page 9
• 'Update pending' escalation if a marked case is left without an update.
• Every relayed update is threaded to the matter and audit-logged.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
onCourtCheckout internal On departure, resolve marked cases and prompt
promptForUpdates internal Send the beep/push/WhatsApp update request
captureUpdate POST Record the associate's update for a case
parseWhatsAppUpdate internal Parse a WhatsApp reply into per-case updates (via P1)
relayUpdate internal Post to WhatsApp group + write master update sheet
escalatePending scheduled Chase updates not entered in the window

  nextDate?: Timestamp; note: string; source: 'app'|'whatsapp';
  relayedToGroup: boolean; createdAt: Timestamp;
}
Interconnection & data

Writes to matter/updates (per case); consolidated daily Update Sheet; WhatsApp group post (via 36); roster 'update 
pending' flags (L2.2).
Calls / triggers Notification Centre (P4); WhatsApp (36); Committee Brain (P1) to parse free-text replies.
Triggered by Court check-out/-in (L2.1); manual 'log update'; escalation timer.
Data required to run The associate's marked cases at the court, the associate's channel preference, and the update text they provide.
Desired output. Every case a departing associate was marked in gets a captured update that is on the WhatsApp 
group and in the matter's master sheet within minutes — automatically.
L2.4 — Client Intake Agent (WhatsApp) — confirmation & wiring
Extends locked Module 5 (Client Intake). Confirms the already-built agent is fully ingested and wired to the platform.
Purpose. Ensure the WhatsApp client-intake agent you have already built is properly ingested into the platform: 
inbound enquiries are captured, AI-classified, conflict pre-checked, and promoted to a client and matter, with one 
continuous thread — nothing left sitting in a personal chat.
Why it earns its place. The intake agent already exists; the risk is that it runs beside the system rather than inside 
it. This extension pins down the wiring so intake is a first-class, governed flow feeding the matter spine, the conflict 
checker, and the associate roster.
How the developer builds it
16.Point the built agent's inbound webhook at the WhatsApp Super Agent (Module 36) so every message lands 
in the universal intake workspace visible to all staff.
17.On a new enquiry, call the Committee Brain (P1) to classify practice area and urgency, and run a Conflict 
pre-check (Module 6) before any promotion.
18.Promote-to-client / promote-to-matter creates the Client (1) and Matter (2) records and preserves the full 
conversation as the matter's first thread.
19.On matter creation, offer to mark the handling associate (writes markedAssociates in L2.2), so the new 
matter enters the roster immediately.
=== PAGE 77 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 2 & Interconnection Reference   ·   Page 10
20.Keep one unified thread per matter across app / WhatsApp / voice, with identical permission checks to the 
app.
Key features
• Inbound capture through the WhatsApp Super Agent into the shared intake workspace.
• AI classification (practice area, urgency) and rule-based routing.
• Conflict pre-check before promotion (Module 6).
• Promote-to-client/matter with full thread continuity; optional immediate associate marking.
• Identical permissions to the app — no side-channel bypass.

5.  Worked Flow — A Marked Associate's Court Day
This is the end-to-end flow you described, traced through the modules, to show how the pieces interconnect.
21.SS is marked to three matters. In Associate Roster (L2.2), markAssociate writes {advocateId, initials:'SS'} 
onto each matter; the roster sheet now shows 'SS' against those three cases. (Event: case.marked.)
22.Overnight the SHC/City-Courts cause list moves two of SS's cases to today before a new court. Roster 
Intelligence (35) reports the change; L2.2 rebuilds the sheet and SS's initials appear automatically against 
today's date and court — no re-marking. (Event: roster.updated.)
23.SS reaches City Courts. Court Time-Log (L2.1) detects geofence entry and records the in-time. (Event: 
court.checkin.)
24.SS leaves at 13:15. L2.1 records the out-time and duration and emits court.checkout with the two marked 
cases attended.
25.Alerts & Update Relay (L2.3) hears court.checkout, looks up SS's marked cases at City Courts, and 
beeps/pushes/WhatsApps: 'You are marked in 2 cases today at City Courts — CP 1234/2024 and Suit 
88/2025. What is the update on each?'
26.SS replies (in-app or by WhatsApp). The Committee Brain (P1) parses the reply into a per-case status (e.g. 
'adjourned, next date 21 Jul' / 'arguments heard, order reserved').
27.L2.3 writes each update into that matter's master update sheet (Module 2) and posts a consolidated note to 
the firm WhatsApp group (Module 36). (Event: update.captured.)
28.Client Reporting (43) and the matter timeline now reflect the day automatically; L2.1's visit report shows SS 
at City Courts 09:42–13:15 on those two cases; any case left without an update is flagged 'update pending' 
back on the roster sheet.

SIKANDER AI — Layer 2 & Interconnection Reference   ·   Page 12

8.  Developer Playbook — Adding a Future Module as a Layer
When the next capability is needed, it is added without touching what exists. The steps:
29.Define the module's own collections (its data), never writing into another module's collections directly.
30.Register the module in the Module Registry, declaring the events it emits and subscribes to.
31.Subscribe to existing events for the inputs it needs (e.g. matter.created, court.checkout) instead of calling 
other modules' internals.
32.Emit new events for anything downstream modules might want, so future layers can attach to it in turn.
33.Attach any external service (AI, portal, storage) as an adapter behind a port — never hard-code a provider.
34.Route all AI through the Committee Brain (P1) and let Opik (P3) trace it; route all alerts through the 
Notification Centre (P4).
35.Ship it as a new layer entry. No locked module is edited; if an edit ever seems required, raise it for a 
deliberate, recorded amendment.
Because modules are coupled only through events and the registry, a new layer is genuinely additive: the code  
beneath it is neither recompiled in spirit nor rewritten — it simply keeps emitting the events the new layer listens to.
=== PAGE 84 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 2 & Interconnection Reference   ·   Page 17
9.  Open Points for This Layer
• Court geofences (L2.1) must be defined per court with a sensible radius; verify GPS reliability inside court 
buildings and keep the manual in/out override prominent.
• WhatsApp-reply parsing (L2.3) depends on the Committee Brain (P1) reading free text reliably; provide the 
structured quick-form as the primary path and parsing as the convenience path.
• The firm WhatsApp group target for update relay (L2.3) must be configured, with permission checks so only 
marked associates' updates post.
• Auto-carry of initials (L2.2) depends on Roster Intelligence (35) detecting listing changes; where a court does 
not publish, fall back to manual roster marking.
• L2.4 assumes the existing intake agent can be pointed at the WhatsApp Super Agent (36); confirm its 
webhook is re-targetable.

Matter timeline, attendance report & client report updated; unfilled cases flagged 'pending'.
#2 · #43 · L2.1
Core data dependencies: the Matter record (#2) · associate identity + initials (#40) · the live cause list / roster (#35). Populate these and the layer runs.
Modules connect only through events and the Module Registry — a new capability is plugged in as a layer, never by rewriting the modules beneath it.
Full read/write/call/data dependencies for every module are tabulated in the Layer 2 & Interconnection Reference (§6).

1.  What This Layer Adds, and What Stays Locked
Layer 3 adds six capabilities on top of the locked architecture. As before, nothing beneath is rewritten: each new  
capability plugs in as a layer, reading from and writing to existing modules through events and the registry. The v6  
modules, the v7 domains and Layer 2 are untouched.
New in Layer 3 In one line
L3.1 Responsive / Defensive Drafting Suite Upload a case filed against you → recommended defence + full set of responsive drafts.
L3.2 Firm-Data Learning & Recall Learn from your drafts and orders; recall ‘what we did and what was allowed.’
L3.3 Voice-Everywhere Input Voice-to-text on every field; the appointed associate dictates a brief.
L3.4 Capture & Bulk Upload Photos and hundreds of PDFs at once, no tight size limit.
L3.5 Unreported Judgments — Manual Library Manually uploaded unreported judgments in a separate, searchable folder (no scraper).
L3.6 Welcome Dashboard & Secure Connections Connect Drive / Dropbox / iCloud / OneDrive / local computer, with a privacy disclosure and 
consent.
L3.7 Cloud Personal Agent Your own PC in the cloud — completes assigned tasks even when your phone / PC is offline.
L3.8 Embedded Document Viewer & Editor View/edit Word, Excel, PDF and Google Docs/Sheets inside the app; pop out to a native window 
when preferred.
One decision was recorded this round: unreported judgments are brought in by manual upload, not by fetching from 
court websites — keeping the locked ‘no scraper in the app’ rule intact.

2.  Layer 3 — Module Specifications
L3.1 — Responsive / Defensive Drafting Suite
NEW. The defensive twin of the Trial Case Analyzer — for a case filed AGAINST your client. Extends Domains 4 & 7.
Purpose. When a case is filed against the firm's client, you upload the opponent's pleading and the suite works out 
the defence: it reads the plaint/petition/application, recommends the defence plea and response strategy, and 
drafts the full set of responsive instruments — written statement, counter-affidavit, rejoinder/replication, para-
wise reply/comments, and a contempt application or counter-petition where warranted.
Why it earns its place. The system already understands your own running case (the Trial Case Analyzer). The 
mirror situation — being sued and having to answer — is just as common and was not covered. This closes the gap 
so that a case served on you becomes a recommended defence and a ready set of drafts.
How the developer builds it
1. Accept the opponent's filed pleading into a Response workspace (via bulk/photo upload, L3.4; 
OCR/split/classify via Documents, D3).
2. Extract the claims, grounds, prayer and each numbered paragraph; flag the limitation, jurisdiction and 
maintainability points available as a defence.
3. Recommend the defence plea and response strategy — preliminary objections, para-wise 
admissions/denials, points of law — through the AI Brain, grounded only in the uploaded document and 
authorities pulled from Research (D5).
4. Ask the appointed associate (from the Associate Roster, L2.2) to give a brief BY VOICE (L3.3) capturing the 
client's version and instructions.
5. Generate the responsive documents — written statement (para-wise), counter-affidavit, 
rejoinder/replication, reply/comments, contempt application, counter-petition — each passed through the 
Citation-Safety Gate (33) and written in the firm's voice (D3).
Key features
• Defence-plea and response-strategy recommender from the uploaded case.
• Para-wise reply builder (admit / deny / not admitted, per paragraph).
• Preliminary-objections library (limitation, jurisdiction, maintainability, cause of action).
• Full responsive document set: written statement, counter-affidavit, rejoinder, reply/comments, contempt 
application, counter-petition.
• Voice brief captured from the appointed associate (L3.3).
• Citation-gated and in the firm's voice.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
ingestOpponentCase POST Take the case filed against the client into a Response workspace
extractClaims internal Pull claims, grounds, prayer, per-paragraph
recommendDefence internal Defence plea + strategy via the AI Brain (grounded)
requestAssociateBrief internal Ask the appointed associate to dictate a brief (L3.3)
generateWrittenStatement POST Para-wise written statement
generateCounterAffidavit POST Counter-affidavit
generateRejoinder POST Rejoinder / replication / reply
generateContemptApplication POST Contempt or counter-petition where warranted
exportResponseSet GET Export the responsive drafts
Firestore shape
// firms/{firmId}/responseFiles/{responseId}
interface ResponseFile {
  matterId: string; opponentDocs: string[];

  generated: string[]; // WS, counter-affidavit, rejoinder, contempt ...
}
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from The opponent's uploaded pleading (via D3 / L3.4); the appointed associate's voice brief (L3.3 / L2.2); authorities 
from Research (D5); bench context (D6).
Writes to responseFiles; the responsive drafts (via Drafting D4); the matter timeline.
Calls / triggers AI Brain (P1) for analysis/drafting; Citation Gate (33); Research (D5).
Data required The case filed against the client, and the client's instructions (dictated brief).
Desired output. From an uploaded case-against-you: a recommended defence plea and a complete set of 
responsive drafts, each anchored to the pleading and citation-checked.
L3.2 — Firm-Data Learning & Recall
NEW. Extends the Style Learner and Internal Precedent Discovery — active recall, not passive storage.
Purpose. Continuously learn from the drafts and orders the firm uploads (and from connected drives, see L3.6), 
and actively recall that experience to advise: ‘we argued this before, the court allowed / disallowed it, here is the 
draft we used.’
Why it earns its place. The firm's own past drafts and the orders it has obtained are its most relevant precedent. 
Storing them is not enough; the system should surface what worked when a similar issue returns, and keep 
sharpening the firm's voice from new material.
How the developer builds it
1. On every uploaded draft and order (and on learning-enabled connected drives), extract the issue, relief, 
argument and outcome (allowed / dismissed / partly) and index it against the firm precedent store.
2. Build a recall index keyed by issue, relief, forum and bench, so a new matter can pull the firm's own closest 
prior work.
3. When drafting or advising, surface the internal recall — the prior draft to reuse and, advisorily, the outcome 
those arguments drew before.
4. Feed the Style Learner (D3) so the firm's voice keeps improving.
5. Respect the privacy filter (L3.6): learn firm know-how, never expose privileged client content beyond the 
firm.
Key features
• Outcome extraction from orders (allowed / dismissed / partly).
• Issue-keyed recall of the firm's own closest prior draft.
• ‘We did this before’ suggestions with the actual prior document.
• Advisory outcome context (never a prediction percentage).
• Feeds the firm style profile.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
ingestFirmDraft internal Index an uploaded draft
ingestOrder internal Index an order + extract outcome
buildRecallIndex internal Key by issue/relief/forum/bench
recallSimilar GET Surface the firm's closest prior work
adviseFromHistory internal Advisory ‘what we did / what was allowed’

SIKANDER AI — Layer 3   ·   Page 6
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from Uploaded drafts and orders (D3); learning-enabled connected drives (L3.6); the firm precedent store.
Writes to Recall index; precedent store; style profile (D3).
Calls / triggers AI Brain (P1) for extraction; feeds Drafting (D4) and Internal Discovery (D5).
Data required The firm's own drafts and orders; the privacy filter that excludes protected client content from any external use.
Desired output. When a new issue arises, the firm's own closest prior draft and what it achieved, surfaced 
automatically.
L3.3 — Voice-Everywhere Input
NEW. Extends Domain 2 Voice into a firm-wide input layer.
Purpose. Make voice-to-text available on every field in the system, so any information — an affidavit, a fact, a 
note, a brief — can be dictated; and let the system ask the appointed associate to give a brief by voice.
Why it earns its place. Counsel and associates dictate far faster than they type, especially in Urdu. Universal voice 
input removes the keyboard as a bottleneck everywhere, not only in the drafting screen.
How the developer builds it
1. Provide a voice-to-text control bound to every input field (a platform input service), routing audio through 
Voice/Whisper (D2), including Urdu, with confirm-before-commit.
2. Provide a ‘dictate a brief’ flow: the system prompts the appointed associate (L2.2) to speak the brief; it 
transcribes, structures and attaches it to the matter or response file (L3.1).
3. Keep identical permission checks; retain the audio and transcript with provenance.
Key features
• Universal field-level voice-to-text (including Urdu).
• ‘Dictate a brief’ for the appointed associate.
• Confirm-before-commit on every dictation.
• Audio + transcript retained with provenance.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
voiceInputField internal Voice-to-text on any field
dictateBrief POST Appointed associate dictates a brief
transcribeConfirm internal Transcribe (incl. Urdu) and confirm before commit
attachBriefToMatter POST Structure and attach the brief
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from Voice/Whisper (D2); the appointed associate (L2.2).
Writes to Transcripts into the relevant record; briefs into the matter / response file (L3.1).
Calls / triggers Voice (D2); triggered from any field or module.
Data required Spoken audio; the field or brief being filled.
Desired output. Every field fillable by voice, and a spoken brief captured and structured.
L3.4 — Capture & Bulk Upload
NEW. Extends Domain 3 ingestion — photos and hundreds of PDFs at once.
Purpose. Let users capture and upload photos, and upload large batches of PDFs — hundreds in one operation — 
without a tight per-file size limit, feeding the ingestion pipeline and the Trial and Response analyzers.
=== PAGE 92 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 3   ·   Page 7
Why it earns its place. Court files arrive as thick scanned bundles and as phone photos taken in a hurry. The 
system must swallow them in bulk, not one small file at a time.
How the developer builds it
1. Add a photo-capture / gallery-upload path that routes images through OCR (D3) into the matter.
2. Support bulk PDF upload — hundreds of files, or one very large bundle, in a single action — processed as 
background Jobs (Job Engine, P2) so the interface is never blocked; raise or remove the per-file size cap 
using chunked, resumable upload (subject only to storage).
3. Auto-split, classify and file each item (Splitter, D3); route low-confidence items to a clerk; feed the Trial 
Analyzer (D7) and the Response Suite (L3.1).
Key features
• Photo capture and upload (camera or gallery).
• Bulk PDF upload — hundreds of files in one operation.
• Large / no per-file size limit (chunked, resumable).
• Background Job processing; the UI stays responsive.
• Auto-split, classify and file; low-confidence to a clerk.

action.
L3.5 — Unreported Judgments — Manual Library (separate folder)
NEW. Extends Domains 3 & 5. Per your decision: MANUAL upload, no scraper — the no-scraper rule stays intact.
Purpose. Let the firm build a library of UNREPORTED judgments by uploading them manually; the system indexes 
them, keeps them in a separate ‘Unreported’ folder tagged distinct from reported citations, and makes them 
searchable alongside the reported corpus.
Why it earns its place. Unreported judgments are often decisive and live only on court websites or in the firm's 
own files. Keeping them in a clearly-separated, searchable folder gives you their benefit without the app scraping 
anyone. You chose manual upload — the lowest legal risk — so nothing in the product fetches from court sites.
How the developer builds it
1. Provide an ‘Unreported’ upload path into a separate folder in the connected legal store (Database 
Connector, D10); tag each record source = unreported.
2. OCR the judgment and extract parties, court, date and coram; index it into the citation store with the 
unreported flag so it never mixes with reported citations.
3. Make unreported judgments searchable in Research (D5) with a reported / unreported / both filter, and cite 
them with proper ‘unreported’ provenance.
=== PAGE 93 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 3   ·   Page 8
Key features
• Manual upload into a separate ‘Unreported’ folder.
• Parties / court / date / coram extraction.
• Distinct tagging — never mixed with reported citations.
• Searchable with a reported / unreported / both filter.
• Correct ‘unreported’ citation provenance.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
uploadUnreported POST Add an unreported judgment to the separate folder
extractJudgmentMeta internal Parties/court/date/coram

Calls / triggers OCR (D3); Research/Viewers (D5). No fetch, no scrape.
Data required The judgment files the firm uploads.
Desired output. A growing, searchable unreported-judgment library, kept separate from reported citations and 
populated by the firm — with no scraper.
L3.6 — Welcome Dashboard & Secure Connections
NEW. The first screen — connect your drives so the AI can learn, with a plain privacy disclosure.
Purpose. A welcome / onboarding dashboard where the firm connects its storage — Google Drive, Dropbox, 
iCloud, OneDrive and the local computer — so the AI can learn how the firm works and answer better, shown 
together with a clear, honest disclosure of how that data is protected.
Why it earns its place. The system is only as good as what it can learn from, and the firm keeps its work across 
several drives and the local machine. A single, friendly connection screen makes that setup easy — and the 
moment you ask a firm to connect its drives, you owe it a straight account of what happens to the data. That 
disclosure is part of the feature, not an afterthought.
How the developer builds it
1. Build a welcome dashboard listing connectable sources: Google Drive, Dropbox, iCloud, OneDrive, and the 
local computer/drive; each connects via its own adapter behind the Database Connector (D10).
2. For each connection, let the firm choose learning scope (which folders the AI may learn from) and toggle 
learning on/off per source.
3. Show the Privacy & Data-Protection disclosure (below) at connection time and require explicit consent 
before any learning begins.
4. Apply the privacy filter: private and client-confidential material is isolated to the firm; the learning index 
stays in the firm's environment (tenant isolation, P5; self-hosted observability, P3).
5. Surface a connection/health panel: what is connected, what is being learned from, and a one-click 
disconnect that stops learning immediately.
Key features
• Connect Google Drive, Dropbox, iCloud, OneDrive and the local computer.
• Per-source, per-folder learning scope with on/off toggles.
• Explicit consent gate before any learning.
• Privacy & Data-Protection disclosure shown up front (see §4).

• Connection health panel with instant disconnect.

data is protected, and switches learning on with informed consent.
L3.7 — Cloud Personal Agent
NEW. Your own machine in the cloud — keeps working when your phone and PC are offline. Runs on the Job Engine.
Purpose. Give each user (or the firm) a persistent agent that lives in the cloud — effectively your own PC in the 
cloud — that carries on with assigned tasks (drafting, research, watching cause lists, chasing case numbers, 
preparing a response set) even when the app is closed on your phone or computer, and hands you the finished 
work when you are back online.
Why it earns its place. Litigation work does not stop when you put the phone down. A long research compendium, 
a full response set, or a watch for a listing should not require the app to stay open on a device. A cloud-resident 
agent runs the task to completion and delivers the result — so the work continues overnight and while you are in 
court.
How the developer builds it
1. Run the agent server-side (Cloud Functions + Job Engine, P2), never on the device, so it is wholly 
independent of any phone or PC being online.
2. Let a user assign tasks to their agent — ‘draft this response’, ‘build this compendium’, ‘watch this matter for 
listing’, ‘chase this case number’ — each becoming a tracked Job.
3. Execute through the same governed services (AI Brain P1, Research D5, Drafting D4, Roster D6) with the 
user's own permissions and the same citation gates — the agent is a worker, not a bypass.
4. Support long-running and scheduled work (an overnight compendium, a daily cause-list watch) via the 
scheduler.
5. On completion or on an event (a listing appears, a deadline nears), notify the user through the Notification 
Centre (P4) / WhatsApp (36) and file the result to the matter.
6. Keep side-effectful actions gated: the agent prepares and delivers, but sending, filing or anything 
irreversible still follows the firm's confirmation rules.
Key features
• A persistent cloud agent per user / firm, running when your devices are off.
• A task queue you assign; each task is a tracked, auditable Job.
• Executes via the governed services with identical permissions and citation gates.
• Event- and schedule-driven (overnight, daily, on-listing).
=== PAGE 95 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 3   ·   Page 10
• Delivers finished work via WhatsApp / notifications; files it to the matter.
• Irreversible actions still require confirmation — supervised autonomy.

runAgentJob Job Execute a task server-side, offline of the device
scheduleAgentTask POST Recurring / overnight agent work

Data required The task assignment and the assigning user's permissions.
Desired output. Your work continues and completes in the cloud while your phone and PC are off, and the finished 
product — with anything irreversible held for your confirmation — is waiting when you return.
L3.8 — Embedded Document Viewer & Editor
NEW. View and edit Word, Excel, PDF and Google Docs/Sheets inside the app. Extends Domain 3 / 4.
Purpose. View and edit Word (.docx), Excel (.xlsx), PDF and Google Docs/Sheets directly inside SIKANDER AI — a 
draft or spreadsheet is opened, edited and saved in place, without exporting to a separate program — with an 
option to pop the final file out into a native Word / Excel or a Google Docs / Sheets window when you prefer the 
full desktop editor.
Why it earns its place. Today the flow is generate → export → open in Word → edit → re-import. Embedding the 
viewers and editors removes that friction: the document you drafted is edited where it lives, versioned against the 
matter, and only leaves the system when you choose. It is much easier than juggling a separate program for every 
edit.
How the developer builds it
1. Embed viewers for DOCX, XLSX and PDF, and connect Google Docs / Sheets through their APIs for files held 
on a connected Drive (L3.6 / D10).
2. Provide in-place editing: for Office files use a licensed embedded editor (Office-for-the-web / WOPI, or the 
Google Workspace editor) so edits save back to the matter's versioned store; for PDF, provide annotate and 
form-fill.
3. Save every edit as a new version (Drafting version history, D4) while preserving the immutable original (D3).
4. Offer ‘Open in separate window’ — launch the file in the native app (Word / Excel) or a Google Docs / 
Sheets browser window (including a background window) when the user wants the full editor.
5. Respect permissions and matter membership; every edit is audit-logged.
Key features
• Inline viewers for DOCX, XLSX, PDF and Google Docs / Sheets.
=== PAGE 96 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 3   ·   Page 11
• In-place editing (Microsoft Word / Google editor incorporated) — no separate program needed.
• Autosave as a new version; immutable original preserved.
• PDF annotate and form-fill.
• ‘Open in separate native / Google window’ (incl. background window) when preferred.
• Permission-checked and audit-logged.

=== PAGE 97 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 3   ·   Page 12
3.  Worked Flow — Sued on Monday, Answered by Evening
The new pieces work together on the single most common defensive task:
1. A case is served on your client. A clerk photographs or bulk-uploads the whole pleading (L3.4) — no size 
limit, processed in the background.
2. Documents (D3) OCR, split and classify it; the Response Suite (L3.1) extracts the claims, prayer and each 
paragraph.
3. L3.1 recommends the defence plea and preliminary objections, grounded in the pleading and authorities 
from Research (D5).
4. The system asks the appointed associate (L2.2) to dictate a brief by voice (L3.3) with the client's version — 
every field fillable by speech, including Urdu.
5. L3.1 drafts the written statement, counter-affidavit and rejoinder, citation-gated (33) and in the firm's voice 
(D3).
6. Firm-Data Learning (L3.2) records the matter and, later, the order it draws — so the next time a similar case 
is served, it recalls this very defence and what it achieved.
=== PAGE 98 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 3   ·   Page 13
4.  Privacy & Data-Protection Disclosure
Shown on the Welcome Dashboard (L3.6) at connection time, and required to be accepted before any learning  
begins. It is written to be accurate, so the firm can rely on it.
Your data stays yours.
When you connect a drive or your computer, SIKANDER AI reads only what it needs to learn how your firm works 
and to answer your queries. Private and client-confidential material is isolated to your firm. It is never sold, never 
used to train external AI models, never shared with any other firm, and never made public.
Everything you connect is scoped to your firm alone (tenant isolation), and privileged content is handled inside your 
own environment (self-hosted observability). You choose which folders the AI may learn from, and you can switch 
learning off or disconnect any source at any time — learning stops immediately.
One honest point, so the promise is exact: when you actually run an AI drafting or research request, the text of that 
specific request is processed by the AI provider under enterprise, no-training terms; nothing else about your corpus 
is exposed by that call. Your stored files, your learning index and your privileged material remain within your firm's 
environment.
This disclosure is not decoration; it is the firm's own assurance to its clients, and the architecture is built to make it  
true — tenant isolation (P5), self-hosted observability (P3), immutable, firm-scoped storage, and a per-source  
consent gate.
=== PAGE 99 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 3   ·   Page 14
5.  Connector Backends & Governance
The Database Connector (Domain 3, module 10) is extended to reach more sources, each as an adapter behind the 
same port, so adding one never touches the core:
• Google Drive · Dropbox · iCloud · OneDrive · the local computer / drive.
• Each connection carries its own learning scope and on/off toggle, and is covered by the privacy disclosure 
and consent gate (L3.6).
Governance: Layer 3 is additive; v6, v7 and Layer 2 are unchanged. The one decision recorded — unreported  
judgments by manual upload, not by court-site fetching — keeps the locked no-scraper rule intact. If any future  
capability would require editing a locked module's substance rather than plugging in beside it, I will raise it with you 
as a deliberate amendment first.
