2.  Layer 4 — Module Specifications
L4.1 — Review & Approval Routing
NEW. Send a document to another user for approval, editing, correction or finalisation — inside the system.
Purpose. Route any document — a draft, notice, agreement or file — to another user for approval, edits, 
corrections or finalisation, with tracked status and versioning, so review happens on the matter record and not 
over email.
Why it earns its place. A draft must usually pass a partner or a colleague before it goes out. Doing that inside the 
system keeps every review, edit and sign-off attached to the matter, with a clear trail of who changed what.
How the developer builds it
1. Add a ‘send for…’ action with a request type: approve / edit / correct / finalise, addressed to one or more 
users.
2. The recipient sees it in a review queue; they edit inline (Embedded Editor, L3.8) or leave comments; status 
moves through pending → changes-requested → approved → finalised.
3. Every change is saved as a version (Drafting history, D4); final approval locks the document.
4. Notify the sender and recipients through the Notification Centre (P4) / WhatsApp (36); keep a full audit trail.
Key features
• Send for approval / edit / correction / finalisation.
• Reviewer queue per user.
• Inline editing and comments.
• Status tracking and version on every change.
• Approval lock on finalisation.
• Full audit trail.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
sendForReview POST Route a document with a request type
getReviewQueue GET A user's pending reviews
submitReview POST Approve or request changes
finaliseDocument POST Lock the finalised document
getReviewHistory GET Who reviewed / changed what
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from Documents and drafts (D3 / D4); the assigned reviewers.
Writes to Versions, review status, approval lock (D4); audit trail.
Calls / triggers Notification Centre (P4) / WhatsApp (36); permissions (P5).
Data required The document and the chosen reviewers.
Desired output. A document routed, reviewed, corrected and finalised inside the system, with a complete trail of 
every hand it passed through.
L4.2 — Grammar, Spell & Style Check
NEW. An in-app checker for clean, error-free documents.
Purpose. Run grammar, spelling and legal-style checking on any draft, catching errors and suggesting corrections 
before a document is finalised or sent — without pasting it into a separate tool.
Why it earns its place. A clean, error-free document is a professional necessity. An in-app checker keeps that step 
inside the workflow and, crucially, understands legal terminology so it does not ‘correct’ case names or citations.
=== PAGE 104 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 4   ·   Page 5
How the developer builds it
1. Run a grammar / spelling / style pass over any draft, via the AI Brain (P1) or a checker service.
2. Make it legal-aware: preserve case names, citations, Latin terms and defined terms; suggest, never silently 
change.
3. Offer one-click accept per suggestion; run on demand and automatically before finalisation (L4.1).
4. Support English and Urdu where the checker allows.
Key features
• Grammar + spelling + style suggestions.
• Legal-term and citation aware (won't mangle citations).
• One-click accept per suggestion.
• On-demand and pre-finalise checks.
• English + Urdu where supported.

L4.3 — Letterhead & Signature Management
NEW. Firm letterhead on every letter; each user uploads a signature.
Purpose. Apply the firm's high-resolution letterhead to every letter and document, and let each user upload their 
signature (PDF, Word or any image format) to be placed on documents — including the signature of the person on 
an order, for dispatch.
Why it earns its place. Every letter must carry the firm's letterhead and the correct signature. Managing both 
centrally makes every generated document firm-ready and correctly signed, without manual pasting each time.
How the developer builds it
1. Let the firm upload a high-resolution letterhead (and variants per template / letter type); apply it 
automatically on generation and export.
2. Let each user upload a signature (PDF / DOCX / PNG / JPG), stored securely and permission-controlled.
3. Place the correct signatory's signature on approval / finalisation, in the right position on the document.
4. Expose the order-signatory's signature to the Auto-Intimation flow (L4.4) for dispatch.
Key features
• High-res company letterhead on every letter / document.
• Per-user signature upload (PDF / Word / image).
• Auto-placement on finalisation.
• Correct signatory per document.
• Secure, permission-controlled storage.

L4.4 — Auto-Intimation Notice on Order Events
NEW. When an order is passed, the brain prepares the intimation and routes it to the clerk to dispatch.
Purpose. When an order is updated on a matter — an injunction granted or vacated, an application dismissed, 
status quo or a stay ordered — the system recognises that an intimation notice should go out, drafts it, attaches 
the order and the signatory's signature, and routes it to the right clerk to print and dispatch to the client, 
defendant or respondent.
Why it earns its place. A significant order often must be intimated at once — serving an injunction or status quo 
on the other side, or informing the client. The system should notice the trigger and prepare the intimation rather 
than waiting for someone to remember it.
How the developer builds it
1. Detect order-outcome events from the matter update / order capture (L2.3, Domain 6): injunction granted / 
vacated, dismissed, status quo, stay.
2. The AI Brain (P1) decides whether an intimation is warranted and drafts the intimation notice grounded in 
the order, in the firm's voice and citation-gated.
3. Attach the order and the order-signatory's signature (L4.3) and the firm letterhead.
4. Route it to the assigned clerk's queue for printing and dispatch to the concerned party (client / defendant / 
respondent); track dispatch (D4 delivery tracking).
Key features
• Order-event detection (injunction / dismissal / status quo / stay).
• Brain-recommended intimation with the drafted notice.
• Order + signatory's signature + letterhead attached.
• Routed to the clerk for print and dispatch.
• Addressee resolved (client / defendant / respondent).
• Dispatch tracking.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose

attachOrderAndSignature internal Attach order, signature, letterhead

served.)
L4.5 — Loose Files Vault
NEW. A home for files held but not yet a case — recallable by client name or reference.
Purpose. A vault for files the firm holds but has not yet attached to a matter — court photocopies, client files, 
documents taken out from the court record — recallable later by client name, reference or any tag, and accessible 
to every user.
Why it earns its place. The firm routinely holds files that are not yet a case — for instance copies pulled from the 
court file. Sometimes they sit for a while, and later a client asks ‘do you have the file you took out from court?’ 
Without a home, these get lost. This vault keeps them findable until they become a matter or are returned.
How the developer builds it
1. Add an ‘unassigned’ upload path into a Loose Files vault (uses bulk/photo upload, L3.4).
2. Tag each file with client name / reference / phone / any recall key; make it searchable by any tag.
3. Give firm-wide read access, with a who-holds-what view.
4. Offer one-click ‘convert to matter’ that moves the file into a new or existing matter (Domain 1); keep an 
audit trail.
Key features
• Store files with no matter yet.
• Recall by client name / reference / any tag.
• Firm-wide access; who-holds-what visibility.
• Convert-to-matter in one click.
• Retention and audit.

L4.6 — Continuous Learning Engine & Governance
NEW. Governed, scheduled, multi-source learning. Extends L3.2.
Purpose. Let the administrator point the system at a folder and have it learn the firm's style — petitions, legal 
notices, the numbering / house style, and the clause library so standard clauses are reused — asking the questions 
it needs to capture that style; run learning continuously in the cloud (every 6–12 hours) from many sources (the 
admin, users, connected computers, and legal-industry updates); and govern what is incorporated with confidence 
thresholds and conflict handling.
Why it earns its place. A drafting system that stops learning plateaus. Continuous, multi-source learning keeps the 
firm's voice and the clause library current and absorbs what is new in practice — but it must be governed so that 
nothing wrong, or in conflict, silently enters the master.
How the developer builds it
1. Admin ‘learn this folder’: ingest petitions / notices / clause libraries; the system asks the finalising questions 
(numbering scheme, house style, preferred clauses) and extracts the style and clause set.
2. Scheduled cloud learning every 6–12 hours (via the Cloud Personal Agent, L3.7, and the Job Engine, P2) from 
admin content, user activity, connected computers (L3.6), and curated legal-industry updates.
3. Confidence gate: a candidate below the firm's ~40% satisfactory ratio is held for administrator approval; 
one that clears the acceptance threshold (~70–85%) is incorporated into the master knowledge as a 
tracked, provenance-stamped amendment.
4. Conflict handling: when two sources teach different things on the same point, the system does not silently 
choose — it reports the conflict to the administrator to decide.
5. Guardrail: auto-learning updates style, clauses and precedent knowledge only; it never relaxes citation 
verification, and any change touching legal substance (not mere style) surfaces for the administrator's 
confirmation before it enters the master.
Key features
• Admin ‘learn this folder’ with a guided style questionnaire.
• Clause-library learning so standard clauses are reused.
• Scheduled 6–12h cloud learning.
• Multi-source: admin, users, connected computers, industry.
• Confidence thresholds: <40% hold for approval; ≥70–85% incorporate as an amendment.
• Conflicts reported to the administrator.
• Full provenance; citation discipline never auto-relaxed.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
learnFolder POST Admin points the system at a folder to learn
askStyleQuestions internal Capture numbering / house style / clause prefs
scheduleLearning POST Run learning every 6–12h in the cloud
scoreCandidate internal Confidence score a learning candidate
incorporateToMaster internal Add an above-threshold amendment to the master
flagConflict internal Report conflicting concepts to the admin
getLearningProvenance GET Where each learning came from
Interconnection & data
=== PAGE 108 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 4   ·   Page 9
Relationship Detail
Reads from Admin folders; user activity; connected computers (L3.6); curated industry updates.
Writes to The master knowledge / clause store (governed amendments); the approval and conflict queues.
Calls / triggers Cloud Agent (L3.7) / Jobs (P2); administrator approvals; Opik (P3) measures quality.
Data required The learning sources and the firm's acceptance thresholds.
Desired output. A firm knowledge base and clause library that keep learning safely — absorbing what clears the 
bar, holding what does not, and surfacing conflicts — without ever loosening citation discipline.
L4.7 — Admin Console — Roles, Permissions & Identity
NEW. Makes the role/permission control administrator-facing. Extends User & Team (40) / P5.
Purpose. A console where the administrator manages every user's identity and the powers granted to their role — 
who may view, upload, draft, approve, export, dispatch, learn, or reach the connected corpus — so each person, 
from the photocopier / clerk to a partner, has exactly the access their role needs.
Why it earns its place. A firm needs one place to say who is who and who may do what. Role clarity is also the 
backbone of the privilege-protection and audit guarantees.
How the developer builds it
1. Provide a user directory with identity and role; a role editor with granular permission flags (view / upload / 
draft / approve / export / dispatch / learn / corpus-access).
2. Assign or relieve roles; instant activate / deactivate; every permission change is audit-logged and enforced 
by Security & Tenancy (P5).
3. Corpus access is a permission (reading the connected legal database); the app still ships no scraper — what 
is granted is read access, consistent with the locked decision.
Key features
• User directory + identity.
• Granular role / permission editor.
• Per-role access (e.g. clerk = upload + dispatch; partner = all).
• Instant deactivate.
• Audit of every permission change.
• Backs the privilege-protection layer (P5).
Representative functions

L4.9 — Statute & SRO Source Manager (in-app fetcher — official legislation only)
NEW. Amends the locked 'no scraper' rule — narrowly, for official public legislation only. Never judgments or subscription 
databases.
Purpose. Let the firm configure 10–15 official government sources — Pakistan Code, the National and provincial 
Assemblies and their gazettes, FBR / SECP / provincial SRO and notification pages — from which the system 
periodically fetches new statutes, SROs and amendments, downloads and section-parses them, indexes them into 
the Statutes Library, and feeds the learning engine, so the firm's statute book keeps itself current.
Why it earns its place. Statutes, SROs and notifications change constantly and are official, public legislation. 
Keeping the statute library current by hand is a losing battle, and because legislation is public (low copyright / ToS 
=== PAGE 110 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 4   ·   Page 11
risk), a scoped in-app fetcher is defensible here where a judgment scraper is not. This is the one category for which 
the no-scraper rule is amended.
How the developer builds it
1. Provide a source manager where the administrator configures up to 10–15 whitelisted official sources — 
name, URL, type (statute / ordinance / act / rules / SRO / notification), jurisdiction, parser and schedule.
2. Schedule the fetch (Job Engine, P2 / Cloud Agent, L3.7): pull new or updated legislation, section-parse it, 
and stage it.
3. Index new statutes and amendments into the Statutes Library (Domain 5, module 22) with amendment 
tracking; file SROs and notifications under their parent statute.
4. Verify text against the official Gazette (Trusted-Source Lock, 33); route low-confidence items to 
administrator review under the learning-governance thresholds (L4.6).
5. Alert counsel when a statute relevant to an active matter is amended (Notification Centre, P4).
6. SCOPE GUARDRAIL, enforced in code: this fetcher accepts official legislation sources only. It never fetches 
reported or unreported judgments (those stay manual — L3.5) and never touches subscription or paywalled 
databases (those stay BYOD, Domain 3). Every source is whitelisted and honours the source's robots.txt / 
terms.
Key features
• 10–15 configurable, whitelisted official statute / SRO sources.
• Scheduled auto-fetch of new statutes, SROs and amendments.
• Section-level parsing with amendment tracking into the Statutes Library.
• Gazette verification (Trusted-Source Lock, 33); low-confidence to admin.
• Amendment alerts on statutes relevant to active matters.
• Hard scope: official legislation only — never judgments, never subscription databases.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
registerStatuteSource POST Configure a whitelisted official source
fetchStatuteUpdates scheduled Pull new/updated legislation
parseLegislation internal Section-parse a statute / SRO
indexIntoLibrary internal Add to the Statutes Library (22) with amendments
verifyAgainstGazette internal Trusted-Source verification (33)

Interconnection & data

index.
Calls / triggers Job Engine (P2) / Cloud Agent (L3.7); Trusted-Source Lock (33); Notification (P4); learning governance (L4.6).
Data required The configured official sources — legislation only.
Desired output. A statute book that keeps itself current from official sources, with amendments tracked and 
matter-relevant changes flagged — judgments and subscription data explicitly and permanently out of scope.
L4.10 — Admin Action Center — ‘Your Attention Is Required’
NEW. The app tells the administrator exactly what it needs and what is blocking it. Sits in the admin/learning section.
Purpose. Continuously check what the app needs from the administrator to function fully — missing configuration 
(firm address, colour / format, letterhead, per-user signatures, connected drives and statute sources), blockers and 
=== PAGE 111 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 4   ·   Page 12
errors, and items awaiting a decision (learning candidates, conflicts) — and surface them to the people with admin 
access as a clear ‘Your attention is required’ list of corrections, so nothing fails silently.
Why it earns its place. When the app cannot perform a function because something is missing — no letterhead, no 
signature, an unconnected source, a failed job — it should say so, to the right person, rather than failing quietly. 
The administrator needs one place that says: these are the corrections you need to make, and this is what each 
one is holding up.
How the developer builds it
1. Run continuous readiness / health checks: required configuration present (firm profile and address, 
letterhead, per-user signatures, colour / format), connections healthy (drives, statute sources L4.9, 
WhatsApp webhook, AI keys), jobs not failing, learning approvals and conflicts pending (L4.6), credentials 
not expiring.
2. Turn each failed check into an ‘attention required’ item with a plain description, the impact (which function 
it blocks), a severity (blocker vs recommended), and a one-click path to fix it.
3. Show the list to users with admin access (permissions via L4.7) and notify them (P4) the moment a new 
blocker appears.
4. Clear an item automatically once the underlying condition is resolved.
Key features
• Continuous readiness / health checks across the app.
• ‘Attention required’ list: what's missing, what it blocks, one-click fix.
• Detects missing address, colour / format, letterhead, signatures, sources.
• Severity — blocker (a function can't run) vs recommended.
• Admin-only; admins notified when a new blocker appears.
• Surfaces learning approvals and conflicts (L4.6) needing a decision.

L4.11 — Send & Share (Email + WhatsApp)

is an irreversible action.
Why it earns its place. The firm constantly needs to get a document to a client or a counterpart. A one-click ‘Send 
to…’ over the channels people actually use — email and WhatsApp — keeps that on the matter record instead of in 
someone's personal inbox, and confirms before anything leaves.

=== PAGE 113 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 4   ·   Page 14
3.  Learning Governance — the Rules the Master Trusts
The continuous learning engine (L4.6) is powerful, so it is governed. These are the rules shown to, and controlled by, 
the administrator.
How a new learning enters the master.
Below ~40% satisfactory — held. The candidate is not used; it waits in the administrator's approval queue.
Above the firm's ~70–85% acceptance threshold — incorporated into the master as a tracked, provenance-stamped 
amendment.
Conflict between two sources on the same point — never silently resolved; reported to the administrator to decide.
Hard guardrail: learning updates style, clauses and precedent knowledge; it never auto-relaxes citation verification, 
and any change touching legal substance surfaces for the administrator's confirmation before it enters the master.
=== PAGE 114 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 4   ·   Page 15
4.  Governance — Including a Recorded Amendment
Layer 4 is additive; v6, v7 and Layers 2–3 are unchanged. Role and permission control (L4.7) extends the existing  
User & Team module and the Security & Tenancy service rather than replacing them.
Recorded amendment to a locked decision. Locked decision #1 was ‘no scraper ships in the app.’ By your express 
instruction this round, it is amended narrowly: the app MAY fetch official public legislation (statutes, ordinances, 
acts, rules, SROs, notifications) from whitelisted government sources (L4.9). The amendment does not extend to 
reported or unreported judgments (which remain manual — L3.5) or to subscription / paywalled databases (which 
remain BYOD, Domain 3). This is a deliberate, scoped change, recorded here so the decision history is honest.
Everything else in the locked framework stands. If any future capability would require editing a locked module's  
substance rather than plugging in beside it, or would widen this amendment, I will raise it with you as a deliberate  
decision first.

1.  What This Layer Adds
Layer 5 is about the act of drafting itself — making it faster, safer and complete. Each capability plugs in beside the 
Drafting domain and the Annexure / Indexer modules; nothing beneath is rewritten.
New in Layer 5 In one line
L5.1 Live Draft Autosave & Recovery Every draft auto-saved; recover any point; save into the main files.
L5.2 Drafting Suggestions Panel A live side panel: add this, you're repeating, cite here, rephrase — advisory.
L5.3 Flexible Draft Input Give content as a paragraph, points or bullets; the engine structures it.
L5.4 Per-Type Drafting Checklist A suit / application / petition checklist that assembles the complete filing.
L5.5 Annexure Docket & Court-Format Builder Pull annexures, number them, append behind the application, one docket — legal size for 
court, A4 for letters.
=== PAGE 118 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 5   ·   Page 4
2.  Layer 5 — Module Specifications
L5.1 — Live Draft Autosave & Recovery
NEW. Every draft auto-saved as you work; recover any point; save into the main files. Extends Domain 4.
Purpose. Save every draft continuously as you work, so nothing is ever lost, let you recover any earlier point, and 
file the finished draft into the matter's main files in one click.
Why it earns its place. Losing a draft to a crash, a closed tab or a lost connection is unacceptable. Continuous 
autosave with a recovery timeline makes drafting safe, and ‘save to main files’ commits the finished work against 
the matter.
How the developer builds it
1. Autosave the working draft on change (every few seconds) as a rolling version; keep a recovery timeline of 
points.
2. Provide ‘restore this point’ to roll back to any saved state.
3. Provide ‘save / download to main files’ that commits the draft to the matter's Document Vault (D3) as a 
named version (D4).
4. Work offline (Offline Mode, 42 / Cloud Agent, L3.7) and reconcile on reconnect.
Key features
• Continuous autosave on change.
• Recovery timeline — restore any earlier point.
• One-click save / download into the matter's main files.
• Offline-safe; syncs on reconnect.
• Every save is a version.

3. Show progress and a ‘ready to file’ state when every mandatory item is complete.
4. Warn, and optionally block finalisation, if a mandatory item is missing.

L5.5 — Annexure Docket & Court-Format Builder
NEW. Pull annexures from the folder, number them, append behind the application, one complete docket — in the right paper 
size. Extends modules 13 & 17.
Purpose. Attach the annexures to the main draft as one complete, numbered docket: pull the annexure documents 
from the client / case folder (or Loose Files), order and number them, append them behind the application, 
generate the index, and produce a single paginated docket — applying the correct page size (legal size for court 
documents, A4 for letters).
Why it earns its place. Assembling a court docket by hand — collecting the annexures, numbering them, ordering 
them behind the pleading, paginating and indexing — is slow, error-prone clerical work. Automating it produces a 
filing-ready docket in one step, in the correct paper size for the instrument.
How the developer builds it
1. Select annexures from the client / case folder or the Loose Files vault (L4.5); order them; auto-number them 
(Annex-A / B … or 1 / 2 …) via the NXR Annexure Wizard (13).
2. Append them behind the main application; generate the index / schedule via the Auto-Clerk Virtual Indexer 
(17); paginate the whole docket.
3. Paginate court filings using the Pakistani convention of ODD-NUMBER pagination — 1, 3, 5, 7 … (one 
number per leaf) — as the default for court documents; use normal sequential pagination for letters and 
other correspondence. The scheme is configurable per court.
4. Apply page-size rules by document type: court documents in legal size, letters and correspondence in A4 — 
overridable where a particular court requires otherwise.
5. Output one complete, paginated docket (print-ready PDF), keeping the body cross-references to the 
annexure numbers in sync.
Key features
• Pull annexures from the client / case folder or Loose Files (L4.5).
• Auto-number and order the annexures (NXR Wizard, 13).
• Append behind the application; generate the index (Indexer, 17).
• Odd-number pagination (1, 3, 5 …) for court filings — the Pakistani paper-book convention; sequential for 
letters. Configurable per court.
• Page-size rules — legal size for court documents, A4 for letters — overridable per court.
• One complete docket; body ↔ annexure references kept in sync.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose

generateDocketIndex internal Build the index (17) and paginate
applyPageSize internal Legal for court docs, A4 for letters

SIKANDER AI — Layer 5   ·   Page 9
3.  How Drafting Flows (all five together)
On a single application, the pieces work as one:
1. You give the facts however they come — a paragraph, points or bullets, typed or dictated (L5.3); the engine 
structures them into numbered paragraphs in the correct architecture.
2. As you refine, the suggestions panel (L5.2) flags a missing maintainability paragraph, a repetition, a clause to 
strengthen, a place to add an authority — each optional.
3. Everything auto-saves continuously (L5.1); you can recover any earlier point.
4. The per-type checklist (L5.4) tracks what the filing still needs — verification, court fee, vakalatnama, 
annexures — and shows ‘ready to file’ when complete.
5. The docket builder (L5.5) pulls the annexures from the client folder, numbers and orders them, appends 
them behind the application, paginates and indexes the whole, and outputs one docket in the right paper 
size — legal for the court document, A4 for any covering letter.
Pakistani court conventions applied automatically and overridable: court filings paginated on ODD numbers (1, 3, 5  
…) in legal size; letters and correspondence sequentially numbered in A4 — each confirmable against a particular  
court's requirement.

deliberate decision first.

2.  Layer 6 — Module Specifications
L6.1 — Print & Cloud-Print Dispatch
NEW. Print any finalised document to a connected or online printer, or send it to the clerk to print. Extends Domain 4 export / 
Send & Share (L4.11).
Purpose. Print any finalised document — a vakalatnama, petition, notice or complete docket — to a locally 
connected printer, to an online / cloud-connected printer, or route it to the concerned clerk to print, straight from 
the matter, with the correct paper size and pagination already applied.
Why it earns its place. The output of drafting is paper for court. The firm should be able to send a finished 
document straight to a printer — the one on the desk, a cloud / online printer at the office or a print shop, or the 
clerk who will print and carry it — without exporting and juggling files by hand.
How the developer builds it
1. Add a ‘Print / Send to printer’ action on any finalised document.
2. Support local connected printers (through the operating system's print path or a small local print agent) and 
online / cloud printers (an IPP / cloud-print service configured per firm).
3. Offer ‘send to clerk to print’ — route the print-ready file to the concerned clerk's queue with print 
instructions (copies, colour, single/double-sided).
4. Apply the correct paper size and the docket pagination automatically (legal size + odd 1-3-5 pagination for 
court filings; A4 for letters — L5.5); confirm before printing; log the print job.
5. Permission-controlled: only users allowed to print / dispatch may do so.
Key features
• Print to a locally connected printer.
• Print to an online / cloud-connected printer.
• ‘Send to clerk to print’ with print instructions.
• Correct paper size and odd pagination applied automatically (L5.5).
• Confirmation before printing; every print job logged.
• Permission-controlled.

L6.2 — Client Personal-Appearance Prompts & WhatsApp Reminders
NEW. Prompts and WhatsApp reminders for the client to appear in person — to swear affidavits, and for criminal bail and trial 
dates. Extends WhatsApp (36) + the Reminder Engine (44/P4).
=== PAGE 129 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 6   ·   Page 5
Purpose. Coordinate the client's in-person appearances automatically. When a petition (or any document with an 
affidavit) is finalised and sent to the clerk, prompt whether to message the client to appear in person to swear the 
affidavit; and, for criminal bail and trial dates where the accused must appear, schedule personal-appearance 
reminders — each sent to the client by WhatsApp with a selected time and the concerned clerk's number.
Why it earns its place. An affidavit must be sworn in person, and in criminal bails and trials the accused must 
appear personally — a missed personal appearance can mean a cancelled bail or an adverse order. Prompting at 
the right moment and reminding the client by WhatsApp, with the time and the clerk's contact, closes a loop the 
firm otherwise chases by hand.
How the developer builds it
1. Trigger A — swearing the affidavit: on finalising a petition / document that contains an affidavit and routing 
it to the clerk (L4.1 / L4.4), fire a prompt — ‘Send the client a message to appear in person to swear the 
affidavit?’.
2. Trigger B — criminal bail / trial appearance: when a bail or trial hearing where the accused must appear 
personally is listed (Hearing Calendar / Roster Intelligence, Domain 6), schedule a personal-appearance 
reminder for the client.
3. Compose a WhatsApp message to the client (via module 36): the purpose (swear the affidavit / appear for 
bail / appear for trial), the selected date and time, the venue, and the concerned clerk's name and number 
to coordinate with.
4. Let the user select the appearance time and choose the concerned clerk (whose number is included); 
confirm before sending (an outward message is irreversible).
5. Schedule follow-up reminders through the Reminder Engine (e.g. the day before and on the morning of), 
and track the client's confirmation; flag non-confirmation to the responsible counsel.
Key features
• Prompt at finalisation: ‘message the client to appear to swear the affidavit?’
• Personal-appearance reminders for criminal bail and trial dates (from the hearing calendar / roster).
• WhatsApp message with purpose + date/time + venue + the concerned clerk's number.
• Select the appearance time; select the concerned clerk.
• Scheduled follow-ups (day-before / day-of) with confirmation tracking.
• Confirmation before sending; non-confirmation flagged to counsel.

2.  Layer 7 — Module Specifications
L7.1 — Document Staging, Temporary Naming & Auto-Merge
NEW. Everything lands in staging under a temporary name, is routed (or the last associate is asked), and merges into the case 
folder on finalisation. Extends Domain 3, Loose Files (L4.5), Auto-Clerk Splitter (11).
Purpose. Every document that is ingested, uploaded or sent by a client, and every work-in-progress draft, is first 
stored in a per-client staging area under a temporary name; the system routes it to the right case / client folder — 
and where it cannot tell, it asks the associate who last worked on that case; when the case (or draft) is finalised, 
the staged items are automatically merged into the main case folder and the temporary names cleared. A monthly 
reconciliation, run by the administrator, finds and re-homes any file that could not be located.
Why it earns its place. Documents arrive faster than they can be filed, and half-finished work needs somewhere 
safe that does not clutter the case file. Staging with temporary names, smart routing with a human ask when 
unsure, and automatic merge-on-finalisation keeps the case folder clean and complete without anything getting 
lost — and a monthly sweep catches whatever slipped.
How the developer builds it
1. On ingest / upload — including client-sent files (WhatsApp 36 / email 37) and work-in-progress drafts — 
place the item in a per-client staging area under a temporary name (for example TMP-<client>-
<timestamp>).
2. Auto-route to the respective case / client folder using classification (Auto-Clerk Splitter, 11) and the sender / 
matter context; if a client sends something ‘regarding the existing case’, file it to that case's folder.
3. When the system cannot confidently decide the folder, ask the associate last associated with the case (from 
the matter's members / last activity) to confirm the destination, and hold the item in staging until answered.
4. On case (or draft) finalisation or closure, automatically merge the staged items into the main case folder, 
drop the temporary names, and preserve provenance.
5. Run a monthly reconciliation (scheduled; reviewed by the administrator): list unlocated / unfiled / orphaned 
staged files and offer one-click re-homing; report what could not be placed.
Key features
• Per-client staging area with temporary names.
• Auto-route client-sent files to the respective case folder.
• Ask the last-associated associate when the folder is unclear.
• Auto-merge into the main case folder on finalisation (temp names cleared).
• Provenance preserved through the merge.
• Monthly administrator reconciliation of unlocated files.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
stageDocument internal Place an item in per-client staging
assignTempName internal Give it a temporary name
autoRouteToFolder internal Route to the right case/client folder
askLastAssociate internal Ask the last associate when unsure
mergeOnFinalization internal Merge staged items into the case folder
runMonthlyReconciliation scheduled Find and re-home unlocated files
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from Ingested / client-sent files (D3 / L3.4 / 36 / 37); matter members and last activity (D1); classification (11).
Writes to Staged items; the merged case folder; the monthly reconciliation report.

Data required The file, its sender / matter context, and who last worked the case.

Desired output. Every document lands safely in staging, is routed to the right folder (or the right person is asked),

L7.2 — Client Registration & Profile (with new-case pre-fill)
NEW. Capture the client once with an auto-assigned code; every new case is pre-filled from the profile. Extends Client Registry 
(1) and Matter Lifecycle (2).
Purpose. A clear client registration page that captures the client's master profile once — assigning a new client 
code / reference for a new client — so that every new case registered for that client is pre-filled from the profile, 
leaving only the case-specific details to enter.
Why it earns its place. Re-typing client details on every matter is slow and error-prone. Capturing the profile once 
and pre-filling new-case registration keeps data consistent, speeds intake, and lets the conflict check run 
automatically the moment a matter is opened.
How the developer builds it
1. Provide a registration form with the fields listed in §3; validate them; assign a fresh client code for a new 
client (atomic sequence, e.g. SKD-2026-0125), or select an existing client to reuse the code.
2. Store the master profile in the Client Registry (module 1), feeding the adverse-party registry and corporate-
group mapping.
3. On new-case registration (Matter Lifecycle, module 2), pre-fill all client fields from the profile; run the 
Conflict Check (6) automatically; leave only the case-specific fields — case number, forum, type, opposing 
party — to enter.
4. Keep the profile editable, with changes audit-logged.
Key features
• One master client profile with an auto-assigned code.
• New client → fresh code; existing client → select and reuse.
• New-case registration pre-filled from the profile.
• Automatic conflict check when a matter is opened.
• Feeds adverse-party registry and corporate-group mapping.

Data required The client's registration fields (see §3).
Desired output. A client captured once with a unique code, whose every new case is pre-filled from the profile and 
conflict-checked on opening.

2.  Module Specification
L8.1 — Client WhatsApp Self-Service Agent
NEW. A numbered WhatsApp menu for the client — scoped strictly to their own cases. Extends Client Portal + WhatsApp (36).
Purpose. Let a client interact with the firm over WhatsApp through a numbered menu — check case status and 
details, retrieve their own case documents (including the other side's filings) as PDF, request a certified copy, and 
book an appointment with an associate or partner — with strict number-based authorization so the client sees 
only their own case files and nothing else.
Why it earns its place. Clients constantly ask the firm the same things — ‘what is the status’, ‘send me the order’, ‘I 
need a certified copy’, ‘can I get an appointment’. A self-service WhatsApp agent answers these instantly, on the 
channel clients already use, while a hard access boundary ensures no one ever sees anything they should not.
How the developer builds it
1. On an inbound message, identify the sender by their WhatsApp number and match it to a registered client 
or concerned-person number (from the client profile, L7.2). If the number is not authorised for any case, 
respond politely that access requires a registered number — and reveal nothing.
2. Present a numbered menu (configurable): 1) My cases & status  2) Get a document (order / pleading / 
response)  3) Request a certified copy  4) Book an appointment (associate / partner)  5) Talk to the office.
3. Scope every response to the sender's own cases only. The client can reach the case files of the matters their 
number is on record for — and nothing else: no other client's documents, and no law, statutes, precedents 
or firm-internal material.
4. ‘Get a document’: if there are several cases, list them numbered; on choosing one, list its documents and 
annexures numbered, each with a brief identifier (for example ‘3. Order dated 12-06-2026 — interim 
injunction’); the client picks a number and receives that document as a PDF.
5. Include the other side's filings — written statements, counter-affidavits, replies, comments by respondents / 
defendants — in the retrievable list where the client is entitled to them.
6. ‘Certified copy’: capture the request and route it to the concerned clerk to obtain and deliver the certified 
copy; confirm back to the client. ‘Appointment’: offer slots with an associate / partner, book, confirm, and 
notify the lawyer.
7. Log every access and every document sent; sending a document out follows the firm's confirmation and 
permission rules.
Key features
• Numbered WhatsApp menu — status, documents, certified copy, appointment, talk to office.
• Number-based authorisation — only a registered client / concerned-person number.
• Strict scope — only the sender's own case files; never other clients' data or the law library.
• Multi-case listing → numbered annexures with brief identifiers → PDF delivery.
• Retrieve the other side's filed responses where the client is entitled.
• Certified-copy request routed to the concerned clerk.
• Appointment booking with an associate / partner.
• Every access and send logged.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
onClientMessage internal Handle an inbound client WhatsApp message
authorizeSenderNumber internal Match the number to authorised cases; deny otherwise
showMenu internal Send the numbered menu
listMyCases internal List the sender's own cases
listCaseDocuments internal Numbered documents/annexures with identifiers
sendDocumentPdf POST Deliver a chosen document as PDF

=== PAGE 144 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 8   ·   Page 6
3.  Access & Authorisation — the Hard Rules
The whole feature turns on who is allowed to see what. These rules are enforced at the data layer (Security &  
Tenancy, P5), not just in the chat logic.
The client sees only their own case files — nothing else.
Only a number on record for a case may access that case. If the sending number is not registered for any matter, the 
agent reveals nothing.
A concerned person's number is scoped to only the case(s) it is registered on — not the client's other cases.
The client can reach the ‘main folder’ of their own cases — the case files only. Never another client's documents, and 
never the firm's law, statutes, precedents or internal material.
Every access and every document sent is logged; sending a document out follows the firm's confirmation and 
permission rules.
=== PAGE 145 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 8   ·   Page 7
4.  Governance
Layer 8 is additive; v6, v7 and Layers 2–7 are unchanged. It relies on the client registration and authorised-number  
data (L7.2) and on the tenant-isolation and privilege-protection layer (P5) to enforce scope; the self-service agent  
cannot widen a client's reach beyond the cases their number is registered for. If any future capability would require 
editing a locked module's substance, I will raise it with you as a deliberate decision first.

2.  Layer 9 — Module Specifications
L9.1 — High-Speed Bulk Upload
NEW / enhances L3.4. High-throughput uploading of large volumes.
Purpose. Upload very large volumes of documents at high speed — hundreds of files or thick scanned bundles at 
once — using parallel, chunked, resumable transfers, so an entire court file goes in fast and processes in the 
background without blocking the app.
Why it earns its place. The firm ingests large scanned files constantly; upload speed is a daily bottleneck. High-
throughput uploading turns a long wait into a background task and keeps the app instantly responsive.
How the developer builds it
1. Upload many files in parallel with controlled concurrency; chunk large files and make each transfer 
resumable so a dropped connection never restarts the whole upload.
2. Compress client-side where safe; process OCR / split / classify as background Jobs (Job Engine, P2) so the 
interface returns immediately.
3. Deduplicate by content hash to skip re-uploading files already held; show per-file progress with automatic 
retry.
4. Route everything through staging (L7.1) so items are named, filed and merged correctly.
Key features
• Parallel high-throughput upload.
• Chunked + resumable large files.
• Hundreds of files in one action.
• Background processing; UI stays instant.
• Hash-dedup to skip duplicates.
• Per-file progress + auto-retry.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
startBulkUpload POST Begin a high-throughput batch

resumeUpload POST Resume after a dropped connection
dedupeByHash internal Skip files already held
getUploadProgress GET Per-file progress

NEW. Activates the gated Multi-Tenant layer (P5) for commercialisation. SaaS billing was deferred; this switches it on.
Purpose. Turn SIKANDER AI from an internal system into a sellable multi-firm SaaS — each firm a fully isolated 
tenant, on a subscription tier, with usage metering, quota enforcement and billing, and self-serve onboarding.
=== PAGE 150 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 9   ·   Page 5
Why it earns its place. Once the platform works for the firm, the same product can be sold to other firms as 
recurring revenue. This layer adds the commercial machinery that the isolation layer (P5) was already built to 
support — it was specced and gated; this activates it.
How the developer builds it
1. Tenant provisioning: each firm gets an isolated tenant (firmId scoping + default-deny rules, P5) with no 
cross-tenant path.
2. Subscription tiers with limits (users, matters, storage, AI requests) — see §3; support upgrade / downgrade.
3. Usage metering: track AI spend (already logged at the Committee Brain), storage and seats; enforce quota 
with 70 / 90% alerts.
4. Billing: subscription management and invoicing (Stripe or a local gateway); monthly cycle; the firm's internal 
cost-governance stays.
5. Self-serve onboarding (L9.3) and a white-label option for larger firms.
6. Privilege protection: a tenant's data is never used to train shared models; observability stays self-hosted per 
the privacy disclosure (L3.6).
Key features
• Isolated tenant per firm.
• Subscription tiers + limits.
• Usage metering + quota (70/90% alerts).
• Billing / invoicing.
• Self-serve onboarding.
• White-label for enterprise.
• Privilege-safe: no cross-tenant training.

L9.5 — Cross-Platform Delivery (iOS · Android · macOS · Windows · Web)
NEW. One product, native on all five platforms, one backend, one design.
Purpose. Deliver SIKANDER AI across iOS, Android, macOS, Windows and the Web from a single codebase, sharing 
one backend and one design, with the platform-specific requirements each target needs (see §4).
Why it earns its place. The firm works across phones, Macs, Windows PCs and the browser; the product must run 
natively on all of them, not force everyone onto one device.
How the developer builds it
1. Build one Flutter codebase targeting iOS, Android, macOS, Windows and Web (Flutter supports all five), 
talking to the shared Firebase / Cloud backend.
2. Use a responsive, adaptive layout that scales phone → tablet → desktop → web; the dashboard (L9.4) 
reflows per size.
3. Put platform-specific integrations behind adapters — push notifications, camera / photo, local printer (L6.1), 
local drive / file access (L3.6) — so the core is shared and only the edges differ.
4. Keep the WhatsApp webhook, AI keys and the Cloud Personal Agent (L3.7) server-side, so work continues 
regardless of which device is on — and auth (Firebase Auth, incl. Google sign-in) is shared.
Key features
• One Flutter codebase → iOS / Android / macOS / Windows / Web.
• Responsive, adaptive UI.

2.  Module Specification
L10.1 — WhatsApp Voice — Whisper Speech-to-Text + ElevenLabs Text-to-Speech
NEW. Brings the existing Voice module (38) onto the WhatsApp channel (36) and the Client Self-Service Agent (L8.1).
Purpose. Let clients and staff use the WhatsApp agent by voice: send a voice note and the system transcribes it 
with Whisper (including Urdu) to understand and act on it, and let the agent reply with a natural spoken message 
using ElevenLabs (English or Urdu) instead of only text.
Why it earns its place. Many clients — and counsel in the field — speak far more easily than they type, especially 
in Urdu, and some clients do not read comfortably. Voice in and voice out on WhatsApp makes the agent usable by 
anyone, on the channel they already live in. (Whisper and ElevenLabs already exist as the Voice module, 38; this 
layer wires them into WhatsApp.)
How the developer builds it
1. Inbound voice notes: when a WhatsApp message is an audio note (WhatsApp Super Agent, 36), download it 
and transcribe it with Whisper (Voice, 38), including Urdu; feed the transcript into the command / menu 
logic (Client Self-Service Agent, L8.1, and WhatsApp Intelligence) exactly as if it had been typed.
2. Outbound voice: for replies and reminders, optionally synthesise a spoken message with ElevenLabs 
(English / Urdu) and send it back as a WhatsApp voice note — chosen by message type and the client's 
preference (for example an appearance reminder can be spoken).
3. Route all AI through the Committee Brain (P1); apply the same permission checks, number-scoping (L8.1) 
and citation discipline; store the audio and transcript with provenance.
4. Let each client and user set a preferred mode — text, voice, or both; default to text with voice on request.
Key features
• Inbound WhatsApp voice notes transcribed (Whisper, incl. Urdu).
• The numbered menu and commands work by voice, not only typing.
• Outbound spoken replies and reminders (ElevenLabs, English / Urdu).
• Per-client / per-user voice preference (text / voice / both).
• Same permissions, number-scoping and citation gate as text.
• Audio and transcript retained with provenance.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
transcribeWhatsAppVoice internal Whisper-transcribe an inbound voice note (incl. Urdu)
routeVoiceCommand internal Feed the transcript into the menu/commands (L8.1)
synthesizeVoiceReply internal ElevenLabs TTS for a reply/reminder (EN/Urdu)
sendVoiceNote POST Send a spoken reply as a WhatsApp voice note
setVoicePreference POST Client/user text / voice / both preference
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from Inbound WhatsApp audio (36); the client's voice preference; the menu/command context (L8.1).
Writes to Transcripts and synthesized audio (retained with provenance); the matter thread.
Calls / triggers Voice — Whisper + ElevenLabs (38); Committee Brain (P1); WhatsApp (36); permissions & scope (P5 / L8.1).
Data required The inbound voice note and the client's preferred mode.
Desired output. A client or staffer can run the WhatsApp agent entirely by voice — speak a request, get a spoken 
answer — in English or Urdu, with the same access rules as text.

SIKANDER AI — Layer 11   ·   Page 3
1.  What This Layer Adds
New in Layer 11 In one line
L11.1 Draft Reviewer / Critique Put a draft in and it says what it doesn't say, checks each contention, and suggests what to 
add / import.
L11.2 Argument Strategist The strongest line, the order to open with, persuasive framing — and where your own case 
is exposed.
L11.3 Cross-Examination Question Builder From submitted evidence, the cross-examination questions to put to each witness.

your own case, so you see the weak point before opposing counsel does. Every suggested authority still passes the  
citation gate.
=== PAGE 166 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 11   ·   Page 4
2.  Layer 11 — Module Specifications
L11.1 — Draft Reviewer / Critique
NEW. An on-demand critique station — put a draft in and it tells you what it does not say and what to add. Extends the live 
suggestions panel (L5.2).
Purpose. A place in the drafting workspace where you drop a draft (or any content) and the system reviews it as a 
senior would: it says what the draft does not say but should, marks whether each contention is sound or weak, and 
offers concrete suggestions — add this, import this clause or authority, this content should be here, this contention 
is well made or needs support.
Why it earns its place. Every draft benefits from a second read that hunts for gaps, weak contentions and missing 
points before it goes to a partner or a court. An on-demand critique station gives that read instantly, framed as 
advice you accept or reject — distinct from the live-typing suggestions, this is a deep review of a finished draft.
How the developer builds it
1. Provide a review pane: paste or select a draft; run a critique through the Committee Brain (P1), grounded in 
the document architecture for the type, the Drafting DNA, the clause library and the firm's precedents 
(Internal Discovery, D5).
2. Produce a structured critique: (a) Gaps — what is missing that should be there; (b) Contention check — 
each argument marked sound / weak / unsupported, with the reason; (c) Suggestions — add this point, 
import this clause or authority, strengthen here; (d) Repetition and consistency; (e) an overall readiness 
verdict.
3. Make each suggestion one-click apply or dismiss; any suggested authority passes the Citation-Safety Gate 
(33).
4. Keep it advisory — nothing is changed automatically.
Key features
• On-demand deep critique of a completed draft.
• Gap detection — what the draft does not say but should.
• Contention check — sound / weak / unsupported, with why.
• Add / import suggestions (points, clauses, authorities).
• Repetition and consistency review; overall readiness verdict.
• One-click apply; citation-safe; advisory only.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
reviewDraft POST Run a deep critique of a draft
findGaps internal What is missing that should be there
checkContentions internal Mark each contention sound/weak/unsupported
suggestAdditions internal Add this / import this / strengthen here
getReadinessVerdict GET Overall readiness
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from The draft; the type architecture; the Drafting DNA; the clause library; firm precedents (D3/D5).
Writes to The critique; applied suggestions as edits (D4).
Calls / triggers Committee Brain (P1); Citation-Safety Gate (33).
Data required The draft and its document type.
Desired output. A structured critique of a draft — its gaps, its weak contentions, and exactly what to add — ready 
to act on.
=== PAGE 167 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 11   ·   Page 5
L11.2 — Argument Strategist
NEW. Not just arguments — how to deploy them, and where your own case is exposed. Extends the Arguments Generator (19).
Purpose. Help decide how to argue the case, not just draft arguments: recommend the strongest line to take, the 
order and placement of contentions (where to open, what to lead with), the most convincing framing, alternative 
arguments to keep in reserve, and — critically — flag where your own case is exposed or weak, so a flank is not left 
open for the other side.
Why it earns its place. Winning turns on which arguments to make and in what order, and on not leaving your own 
case exposed. The generator produces arguments; the strategist advises how to deploy them and shows where 
you are vulnerable — the adversarial discipline of steelmanning the opponent and playing devil's advocate against 
your own position.
How the developer builds it
1. From the issues and the record, generate candidate arguments (Arguments Generator, 19), then rank them 
by legal strength and likely persuasiveness.
2. Recommend the opening and the order — which contention to lead with, how to build, where to place the 
strongest point.
3. Frame each contention for maximum persuasiveness; keep weaker or alternative arguments in reserve 
rather than up front.
4. Run the adversarial pass: steelman the opponent and play devil's advocate against your own case; flag the 
exposed flank — the weak point the other side will attack — and suggest how to shore it up or avoid 
opening it.
5. Cross-check the bench (Bench Intelligence, D6) for what tends to persuade before that court.
Key features
• Strongest-line recommendation.
• Contention order and placement — where to open, what to lead with.
• Persuasive framing per contention.
• Reserve / alternative arguments held back.
• Exposed-flank and weak-point flagging — do not leave your side open.
• Bench-aware; advisory.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
rankArguments internal Rank by strength and persuasiveness
recommendOrder internal Opening and order of contentions
frameForPersuasion internal Most convincing framing
flagExposedFlanks internal Where the firm's own case is weak / open
reserveArguments internal Hold back weaker/alternative lines
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from The issues, the record and the generated arguments (19 / D5); Bench Intelligence (D6).
Writes to The deployment plan and the weak-point map.
Calls / triggers Committee Brain (P1); Citation-Safety Gate (33).
Data required The issues and the record.
Desired output. A deployment plan for the argument — what to lead with, in what order, framed to persuade — 
and a clear map of where your own case is exposed.
L11.3 — Cross-Examination Question Builder
NEW. For trials — once evidence is submitted, the cross-examination to put. Extends Cross-Examination Prep (Domain 7).
=== PAGE 168 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 11   ·   Page 6
Purpose. Once evidence is submitted in a trial, generate the cross-examination questions to put to the other side's 
witnesses — built from the contradictions, omissions and admissions in their evidence, and from the facts in issue.
Why it earns its place. Effective cross-examination turns on the contradictions and gaps in the other side's 
evidence. Surfacing them as ready, sequenced questions saves preparation and sharpens the cross.
How the developer builds it
1. Read the submitted evidence, the witness statements and the record (via the case file, D3 / L3.1 / Domain 
7).
2. Identify contradictions, omissions and admissions in the other side's evidence, and against the firm's own 
case.
3. Generate targeted cross-examination questions per witness, grouped by point and in a logical sequence; for 
each, note the answer being sought and the document or line it rests on (provenance).
4. Flag risky, open-ended questions that could help the witness — advisory, so counsel decides.
Key features
• Per-witness cross-examination questions.
• Built from contradictions / omissions / admissions in submitted evidence.
• Grouped by point, in a logical sequence.
• Each question tied to the document or line, with the answer sought.
• Risky / open-question flags.
• Grounded in the actual evidence — never invented facts.
Representative functions

sequenceByWitness internal Group and order per witness

Interconnection & data

Writes to The witness-by-witness cross-examination set.
Calls / triggers Committee Brain (P1); Bench context (D6).

Desired output. A witness-by-witness set of cross-examination questions grounded in the record, ready to take 
into trial.

2.  Layer 13 — Module Specifications
L13.1 — Universal Field Input
NEW. Every field accepts text, paste, voice, PDF or photo. Unifies Voice (L3.3) and Capture (L3.4) at the field level.
Purpose. Make every input field in the system accept content in any form — type it, paste it, dictate it (voice-to-
text), upload a PDF, or take / upload a photo — with the system OCR-ing or transcribing the input straight into the 
field.
Why it earns its place. Information reaches counsel in every form — a photo of a document, a voice note, a pasted 
paragraph, a PDF. Forcing all of it through the keyboard is the bottleneck. A single universal input control on every 
field removes it.
How the developer builds it
1. Provide a reusable field-input control offering: type / paste; voice-to-text (Whisper, L3.3, including Urdu); 
PDF upload (OCR → text into the field); photo / image capture (OCR → text into the field).
2. Confirm-before-commit so the extracted / transcribed text is checked before it fills the field.
3. Use the same control across all forms — matters, agreements, affidavits, notices, registration — so every 
field behaves identically.
Key features
• Type / paste on every field.
• Voice-to-text (including Urdu).
• PDF upload → OCR → field.
• Photo / image capture → OCR → field.
• Confirm before commit.
• Consistent across every form in the system.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose

L13.2 — ID / CNIC Capture & Party Auto-Fill
NEW. Scan a CNIC / ID card and auto-fill the party's particulars into any instrument.
Purpose. Photograph or scan a CNIC or ID card (front and back), extract the party's particulars — name, father's / 
husband's name, CNIC number, date of birth and address — and auto-populate the party fields in agreements, 
affidavits, powers of attorney, receipts and client registration.
Why it earns its place. Every instrument needs the parties' exact particulars, and typing a CNIC number and name 
correctly is error-prone. Capturing them straight from the ID card is faster and more accurate, and it feeds 
registration and drafting alike.
=== PAGE 182 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 13   ·   Page 5
How the developer builds it

CNIC format.
3. Let the user confirm the extracted values, then auto-fill the party block in the target instrument — 
agreements (L12), affidavits (29), powers of attorney (28), receipts (L12.4) and client registration (L7.2).
4. Store the ID securely and privilege-scoped (Security & Tenancy, P5), with the client's consent; never share it.

• OCR extraction of party particulars.

L13.3 — Brief-to-Agreement Assembly
NEW. From a small brief plus the parties to a full, best-clause agreement. Ties L12.1 + the clause library + the Drafting DNA.
Purpose. Give the system a short brief of the deal — the parties (auto-filled from ID capture), what is being agreed 
and the key commercial terms — choose the recitals and conditions, and it develops the full agreement, selecting 
the best clauses and segments from the clause library and the Drafting DNA.
Why it earns its place. Counsel thinks in terms of the deal's essence, not clause by clause. From a brief plus the 
parties, the system should assemble the right agreement with the best-fitting clauses, ready to refine — turning a 
two-line instruction and an ID scan into a complete draft.
How the developer builds it
1. Capture a short brief in any input mode (type / paste / voice / photo, L13.1) and the party details (L13.2).
2. Select the agreement type and the recitals and conditions to include.
3. The Corporate Agreements Engine (L12.1) assembles the full agreement, picking the strongest matching 
clauses from the clause library (30) and the Drafting DNA.
4. Run the reviewer / critique (L11.1) and the Citation-Safety Gate (33); present the draft for refinement 
before finalising.

LAYER 14 — PERSONALIZATION, USER MEMORY & MASTER SELF-UPDATE
Additive layer — the system learns the individual, and the master keeps itself current. v6 / v7 and Layers 2–13 
unchanged.
Advocate Shamail Sikander · NHS Legal, Karachi · 6 July 2026
=== PAGE 186 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 14   ·   Page 2
Table of Contents
=== PAGE 187 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 14   ·   Page 3
1.  What This Layer Adds — and What Is Genuinely New
Firm-level continuous learning already exists (Layer 4, L4.6). Layer 14 adds two things that were not in any earlier  
version: a personal memory for each individual user, and a master framework that updates itself automatically. The 
novelty is honest — per-user, LLM-style memory and an auto-evolving master are new to this build.
New in Layer 14 In one line
L14.1 User Personalization, Customization & Memory A per-user memory (like an LLM's) — learns and remembers the individual; view / edit / 
reset.
L14.2 Master Framework Auto-Update The master (clauses, style, taxonomy) updates itself from use, governed by the L4.6 
thresholds.
=== PAGE 188 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 14   ·   Page 4
2.  Layer 14 — Module Specifications
L14.1 — User Personalization, Customization & Memory
NEW. A per-user memory — the way an LLM remembers you — for each user. Genuinely new to this build.
Purpose. Give each user a personal profile the system learns and remembers — their drafting voice and 
preferences, tone, working patterns, frequent choices, and the personal notes they choose to store — so the 
system adapts to the individual (not only the firm), and let the user customise their workspace, defaults and 
shortcuts. The user can view, edit, add to, or reset their memory, exactly as with an LLM.
Why it earns its place. A firm-wide style is not the same as how a particular partner or associate actually works. 
Remembering the individual — their preferred phrasings, the clauses they reach for, their layout, their language — 
makes the system feel like it knows them and speeds their work. Crucially, it is transparent and under the user's 
own control, not a hidden profile.
How the developer builds it
1. Maintain a per-user memory: preferences (layout, defaults, shortcuts, language), drafting voice / tone 
signals learned from the user's edits, frequent choices, and user-entered personal notes (‘remember that 
I…’).
2. Adapt to it: personalise the dashboard (L9.4), the drafting suggestions (L5.2), the reviewer (L11.1) and the 
defaults for that user.
3. Make it transparent and editable: the user can view their whole memory, add, edit or delete entries, and 
reset it — like managing an LLM's memory.
4. Keep it private to the user and firm (Security & Tenancy, P5); a user's personal memory is never shared, and 
it feeds the firm master only in de-identified, governed form (L14.2).
Key features
• Per-user memory: preferences, voice/tone, patterns, personal notes.
• LLM-style view / edit / add / reset — fully under the user's control.
• Personalises the dashboard, suggestions, reviewer and defaults.
• Per-user language and tone.
• Private to the user and firm; never shared.
• Feeds the firm master only de-identified and governed (L14.2).
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
getUserMemory GET Show the user their whole memory
updateUserMemory POST Edit a memory entry
addMemoryNote POST Add a personal note ('remember that…')
resetUserMemory POST Clear / reset memory
personalizeFor internal Adapt UI, suggestions, defaults to the user
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from The user's activity, edits and preferences; their personal notes.
Writes to The per-user memory profile.
Calls / triggers Personalises Dashboard (L9.4), Suggestions (L5.2), Reviewer (L11.1), Drafting (D4); private via P5.
Data required The individual user's behaviour and the notes they choose to store.
Desired output. A system that adapts to each user's style and preferences and remembers what they ask it to — 
transparently, and entirely under their control.
=== PAGE 189 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 14   ·   Page 5
L14.2 — Master Framework Auto-Update
NEW / extends the Continuous Learning Engine (L4.6). The master keeps itself current — automatically, but governed.
Purpose. Let the master framework — the firm's clause library, house style, document taxonomy and precedent 
knowledge — update itself automatically from aggregate use across the firm and its users, governed by the same 
confidence thresholds and conflict handling, and informed by the personalisation signals, so the master stays 
current with how the firm actually works.
Why it earns its place. The firm's best practices evolve with every matter. Rather than waiting for someone to 
curate the master by hand, the system proposes and — above the firm's threshold — incorporates improvements 
automatically, while never letting anything wrong, conflicting, or legally substantive slip in unreviewed. This closes 
the loop from daily use back into the master.
How the developer builds it
1. Aggregate de-identified signals from use — which clauses are chosen and edited, which phrasings survive 
review, recurring new content — across users and matters (drawing on L14.1 in de-identified form only).
2. Propose master updates through the Continuous Learning Engine (L4.6): below ~40% satisfactory held for 
administrator approval; above the firm's ~70–85% acceptance threshold incorporated as a tracked, 
provenance-stamped amendment; conflicts reported to the administrator.
3. Auto-update the clause library, style profile and taxonomy where the bar is cleared; version every change.
4. Hold the citation discipline: any change touching legal substance surfaces for the administrator's 
confirmation before it enters the master, and citation verification is never auto-relaxed.
5. Show the master's change history and the personalisation influence, so the firm can see why the master 
changed.
Key features
• Master self-updates from aggregate, de-identified use.
• Governed by the L4.6 thresholds (<40% hold; ≥70–85% incorporate).
• Conflicts reported to the administrator.
• Auto-updates clause library, style and taxonomy; every change versioned + provenance.
• Legal-substance changes still confirmed by the administrator.
• Visible master change history.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
aggregateUsageSignals scheduled Collect de-identified usage signals
proposeMasterUpdate internal Propose a master change
incorporateOnThreshold internal Incorporate above the acceptance bar
versionMasterChange internal Version + provenance every change
getMasterChangeHistory GET Why the master changed
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from De-identified usage signals (from L14.1 and use across D4/D5/L11); the current master.
Writes to Governed master amendments (clause library, style, taxonomy); the change history.
Calls / triggers Continuous Learning Engine (L4.6); Opik (P3) measures quality; administrator approvals.
Data required Aggregate, de-identified firm usage and the acceptance thresholds.
Desired output. A master framework that keeps itself current from how the firm actually works — safely, 
transparently, and without ever loosening citation discipline.
=== PAGE 190 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 14   ·   Page 6
3.  The Two Guardrails That Make This Safe
Personal stays personal. A user's memory is private to them and the firm, under their own view/edit/reset 
control, and never shared. Personal or client-confidential content never leaks into the shared master — only 
de-identified, governed signals do.
The master never drifts unsupervised. Auto-update is governed by the L4.6 thresholds — below ~40% held for 
the administrator, above ~70–85% incorporated as a tracked amendment, conflicts reported. Anything 
touching legal substance is confirmed by the administrator, and citation verification is never auto-relaxed.
Layer 14 is additive; v6, v7 and Layers 2–13 are unchanged. If any future capability would require editing a locked  
module's substance, I will raise it with you as a deliberate decision first.

2.  Layer 15 — Module Specifications
L15.1 — Signature Block & Sign-Off on Letters
NEW / extends Letterhead & Signature Management (L4.3). The full sign-off under every letter.
Purpose. Place a complete sign-off block at the foot of every letter and document — the person's signed-signature 
image, then their name, then their designation — drawn from the user's uploaded signature (PDF or a common 
image format), configurable per user and applied automatically on finalisation.
Why it earns its place. Every letter closes with a signature block: the signature, the name, and the designation. 
Automating the whole block — not just the image — means every letter goes out correctly signed off, in the right 
person's name and title, without manual typing.
How the developer builds it
1. Extend the signature store (L4.3): each user uploads their signature (PDF / PNG / JPG or another supported 
image) and sets the name and designation lines for the block (and an optional firm line).
2. On finalising a letter or document, place the sign-off block at the foot — signature image, then the name 
line, then the designation line (for example ‘Advocate, High Court’ or ‘Senior Partner, NHS Legal’) — 
consistently formatted.
3. Resolve the correct signatory for the document (the author or the assigned partner); support a ‘for [Firm]’ 
prefix where needed.
4. Apply automatically on export and print (L6.1); permission-controlled.
Key features
• Full sign-off block — signed signature + name + designation — under every letter.
• Per-user signature, name and title (and optional firm line).
• PDF and image signature formats supported.
• Correct signatory resolved per document; ‘for [Firm]’ prefix where needed.
• Auto-applied on finalise / export / print.
• Permission-controlled.
