1.  What This Layer Does
Layer 16 is the first layer that acts on the forensic audit rather than adding practice features. It gives you control  
over which model runs (or whether any model runs), removes the Committee Brain as a single point of failure, and 
consolidates the overlapping components the audit found — while adding the one genuine gap it surfaced (selecting 
client documents for drafting).
In Layer 16 In one line Audit item
L16.1 Model Selection & Deterministic-First Auto or Advanced model choice; no AI when the clause library 
suffices
§6, §8, §10
L16.2 Committee Brain Resilience One logical brain, many stateless instances; degrade gracefully §9 hazard, R3
L16.3 Corrective Consolidation Learning 5→1, review 3→1, client 3→1; single master write path §13, R2, R4
L16.4 Client Document → Drafting Select which client documents the drafter uses §13 gap
=== PAGE 200 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 16   ·   Page 4
2.  Layer 16 — Module Specifications
L16.1 — AI Model Selection & Deterministic-First Routing
NEW / enhances the Committee Brain (P1). Choose the model — or use no AI at all.
Purpose. Wherever AI is used, give the user the full choice of models in two modes — Automatic (the software 
picks the best model for the task) and Advanced (the user selects the exact model per task) — and make the 
system smart enough NOT to use AI at all when a task can be produced deterministically from the clause library, 
templates and captured facts.
Why it earns its place. Two things the forensic audit flagged: over-use of AI (cost and latency), and control. Not 
every application needs a model — a standard instrument that can be assembled from the clause library should be 
produced without an AI call. And where AI is used, the firm should be able to pick the model or let the system 
choose.
How the developer builds it
1. Expose model choice at every AI touchpoint: Automatic — the Committee Brain routes to the best model 
per task type, as today; or Advanced — a picker listing every available model (Claude Opus / Sonnet, 
GPT-4o, Gemini, Groq / Llama, DeepSeek, Perplexity, and any added such as Grok) selectable per task.
2. Deterministic-first gate: BEFORE any AI call, check whether the task can be satisfied without a model — a 
standard instrument assembled from the clause library / templates with the captured facts, a lookup, or a 
calculation. If yes, produce it deterministically and skip the model entirely; if no, route to AI.
3. Show the user which path was taken (deterministic, or which model) and the cost.
4. Apply the citation-safety gate and governance regardless of path.
Key features
• Automatic vs Advanced model modes.
• Full model picker per task (Advanced) — any available model.
• Deterministic-first: no AI when the clause library / templates suffice.
• Path and cost shown to the user.
• Citation gate and governance applied on every path.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
setModelMode POST Auto (system picks) or Advanced (manual)
pickModelForTask POST Choose a specific model in Advanced mode
deterministicFirstCheck internal Can this be produced without AI?
routeOrAssemble internal Assemble deterministically, or route to the model
showPathAndCost GET Which path / model was used and the cost
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from The task; the clause library (30) / templates; available models.
Writes to The output; the path + cost record.
Calls / triggers Committee Brain (P1); Citation-Safety Gate (33).
Data required The task and the user's model-mode preference.
Desired output. Every AI touchpoint offers automatic or manual model choice — and simple, standard work is 
produced with no AI at all: faster, cheaper, and still citation-checked.
L16.2 — Committee Brain Resilience (removing the single point)
NEW / hardens the Committee Brain (P1). Directly answers the audit's single-point-of-failure finding.
=== PAGE 201 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 16   ·   Page 5
Purpose. Keep the Committee Brain as the single logical point of AI governance — one place for cost, guardrails, 
failover and observability — while removing it as a single point of failure and a throughput chokepoint, by running 
it as many identical stateless instances with cross-provider failover and graceful degradation.
Why it earns its place. The audit flagged the router as both a strength (one governance point) and a hazard (one 
failure surface and scale bottleneck). The fix is not to remove the single governance point — that would scatter the 
guardrails across the codebase — but to make that one logical point physically redundant and scalable. One brain, 
many bodies.
How the developer builds it
1. Run the Committee Brain as multiple stateless instances behind a load balancer / queue — one logical 
service, many runtime copies; no single instance is the system.
2. Extend failover across providers and regions, so no single provider outage stops AI.
3. Graceful degradation: if all AI providers are unavailable, fall back to the deterministic path (L16.1) for what 
can be produced without AI, and queue the rest through the Job Engine rather than failing.
4. Make heavy calls asynchronous by default (Job Engine) so the request path is never blocked on a slow 
model.
5. Keep all governance — cost, guardrails, kill switch, tracing — at the logical service, unchanged.
Key features
• One logical governance point, many stateless instances.
• Cross-provider and cross-region failover.
• Graceful degradation to the deterministic path when AI is unavailable.
• Heavy calls asynchronous via the Job Engine.
• Governance (cost, guardrails, kill switch, tracing) unchanged.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
scaleInstances infra Run N stateless router instances
failoverProvider internal Switch provider/region on failure
degradeToDeterministic internal Fall back to no-AI path (L16.1)
queueHeavyCall internal Async heavy calls via P2
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from AI requests; provider health.
Writes to Routed output; usage/trace logs.
Calls / triggers LLM providers; Job Engine (P2); deterministic path (L16.1).
Data required The AI request and provider availability.
Desired output. The AI router remains the single governed entry point but is no longer a single point of failure or a 
throughput bottleneck — it scales out and degrades gracefully instead of stopping.
L16.3 — Corrective Consolidation of Overlapping Components
CORRECTIVE — enacts the audit's finding. Merges duplicated responsibilities; removes the overlaps.
Purpose. Consolidate the overlapping components the audit identified so each responsibility has one owner: 
merge the five learning components into one Learning service, the three advisory-review components into one 
Advisor service, and the three client-facing channels into one Client-Channel service — removing the duplication 
without losing any capability, and giving the master knowledge a single write path.
Why it earns its place. The audit found one responsibility fragmented across many components — learning across 
five, advisory review across three, client access across three — causing duplication, cost and diffuse authority over 
=== PAGE 202 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 16   ·   Page 6
the master. Consolidation is the correction; nothing is lost, ownership becomes clear, and the master gets a single 
owner (also closing the self-mutating-master hazard).
How the developer builds it
1. Learning service (one): absorb Style Learner (15), Firm-Data Learning (L3.2), Continuous Learning Engine 
(L4.6), User Memory (L14.1) and Master Auto-Update (L14.2) as MODES (per-user vs firm; manual vs auto). 
One owner of the single master write path; the governance thresholds are unchanged.
2. Advisor service (one): absorb Suggestions Panel (L5.2), Draft Reviewer (L11.1) and Argument Strategist 
(L11.2) into one advisory pass — composed into a single Committee Brain call instead of three, cutting cost 
and latency.
3. Client-Channel service (one): absorb Client Reporting (43), WhatsApp Self-Service (L8.1) and Client Update 
into one client-facing owner — WhatsApp menu, push updates and reports under one roof, with the 
number-scoping rules unchanged.
4. Preserve every capability as a mode or sub-feature of the merged service; retire the duplicate wiring.
Key features
• Five learning components → one Learning service (per-user / firm, manual / auto modes).
• Three advisory-review components → one Advisor service (one composed AI pass).
• Three client channels → one Client-Channel service.
• The master knowledge gets a single write path (closes the self-mutating-master hazard).
• No capability lost — everything preserved as a mode.
• Lower cost, clearer ownership, fewer moving parts.
Interconnection & data
Relationship Detail
Reads from The merged components' inputs (unchanged).
Writes to Through the single owner of each responsibility; single master write path.
Calls / triggers Committee Brain (P1) — one composed call for advisory review.
Data required Same as the merged components.
Desired output. Eleven overlapping components become three clean services — capabilities intact, ownership 
clear, duplication and cost removed. Recorded as a deliberate consolidation; the specific merges can be adjusted 
on your word.
L16.4 — Client Document Selection → Drafting
NEW / enhances ingest (D3 / L7.1). The gap the audit surfaced.
Purpose. In the ingest system where clients send documents, let the user select which of those documents to 
move into the drafting section — so the drafter works from exactly the documents chosen, not the whole inflow.
Why it earns its place. Clients send many documents; only some are relevant to a given draft. Honest finding: the 
ingest system split, classified and filed client documents, but there was no explicit action to pick specific client-sent 
documents and push them to the drafting workspace. This adds it.
How the developer builds it
1. In the ingest / staging view (client-sent documents via WhatsApp / email / portal, L7.1 / D3), show the 
documents with select checkboxes and brief identifiers.
2. A ‘Move to drafting’ action pushes the selected documents into the drafting workspace (D4) for the current 
matter / draft, as the working set.
3. The drafter (Ghost Writer, Advisor) then works from exactly those documents.
4. Audit which documents were moved to drafting and by whom.
Key features
• Select client-sent documents (checkbox + brief identifier).
=== PAGE 203 ===
SIKANDER AI — Layer 16   ·   Page 7
• ‘Move to drafting’ into the working set.
• The drafter works from the chosen documents only, not the whole inflow.
• Audited.

3.  Governance
L16.1, L16.2 and L16.4 are additive. L16.3 is corrective: it consolidates and therefore modifies how the locked  
learning, review and client components are owned — done on your express instruction to remove overlaps, recorded 
here, and reversible. No capability is removed; responsibilities are merged so each has one owner. Crucially, the  
consolidation gives the master knowledge a single write path, which closes the self-mutating-master hazard the  
audit rated HIGH. If a specific merge does not suit you, name it and I will adjust.

SIKANDER AI
FORENSIC ARCHITECTURE AUDIT
Independent architectural review — the structure, not the code. Critical and evidence-based.
Subject: the locked v6/v7 base (7 domains, 49 units) + 14 additive layers (L2–L15)
Prepared for Advocate Shamail Sikander · NHS Legal, Karachi · 6 July 2026
=== PAGE 206 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 2
Table of Contents
=== PAGE 207 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 3
Executive Verdict (read this first)
The core of this architecture is strong; the accumulated whole is not yet in balance. The platform spine — a single  
governed AI router, a citation-safety gate applied everywhere, tenant isolation at the data layer, and a matter record 
that threads the practice — is genuinely well-conceived and, in its niche, better than the horizontal legal-AI tools it  
competes with. That core would rate B (sound, minor corrections).
But three structural facts pull the whole down to C — functional but architecturally inefficient. First, breadth has  
outrun implementation: roughly ninety components across fourteen additive layers now sit on a base that, per the  
firm's own record, is not yet operational (front ends unbuilt, WhatsApp webhook unregistered, provider keys  
unloaded). Second, the master knowledge framework is now self-modifying (two learning loops plus per-user  
personalization write to it), which makes the one thing every module trusts a moving target. Third, a single  
responsibility — learning — is fragmented across five components, and client-facing access across three, producing 
duplication and diffuse ownership. None of these is fatal; all are correctable, and the correction is mostly  
consolidation and sequencing, not redesign.
Governing answer to the audit's central question — is this the simplest, safest, fastest, most scalable and  
economically rational structure for the business? Not yet. It is close on safety and integration, weak on simplicity and 
cost, and unproven on scale because it has not run. The single highest-value action is not another feature or another 
layer; it is to freeze accretion, make the essential spine operational, and put controls on the self-updating master.

SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 4

Reconstructed from the canonical documents. The system is a legal operating system for a Pakistani litigation firm,  
organised as a shared platform beneath seven functional domains, with fourteen additive layers of capability plugged 
on top through events and a module registry.

=== PAGE 209 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 5
2.  Architectural Logic
Sound. Most major components have a clear purpose; the domain grouping is rational; the matter record is a 
correct single spine; the platform-beneath-modules separation is right; and dependencies mostly flow the correct 
way (modules call platform; higher layers subscribe to lower ones).
Inconsistent. Three logic problems are real:
• Overlapping functions. ‘Learning’ is performed by Style Learner (15), Firm-Data Learning (L3.2), the 
Continuous Learning Engine (L4.6), User Memory (L14.1) and Master Auto-Update (L14.2). ‘Draft assistance’ 
is split across Suggestions Panel (L5.2), Draft Reviewer (L11.1) and Argument Strategist (L11.2). ‘Client 
access’ spans Client Reporting (43), WhatsApp Self-Service (L8.1) and Client Update (B). These are one 
responsibility fragmented, not five distinct ones.
• Diffuse authority over the master. Several components write to the master knowledge; no single owner 
arbitrates it. For a legal product, the master should have one authoritative owner and one write path.
• Layer inflation. ‘Layer’ is used as a documentation device; several ‘layers’ are single small extensions (e.g. 
WhatsApp Voice, Signatures). This inflates the apparent structure and risks confusing narrative layering with 
deployable modules.
No circular dependencies were found at the domain level; the risk is depth, not cycles (see §12).
=== PAGE 210 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 6
3.  Forensic Process Traces
3.1  Draft a pleading
ENTRY  facts (typed/voice/upload, L13.1) →
PROC   Matter (D1) binds context →
AI     Ghost Writer (D4) via Committee Brain (P1) → outline (HITL approve) → draft →
DATA   clause library (30) + Drafting DNA + research (D5) →
VALID  Citation-Safety Gate (33) + Reviewer (L11.1) →
OUTPUT DOCX/PDF; docket (L5.5); sign-off block (L15.1) →
HUMAN  partner approval (L4.1) →  AUDIT  job + version history
Verdict: complete and correctly sequenced; the HITL and citation gate are appropriate. Risk: multiple advisory AI  
passes (suggestions, reviewer, strategist) each hit P1 — latency and cost can stack if all run synchronously.  
Recommend making advisory passes optional/async.
3.2  Marked-associate court day (automation-heavy)
ENTRY  roster change (D6) → RPA fetch cause list →
PROC   Associate Roster auto-marks initials (L2.2) →
RPA    Court Time-Log geofence in/out (L2.1) → court.checkout event →
AI     parse update reply (P1) →
OUTPUT matter update sheet (D1) + WhatsApp group (36) →
HUMAN  associate provides the update  AUDIT  matter timeline
Verdict: logically sound and well-decoupled through events. Dependency on court-portal publication is the fragile  
link (acknowledged; per-court connectors). Human control is present (associate supplies the update; nothing auto-
served).
3.3  Client self-service (access-critical)
Entry by WhatsApp number → authorise against registered numbers (L8.1/P5) → scope to own matters only →  
deliver documents as PDF. Verdict: correctly gated at the data layer, not just in chat logic — the strongest access  
design in the system. Risk: this path now overlaps Client Reporting and Client Update; consolidate to one client-
channel owner.
=== PAGE 211 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 7
4.  Architectural Integrity
Property Assessment
Separation of responsibilities Good at domain level; weakened by the learning/review/client overlaps (§2).
Modularity Strong — events + registry genuinely enable additive change.
Dependency management Mostly rational; chains are getting deep (§12); no cycles found.
Data ownership Weak point — the master knowledge has multiple writers and no single arbiter.
Orchestration Job Engine + event bus is a sound, mostly centralised orchestration.
Control mechanisms Strong — citation gate, HITL, permission scoping, kill switch.
Traceability Strong — jobs, versions, audit logs, Opik tracing.
Fault isolation Moderate — the Committee Brain is a shared failure surface for all AI.
Maintainability At risk from sprawl and duplicated learning components.
Extensibility Excellent — the additive-layer pattern is the system's best structural trait.
Propagation risk: a wrong entry into the master knowledge (via an auto-learning loop) would propagate into every  
draft, generation and review that reads the master. This is the single most important integrity hazard and is  
addressed in §9 and §15.
=== PAGE 212 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 8
5.  RPA & Automation Architecture
Automations: roster/cause-list fetch (D6), statute/SRO fetch (L4.9), auto-intimation on order events (L4.4), marked-
case update relay (L2.3), reminders (P4), the Cloud Personal Agent (L3.7), monthly file reconciliation (L7.1), and  
scheduled 6–12h learning (L4.6). Triggers are events or schedules; orchestration is centralised through the Job  
Engine, which is correct.
Well-controlled. The consequential automations are human-gated: auto-intimation prepares but the clerk 
dispatches; the cloud agent holds irreversible actions for confirmation; learning above threshold still surfaces 
substantive changes to the admin. This is the right posture.
Hazard. Three automations act on the real world or the master — auto-intimation (dispatch), cloud agent 
(autonomous task completion), and auto-master-update (mutates the trusted knowledge). Individually gated; the 
architectural risk is their combination running unattended (e.g. the cloud agent triggering learning that mutates 
the master). Recommend an explicit automation authority ceiling and a rule that no automation may write to the 
master without human confirmation.
=== PAGE 213 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 9
6.  AI Architecture
Strong pattern. Routing every model call through the Committee Brain is the standout architectural decision: one 
place for provider choice, failover, cost governance, the citation guardrail and observability. AI recommendations 
are distinguished from decisions almost everywhere (HITL, advisory suggestions, ‘advisory only’ bench 
intelligence).
Concerns. (a) AI proliferation — drafting, research, reviewer, strategist, cross-exam, suggestions, RFP, corporate, 
learning and personalization all invoke AI; several are advisory passes over the same draft and duplicate 
effort/cost. (b) The single router is also a single runtime chokepoint (see scale, §7). (c) Important AI outputs are 
validated (citation gate, Opik), which is correct — but the sheer number of AI touchpoints widens the surface that 
must be measured.
Assessment: the AI layer is appropriately integrated in principle but has become broad in practice. Consolidate the  
advisory-review passes (suggestions + reviewer + strategist) behind one ‘Advisor’ service that calls P1 once with a  
composed prompt, rather than three services calling it separately.
=== PAGE 214 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 10
7.  Scalability
Scale Assessment First constraint
10× Comfortable. Firestore and Cloud Functions absorb firm-level growth; Jobs decouple heavy work. None material.
100× Workable with tuning. AI cost and provider rate limits bite; Firestore hot collections and query fan-out 
need attention; per-source court/statute fetchers are fragile at volume.
Committee Brain throughput + AI 
cost.
1000× Requires redesign of specific pieces — not the whole. The single AI router, the self-hosted single-
instance Opik, the monolithic master knowledge, and per-firm synchronous learning would need 
horizontal scaling, queue-based decoupling and multi-region.
Central AI router + master 
knowledge + observability 
instance.
Structural note: most domains can scale independently (they are event-coupled), which is good. The exceptions that 
cannot scale independently are the ones every module shares — the Committee Brain and the master knowledge.  
Those are the components to harden for scale; the rest is largely fine.
=== PAGE 215 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 11
8.  Speed & Efficiency
• Positive: the Job Engine removes heavy work from the request path; events avoid tight coupling; caching of 
reads is available.
• Drag: stacked advisory AI passes on a single draft (suggestions → reviewer → strategist), each a separate P1 
round-trip; sequential HITL + citation-gate hand-offs (necessary for safety, but serialise the path); repeated 
OCR/parse across ingestion, staging and analyzers if not shared.
• Opportunity: run research and drafting in parallel; compose the advisory passes into one call; share a single 
OCR/parse result across splitter, staging, trial and response modules rather than re-processing.
Simplification can raise speed here without touching integrity — chiefly by consolidating AI passes and sharing OCR 
output.

10.  Cost Architecture
The architecture creates avoidable cost in four places: (a) duplicated learning components (five) and overlapping  
review/client components — duplicated build and maintenance; (b) recurring AI spend from many AI touchpoints,  
including continuous 6–12h scheduled learning that runs even when the firm is idle; (c) stacked advisory AI passes  
over the same draft; (d) infrastructure breadth — self-hosted Opik, the cloud agent, multiple provider integrations  
and connectors. The cost-governance at the Committee Brain (70/90% alerts, kill switch) is the right control and  
should be kept.
Change Cost to implement Ongoing cost Expected saving Scale benefit Business benefit
Consolidate 5 learning components → 
1 service
Medium Lower Maintenance + AI High Clear ownership of the 
master
Compose 3 advisory AI passes → 1 call Low Lower AI spend + latency Medium Faster drafting
Gate/space scheduled learning (not 
every 6–12h)
Low Lower Recurring AI Medium Cost predictability
Consolidate client channels → 1 Medium Lower Maintenance Medium Consistent client UX
Objective met by these four: better cost-to-performance and cost-to-scale without losing capability — they remove 
duplication, not function.
=== PAGE 218 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 14
11.  Business Alignment
Class Components
ESSENTIAL Matter spine (D1); Documents (D3); Drafting + citation gate (D4/33); Research (D5); Court Ops & Roster (D6); WhatsApp (D2); 
Committee Brain (P1); Isolation (P5); Job Engine (P2).
SUPPORTING Intake; client self-service (L8.1); printing/dispatch (L6); staging/registration (L7); dashboard (L9.4); approvals (L4.1); 
reminders.
OPTIONAL (defer) Cloud Personal Agent (L3.7); auto-intimation (L4.4); much of the Corporate Suite (L12) until the corporate practice 
materialises; RFP intelligence; master auto-update (L14.2); per-user memory (L14.1).
UNNECESSARY COMPLEXITY / 
consolidate
Duplicated learning components; overlapping review passes; overlapping client channels; layer inflation.
The core is tightly aligned to the litigation business. The misalignment is one of sequence and proportion: high-
sophistication optional components (self-updating master, per-user memory, cloud agent) were specced while the  
essential spine remains unbuilt. Technical sophistication has, in places, outrun commercial need.
=== PAGE 219 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 15
12.  Dependency Map & Choke Points
Component Depends upon Used by Criticality Failure impact Scale impact
Committee Brain (P1) LLM providers, secrets ~all AI modules Critical All AI stops First constraint at 100×+
Matter record (D1) P5 All domains Critical Practice halts Scales well (Firestore)
Isolation (P5) Firebase Auth Everything Critical Security breach/lockout Scales well
Master knowledge D3/D5 + learning loops D4, D5, review Critical Bad drafts propagate Central; harden for scale
Database Connector 
(BYOD)
External DB Research, viewers High Research degraded Depends on external
Court/statute fetchers Court/govt portals D6, library Medium Coverage gaps Fragile per source
Job Engine (P2) Firestore Heavy/AI tasks High Async work stalls Scales with queueing
Two choke points dominate: the Committee Brain and the master knowledge. Both are shared by nearly everything 
and neither scales independently as designed. Everything else is either domain-local (scales fine) or externally  
bounded (BYOD, court portals). Harden these two and the dependency profile is healthy.
=== PAGE 220 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 16
13.  Gaps, Duplications, Conflicts, Over/Under-Engineering
Gaps. Operational readiness (front ends, webhook, keys) is the material gap — the architecture describes far more 
than exists. A named, single owner of the master knowledge is missing. An explicit automation-authority ceiling is 
missing. A disaster-recovery / rollback story for the master is missing.
Duplications. Learning (5 components); advisory review (3); client channels (3); voice (module 38, L3.3, L10.1); 
document ingestion/OCR re-processed in several modules.
Conflicts. Two loops (L4.6, L14.2) plus personalization (L14.1) write to the master with no single arbiter — 
competing authority over the source of truth.
Over-engineering. Self-updating master, per-user memory, cloud personal agent, and parts of the corporate suite 
— high complexity, uncertain near-term value, built ahead of the operational core. Layer inflation (single-feature 
‘layers’).
Under-engineering. Fault isolation around the Committee Brain (single failure surface); horizontal scale of the 
master and observability; drift-monitoring of court/statute fetchers; a hardened multi-tenant path proven under 
load (SaaS is specced, not battle-tested).
=== PAGE 221 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 17

R1 — Freeze scope; ship the MVP spine
Field Detail
Current 14 layers specced; base not operational
Problem Breadth outruns implementation
Why it matters Nothing shippable; cost/effort spread thin
Proposed change Freeze new features; implement Matter+Documents+Drafting+Citation gate+Committee Brain+one channel; treat L3–L15 as a 
prioritised backlog
Benefit A working product; proof before more breadth
Risk Perceived loss of momentum
Cost impact Saves ongoing spec/build cost
Dependencies affected All layers (as backlog)
Business impact Gets to revenue-capable MVP fastest
R2 — One owner + control for the master knowledge
Field Detail
Current Multiple writers to the master (L4.6, L14.2, L14.1)
Problem No single arbiter; self-mutating
Why it matters A wrong learning propagates everywhere
Proposed change One Master-Knowledge service; all substantive changes human-confirmed; version + rollback; separate immutable citation 
rules from mutable firm knowledge
Benefit Integrity of the one shared source of truth
Risk Slightly slower learning
Cost impact Low
Dependencies affected Learning, drafting, review
Business impact Protects every draft the firm files
R3 — Scale and isolate the Committee Brain
Field Detail
Current Single AI router, single runtime surface
Problem Central chokepoint + SPOF
Why it matters AI outage halts all AI features
Proposed change Keep it as the single governance point but make it horizontally scalable, add graceful degradation and async paths
Benefit Availability + throughput at scale
Risk Modest engineering
Cost impact Medium
Dependencies affected All AI modules
Business impact Keeps the product up as it grows
R4 — Consolidate duplicated responsibilities
Field Detail
Current Learning (5), review (3), client channels (3)
Problem One responsibility fragmented
Why it matters Duplication, conflict, cost
Proposed change Fold into: one Learning service, one Advisor service, one Client-Channel service
Benefit Simplicity, lower cost, clear ownership
Risk Refactor effort

=== PAGE 223 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 19
15.  Recommended Target Architecture
The target is the current core, disciplined — not a new design. Same platform spine, same domains, with three  
structural corrections and a strict sequencing rule.
PLATFORM CORE (unchanged, hardened)
  Committee Brain* (scaled, isolated)  Job Engine  Observability  Notify  Isolation
        |                                                         *single governance point
ONE OWNER PER SHARED TRUTH
  Master-Knowledge service (single writer, human-gated, versioned)
  Learning service (per-user + firm modes)   Advisor service (one AI review pass)
  Client-Channel service (WhatsApp self-service + reporting + update)
        |
SEVEN DOMAINS (unchanged)  D1..D7  — event-coupled, independently scalable
        |
CAPABILITY BACKLOG  (former L3–L15, prioritised, added as the business proves need)
        |
DATA  Firestore · Storage/R2 · BYOD
Design intent, in the audit's own terms: simplicity (consolidate the duplications), modularity (keep the event/registry 
pattern), speed (compose AI passes; share OCR; parallelise), scalability (scale the two choke points; keep domains  
independent), resilience (isolate the AI surface; version the master), security (unchanged — it is a strength),  
auditability (unchanged — a strength), cost efficiency (remove duplication and idle learning), business alignment  
(build essential first, optional on proven demand). No new layers are added for sophistication.
=== PAGE 224 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 20
16.  Final Forensic Verdict
Dimension Score /10
Architectural integrity 7.0
Structural clarity 6.5
Scalability 6.0
Speed capability 6.5
Resilience 6.0
RPA / AI integration 7.5
Cost efficiency 5.5
Business alignment 5.5
Weighted read: a strong core (integration, security, extensibility, control) carrying real drag from sprawl,  
duplicated responsibilities, a self-mutating master, and — decisively — the fact that it has not yet run.
Five strongest architectural features
• The single governed AI router (Committee Brain) — one chokepoint for cost, failover, guardrails, 
observability.
• The citation-safety gate as a cross-cutting control — the correct safety spine for legal AI.
• Tenant isolation and privilege protection enforced at the data layer, not just in code.
• The matter record as the single spine threading the whole practice.
• The additive-layer pattern (events + registry) — genuine extensibility without rewriting.
Five principal structural weaknesses
• Feature breadth has outrun implementation; the product is not operational.
• The master knowledge is self-modifying with diffuse ownership.
• ‘Learning’ is fragmented across five components; ‘client access’ across three.
• The Committee Brain is a shared runtime failure surface and scale chokepoint.
• Dependency chains are deepening (esp. corporate/brief-to-agreement).
Five largest architectural hazards
• Sprawl ahead of a working MVP (HIGH).
• Self-mutating master propagating a wrong learning (HIGH).
• Committee Brain single point of failure/throughput (HIGH).
• Fragmented learning ownership (MED).
• Combined unattended automations writing to the master (MED).
Five highest-value improvements
• Freeze scope and ship the essential spine (MVP).
• Give the master one owner with human-gated, versioned writes.
• Scale and isolate the Committee Brain.
• Consolidate learning, review and client channels into one service each.
• Compose advisory AI passes and share OCR output (speed + cost).
Components that should remain unchanged
Committee Brain pattern (scale it, don't redesign it); the citation-safety gate; the matter spine (D1/D2); tenant  
isolation (P5); the Job Engine and event bus; and the additive-layer pattern. These are the system's structural  
strengths and should be preserved.
=== PAGE 225 ===
SIKANDER AI — Forensic Architecture Audit   ·   Page 21
Classification
C — FUNCTIONAL BUT ARCHITECTURALLY INEFFICIENT
With the qualification that the core alone would rate B (sound, minor corrections). The whole rates C because of  
accumulated breadth, duplicated responsibilities, the self-mutating master, and non-operational status — all  
correctable by consolidation and sequencing rather than restructuring. It is not D (no material restructuring is  
required) and emphatically not E (it is well-suited to the business). The path from C to B-then-A is short and is set  
out in §14–§15: freeze, ship the spine, own the master, scale the router, consolidate the duplicates.
Governing question, answered: the architecture is safe and well-integrated but not yet the simplest, fastest or  
most economical structure for the business — because it has grown faster than it has been built, and because a  
few shared components (the master, the router) and a few duplicated responsibilities need consolidating. Correct 
those, get it running, and this becomes a genuinely strong, scalable legal operating system for its market.

3.  The Two Refinements Applied
So that the consolidation is honest to the last feature, two changes were made to the v7 document and the  
flowchart:
• Contract Review & Risk [26] is now a named capability in Domain 4, separate from the 159-type Generator. 
Reviewing a third-party contract (clause analysis, amendment suggestions, version comparison, risk report) 
is analysis, not generation, and its features deserve their own line so a developer never overlooks them.
• Legal Opinion and Case-Type/Forum Advisor are now explicit in Domain 5. They were part of the original 
research suite but had no standalone number in the 44; naming them prevents a silent gap.
Both are reflected in the updated Consolidated v7 document and the grouping flowchart accompanying this audit.
Sub-features now stated explicitly
These were correctly inside their capability but easy to miss in a one-line summary; they are called out so they are  
built, not assumed:
• Stamp-duty calculator (Property, D4); court-fee calculator (Limitation, D1); notice delivery-tracking (D4).
• GPS court check-in (D6) — retained alongside the new in/out time-log (L2.1).
• The 12 document-type categories behind the 159 types (D4).
• Account-security depth — MFA, lockout, scoped API keys, session management (D1, Firm & People).
• The full 31-command WhatsApp surface, not the reduced 7 (D2).
• Cost governance at 70/90% with PKR logging and kill switch (Platform · AI Brain).

Formal outcome / judge prediction Reframed to advisory Bench Intelligence (D6)
Meilisearch dedicated engine Deferred; Firestore/pg_trgm search retained
Custom model fine-tuning Rejected — Drafting DNA superior
=== PAGE 234 ===
SIKANDER AI — Completeness & Reconciliation Audit   ·   Page 9
5.  Conclusion
The seven-domain consolidation (v7) is a faithful, non-destructive regrouping of the finalized architecture. Every  
platform service, every one of the 44 modules, and all four Layer-2 additions are present and located; the distinctive 
sub-features are retained and, where they risked being obscured, now named; the intentional exclusions are  
unchanged. Over and above that, the new Trial & Case Strategy domain — with the whole-file Trial Case Analyzer — 
is added. Nothing finalized has been dropped.

=== PAGE 242 ===
0. How to Read This Layer
Layer 17 does three things that the earlier layers were not asked to do. It rules on which document governs the whole project, because two documents  
presently claim to; it corrects the internal contradictions that the v9 set carries; and it adds the components that the project record contains but the v9 set 
does not. Everything additive follows the house format of Layers 3–16 (purpose, why it earns its place, how the developer builds it, key features, functions, 
wiring, desired output). Everything corrective is stated as an erratum against a named page or module so that the locked v6 text is never silently edited — it 
is overridden here, on the record.
Reading order: §1 (the governing decision) first, because every other section assumes it; §2 (errata) before any developer reads v6; §3 (new components) 
when building; §4–§6 (index, decisions, matrix) when checking completeness; Annexes when wiring the external corpus or correcting the scraper prompt.
Label Meaning in this document
GOVERNING A ruling that binds the whole set. Two are made here: the canonical base (§1.1) and the amendment protocol (§1.4).
ERRATUM A correction to text in v6, v7 or Layers 2–16 that is wrong, contradictory or superseded. The original text is not edited; this layer overrides it.
NEW A component with no counterpart anywhere in the v9 set.
CARRIED A component or rule carried in from rev-17 or from the August–September project record, restated here so it survives.
REINSTATED A capability recorded as removed in v9 and now put back by the instruction of 6 September 2026.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 3
=== PAGE 243 ===
1. The Governing Decision — Which Document Rules
1.1 Ruling
The Architecture Document Set v9 (2 September 2026), as amended by this Layer 17, is the sole canonical architecture of SIKANDER AI. The Final  
Unified Architecture rev-17 of 21 August 2026 is absorbed into it and superseded as a standalone document. Nothing in rev-17 is lost: every service,  
module, rule, open item and roadmap entry of rev-17 is mapped to its home in the v9 set in §1.3 below, and anything without a home is added in §3. The 
standing rule against parallel architecture documents is preserved, and from this date it attaches to the v9 set: all further change travels as a numbered,  
dated layer or erratum bound into this set, never as a new master.
Why v9 and not rev-17. rev-17 records the count and shape of the system (four services, fourteen modules, Module P) but the fourteen module names were 
never recorded and were reconstructed from memory on 6 September, producing a register that dropped fifteen designed capabilities and invented one.  
The v9 set, by contrast, carries every module with a full specification, a completeness audit proving nothing was lost from v6 to v7, a coverage matrix  
against every historical build, and an independent forensic audit. It is the richer, self-proving document. rev-17 contributed discipline — supersession,  
amendment protocol, the Module P source ruling, the channel-authority invariant — and those are carried in below rather than discarded.
1.2 Supersession clause
With effect from 7 September 2026 the following are superseded as architecture authorities and retained as history only: Final Unified Architecture rev-17 
(21 August 2026); rev-17.1 Amendment No. 1 draft (6 September 2026); the Consolidated Master Register of 25 August 2026 as a source of architecture 
(it remains the record of decisions and lineage); the AWS v5.0 FINAL and its v5.1/v6.0 reconciliation; the 42-module Definitive Master; the Firebase  
Developer Reference (rev-16) except as the code-verified description of what is presently deployed; the April 2026 Python/FastAPI Developer Manual 
except as the port specification for the Q4 2026 migration (§3, L17.14). Where any of these conflicts with the v9 set as amended, the v9 set governs.

1.4 Amendment protocol (GOVERNING)
1. The v9 set is amended only by a numbered layer (L18, L19 …) or a numbered erratum (E-n) bound into the set behind this document, each dated 
and each stating what it overrides.
2. Locked v6 text is never edited. It is overridden by erratum, so that a reader can always see what was decided and what was changed.
3. A gap analysis from any model or person is triaged into three bins before anything is adopted — genuinely new; already present under another  
name; excluded by design — and only the first bin may become a layer.
4. Every session on this project opens from the v9 set as amended; no session reconstructs the architecture from memory.
5. Nothing in an amendment may reopen an item in the exclusion register (§5) without an express, recorded reversal of the decision that excluded it.
1.5 Rules carried in from rev-17 and the August record (CARRIED)

P5, Module 36, L8.1, L10.1, L4.11. v9 line 'WhatsApp and app share identical permission checks' (v6

Stripe is deleted. Gateway options are JazzCash, Easypaisa, or a 
Pakistani bank acquiring gateway; recurring token-payment 
endpoint schema is a firm-level dependency (L17.13).

3. New Components — L17.1 to L17.14
Fourteen components. Each follows the house format. Where a component implements a forensic-audit recommendation or a rule carried from rev-17, the 
source is named in its status line.
L17.1 — Temporal-Law Binding (point-in-time statute text)
Status. NEW. Source: review of 31 August 2026 ('no temporal-law binding'). Extends Modules 22, 33, 44 and D4.
Purpose. Bind every draft, opinion and citation check to the text of the statute as it stood on the legally relevant date — the date of the cause of action, the 
offence, the transaction or the order under challenge — rather than to the current text.
Why it earns its place. An offence committed in 2015 under a section amended in 2018 must be pleaded on the 2015 text. A draft citing the current text is 
defective in a way every existing check reports as correct: the section exists, the citation is well-formed, the authority is verified. In Pakistani practice this 
is a larger source of real error than a hallucinated case, and the set has no control for it. Module 22 stores amendment history for viewing; nothing  
consumes it at drafting time.
How the developer builds it
6. Data model: the corpus contract (Annex A) carries statute_section_version with effective_from, effective_to, amending_instrument and  
consolidated text per version. Module 22's getSection takes an optional as_at date and returns the version in force on that date; with no date it  
returns current text and flags it 'current'.
7. Matter binding: Module 2 gains relevant_law_dates on the matter — one or more named dates (cause of action, offence date, transaction date,  
impugned order date) entered at intake or by the Trial Case Analyzer (D7) from the chronology. Every draft inherits the matter's dates; a document 
type declares which date it pleads on (the Document Type Selector, Module 14, carries the rule per type).
8. Drafting: when Ghost Writer (12) or any generator inserts or paraphrases a statutory provision, it calls getSection(as_at = the type's relevant date) 
and stamps the inserted text with the version reference. The Suggestions Panel (L5.2) shows 'as at DD Mon YYYY' beside each provision.
9. Citation gate: Module 33 gains a temporal check — for every statutory reference in a court-facing draft it compares the cited text against the  
version in force on the matter's relevant date and blocks approval on mismatch, with the two versions shown side by side.
10.Research: Module 18's searchStatutes accepts as_at and ranks the version in force; the Compendium (20) records the version relied on.
11.Limitation: Module 44 computes limitation on the Limitation Act text in force at the accrual date, since amendments to periods are not  
retrospective unless expressly so.
12.Where the corpus has no version history for a section, the gate reports 'temporal check unavailable — verify manually' rather than passing silently.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 11
=== PAGE 251 ===
Key features
• Point-in-time retrieval of any section by date.
• Matter-level relevant dates, inherited by every draft.
• Per-type rule for which date a document pleads on.
• Version stamp on every inserted provision; side-by-side view on mismatch.
• Hard block at the citation gate on temporal mismatch; explicit 'unavailable' state, never a silent pass.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
getSectionAsAt GET Section text in force on a given date
setRelevantLawDates POST Record the matter's legally relevant dates
checkTemporalConsistency internal Gate check: cited text vs version in force
listSectionVersions GET All versions of a section with effective ranges
Wiring Detail
Reads from Statute versions (corpus via Module 10); matter dates (2); document-type rules (14).
Writes to Matter relevant_law_dates; version stamps on drafts (12); gate findings (33).
Human control Relevant dates are confirmed by the responsible associate at intake; the gate's block is cleared only by a partner recording a reason.
Depends on L17.10 corpus contract (versioned statutes); Modules 2, 12, 14, 18, 22, 33, 44.
Desired output. No draft leaves the firm pleading the wrong edition of the law, and every provision carries the date it speaks from.
L17.2 — Entity Resolution & Un-merge
Status. NEW. Source: 20-gap adversarial review (25 August 2026) and review of 31 August 2026. Extends Modules 1, 6, 5, L7.2, L13.2.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 12
=== PAGE 252 ===
Purpose. Resolve the same person, company or property appearing under variant names, spellings, CNICs or addresses into one entity — with candidate 
and confirmed states, a human threshold, a full merge history and a reversible un-merge path.
Why it earns its place. Entity resolution is probabilistic. 'Muhammad Ali' is not a distinguishing name in Karachi. The two failure directions are not  
symmetric: a false split misses a conflict, which is recoverable; a false merge can record an opposing party as an existing client, so the Conflict Checker (6) 
reads the matter as consent-covered and reports clear. That is a professional-conduct exposure under PBC Rule 85, and it is irreversible unless merges can 
be undone. The v9 set has no entity model at all — the word does not appear.
How the developer builds it
13.Entity model: entities (person / company / property / court / counsel) with canonical fields, plus entity_aliases (name variants,  
CNIC/NTN/registration numbers, phone numbers, addresses, Urdu/roman spellings) each with a source document reference.
14.Matching: deterministic keys first (CNIC, NTN, SECP number, phone) then probabilistic scoring on name (transliteration-aware for Urdu/Sindhi 
names), father's name, address, date of birth. Score bands: ≥0.95 auto-link as candidate-confirmed pending review; 0.70–0.95 candidate, queued to 
the responsible associate; <0.70 no link.
15.States: candidate → confirmed → (optionally) split. No candidate link is used by the Conflict Checker as evidence of identity; only confirmed  
links are. A candidate that would change a conflict result is surfaced as 'possible conflict — unresolved identity' rather than as clear or as conflict.
16.Merge history: every merge writes an immutable merge_event (who, when, evidence, score); un-merge replays the event in reverse, restoring both 
entities and re-pointing every matter, document and correspondence reference to its pre-merge owner.
17.Feeds: intake (5, L2.4), client registration (L7.2), CNIC scan (L13.2), party extraction from ingested documents (11, D7 chronology), and the  
roster/cause-list party names (35).
18.Every conflict check (6) reports the identity basis of its result: 'confirmed identity' or 'candidate only — human review required'.
Key features
• Candidate/confirmed distinction enforced across the system.
• Transliteration-aware name matching for Urdu, Sindhi and Roman spellings.
• Immutable merge history; one-step un-merge.
• Conflict Checker never treats a candidate as confirmed.
• Duplicate-client prevention at registration with 'is this the same person?' prompt.
Representative functions
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 13
=== PAGE 253 ===
Function Type Purpose
resolveEntity internal Score and propose candidate links
confirmMerge POST Human confirmation of a candidate
unmergeEntity POST Reverse a merge from its event record
listAliases GET All known variants of an entity
explainConflictBasis internal Identity basis behind a conflict result
Wiring Detail
Reads from Clients and parties (1, 5, L7.2); scanned IDs (L13.2); document parties (11, D7); roster parties (35).
Writes to entities, entity_aliases, merge_events; conflict results (6) annotated with identity basis.
Human control All merges in the 0.70–0.95 band are human-confirmed; un-merge is partner-approved; auto-links above 0.95 are reviewable for 30 days.
Depends on P5 (entities are firm-scoped); Modules 1, 5, 6, 11, 35; L7.2, L13.2.
Desired output. One identity per real-world party, provably built, and a conflict check that can never be fooled by a merge it cannot undo.
L17.3 — Privilege Classification Propagation at Derivation
Status. NEW. Source: migration 019 (AWS lineage, 22 August 2026) closing finding 1 of the adversarial review. Extends P5, P1, Modules 7, 11, 12, D7.
Purpose. Ensure that anything derived from a classified document — a summary, chronology, OCR text, extracted parties, a draft that quotes it, an  
embedding — inherits at least the source's privilege classification, and that the Committee Brain's external-call check reads the classification of what is 
actually being sent, not of a document it is rarely handed.
Why it earns its place. P5 protects the stored document. But the AI gateway is almost never handed a document; it is handed text derived from one. If a 
privileged instruction letter is summarised and the summary is unclassified, the summary flows to an external provider that the original never could. The 
stored level is protected; the derivation path is not. This is the classification leak recorded as unresolved on 25 August.
How the developer builds it
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 14
=== PAGE 254 ===
19.Classification is a first-class attribute on every artefact type: document, document_version, ocr_text, extraction, chronology_entry, draft,  
draft_version, embedding_row, research_session, message. Levels: public < internal < confidential < privileged (client-attorney) < privileged-
restricted (partner-only).
20.Monotonic propagation: any create operation that takes one or more source artefacts writes classification = max(source classifications). Enforced 
at the data layer (Firestore rules / Postgres trigger), not only in application code; a write that would lower a derived level is rejected.
21.Gateway check: P1 receives the composed prompt with its aggregate classification (max over all included fragments). Routing policy per level:  
privileged-restricted never leaves the firm-controlled region; privileged only to providers under an executed DPA; confidential and below per  
normal routing. The policy table lives in P1 and is admin-editable (L4.7).
22.Embeddings and search indexes are classified rows; research (18, 32) filters by the caller's clearance before ranking.
23.Downgrade is a deliberate human act: a partner may declassify an artefact with a recorded reason; the change does not cascade to already-derived 
artefacts, which keep their higher level.
24.Audit: P5's auditPrivilegeAccess logs every derivation and every gateway decision with the classification that governed it.
Key features
• Classification on every artefact, not just documents.
• Monotonic inheritance enforced at the data layer.
• Gateway routes on the classification of the prompt actually sent.
• Provider policy table per level, with region and DPA constraints.
• Human-only downgrade with reason; no cascade.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
deriveArtefact internal Create a derived artefact with inherited classification
classifyPrompt internal Aggregate classification of a composed prompt
routeByClassification internal P1 provider selection under the policy table
declassify POST Partner downgrade with recorded reason
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 15
=== PAGE 255 ===
Wiring Detail
Reads from Source artefact classifications; provider policy table (P1); user clearance (P5).
Writes to Classification on every derived artefact; gateway decision log (P3, P5).
Human control Initial classification set at ingest (default privileged for client-sourced material); downgrade is partner-only.
Depends on P1, P3, P5; Modules 7, 8, 11, 12, 18, 32; D7.
Desired output. Privilege travels with the words, wherever the words go — and the AI gateway can prove which level governed every call.
L17.4 — Proposition Engine — Fact, Analysis and Hypothesis Kept Apart
Status. NEW. Source: migration 018 (AWS lineage) and §30 principle of the Master Architecture. Extends D7, Modules 19, 20, L11.2, L3.1.
Purpose. Store every statement the system makes about a case as a typed proposition — FACT (from a document in the record), ANALYSIS (an inference 
from facts), HYPOTHESIS (a possibility not yet supported) — each with its provenance, so that analysis can never be mistaken for evidence and a  
hypothesis can never be pleaded as fact.
Why it earns its place. The Trial Case Analyzer, the Argument Strategist and the Responsive Drafting Suite all generate statements about what happened 
and what it means. Without a type system, a plausible inference becomes a 'fact' in a chronology, is carried into a draft, and is verified as internally  
consistent because nothing marks it as unproven. The forensic record shows the architecture once violated its own rule on this point (the conflated status 
enum in D21). The engine makes the rule enforceable.
How the developer builds it
25.Propositions: id, matter, type (FACT | ANALYSIS | HYPOTHESIS), statement, supports (document/page/paragraph anchors for FACT;  
proposition ids for ANALYSIS), confidence, author (human or AI job), created, superseded_by.
26.Type rules: a FACT must cite at least one record anchor; an ANALYSIS must cite at least one FACT or ANALYSIS; a HYPOTHESIS cites  
nothing and may not be promoted without becoming one of the other two by acquiring support. Promotion is a human act.
27.Consumers: D7's chronology (buildChronology) writes FACTs only; the Strategy Builder and L11.2 write ANALYSIS; the cross-examination  
builder (L11.3) writes HYPOTHESES about what a witness may concede; L3.1 written statements draw only on FACT and confirmed  
ANALYSIS.
28.Drafting: Ghost Writer (12) may insert a FACT as pleaded fact, an ANALYSIS only in argument/grounds sections, and a HYPOTHESIS never; 
the Reviewer (L11.1) flags any breach.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 16
=== PAGE 256 ===
29.Every proposition carries L17.3 classification and, where it states law, L17.1 temporal binding.
Key features
• Typed propositions with mandatory provenance.
• Chronologies are FACT-only.
• Hypotheses can never reach a pleading.
• Promotion is human, recorded, and traceable.
• Every argument can be traced to the record entries it rests on.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
assertProposition internal Create a typed proposition with supports
promoteProposition POST Human promotion with added support
traceSupport GET Full support chain to record anchors
listUnsupported GET Hypotheses still open on a matter
Wiring Detail
Reads from Ingested record (D3), chronology (D7), evidence map (D7).
Writes to propositions; annotations on drafts (12), briefs (D7), arguments (19, L11.2).
Human control Promotion of any proposition to FACT is confirmed by the responsible associate; the Reviewer's breach flags block partner approval until 
resolved.
Depends on D7; Modules 12, 19, 20; L3.1, L11.1, L11.2, L11.3; L17.1, L17.3.
Desired output. A case file in which the firm always knows what it can prove, what it infers, and what it merely suspects.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 17
=== PAGE 257 ===
L17.5 — Master-Knowledge Service — Single Owner of the Master
Status. NEW. Implements Forensic Audit R2 and completes L16.3. Amends L4.6, L14.1, L14.2, Modules 15, 16, 30.
Purpose. Be the one and only writer to the master knowledge — clause library, style profile, 159-type taxonomy, precedent bank, Drafting DNA — with 
human-gated, versioned, reversible change.
Why it earns its place. The audit's highest-severity finding is that two learning loops and per-user personalization write to the thing every module trusts, 
with no arbiter. A wrong learning propagates into every draft the firm files. L16.3 promised a single write path but specified no component to own it.
How the developer builds it
30.All learning components (15, 16, L3.2, L4.6, L14.1, L14.2, L15.2) become proposers: they emit change_proposals (what, evidence, expected  
effect) and never write to the master.
31.The service classifies proposals: cosmetic (formatting, ordering) may auto-apply and are logged; substantive (a clause's wording, a taxonomy rule, 
a citation protocol) require a partner's confirmation in the Admin Action Center (L4.10).
32.Versioning: the master is versioned as a whole; every applied change is a commit with author, proposal, and diff; any version is restorable in one 
action; drafts record the master version they were produced under.
33.Separation: immutable citation rules and the verification protocol (33) live in a read-only partition that no learning may touch; firm knowledge  
(clauses, style) lives in the mutable partition.
34.Per-user memory (L14.1) is stored per user, outside the master, and can never be promoted into it except by a proposal.
35.Learning cadence is event-driven (on finalisation, on approval) rather than every 6–12 hours, per the audit's cost finding; a scheduled sweep runs 
weekly.
Key features
• One writer; everyone else proposes.
• Cosmetic auto-apply; substantive human-confirm.
• Whole-master versioning with one-step rollback.
• Immutable citation partition.
• Per-user memory kept outside the master.
Representative functions
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 18
=== PAGE 258 ===
Function Type Purpose
proposeChange internal Any learner submits a proposal

Wiring Detail
Reads from Proposals from all learners; approval outcomes (L4.1).
Writes to master (single path); master_versions; proposal log.
Human control Every substantive change is partner-confirmed; rollback is partner-only.
Depends on L4.10 (queue), L4.7 (roles), P2 (jobs), Modules 15, 16, 30.
Desired output. A master the firm can trust because every change to it was seen, chosen and can be undone.
L17.6 — Accounts Intimation Chain
Status. NEW. Source: instruction of 6 September 2026. Notification Centre scope; does not reopen decision #4 (Erratum E-11). Extends P4, Modules 2, 
40, L4.1.
Purpose. When a new matter is opened, a filing is made or an engagement is confirmed, notify the accounts department automatically, obtain their  
confirmation that the entry has been made, and close the loop back to the person who instructed the work — with a prior check that the client has actually 
confirmed the engagement before accounts is told the matter is live.
Why it earns its place. Work begins before the fee record does, and the gap is where unbilled matters are born. The chain is a workflow, not a ledger: it 
moves a confirmation between three people and records that it happened. Ledgers, invoices and accounting remain outside the system by decision #4.
How the developer builds it
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 19
=== PAGE 259 ===
36.Triggers: matter.created (2), filing.recorded (D6 / L4.4 order events), engagement.confirmed (L7.2 or a client's WhatsApp confirmation via L8.1), 
fee_note.created (2).
37.Pre-condition gate: on matter.created the chain first asks the instructing lawyer 'Has the client confirmed the engagement?' (WhatsApp/app  
prompt). If no, the matter carries status 'engagement unconfirmed' and accounts is not notified; the question is re-asked on each subsequent trigger.
38.Notification: on a confirmed engagement, P4 sends accounts a structured intimation (client, matter number, nature, instructing lawyer, fee note if 
any) by the accounts role's channels (email + WhatsApp).
39.Confirmation: accounts replies 'entered' (one tap / one word); the chain records accounts_entry_confirmed with timestamp and person, and notifies 
the instructing lawyer. No reply within a configurable period escalates in L4.10.
40.Register: a per-matter intimation record (triggered, asked, confirmed, entered) visible on the matter and in the dashboard (L4.8).
41.The accounts role is defined in L4.7 with read access to the intimation register only — no matter documents.
Key features
• Three-party confirmation loop on every new matter and filing.
• Client-confirmation gate before accounts is told.
• Escalation on silence.
• Per-matter intimation record; no financial logic.

L17.7 — Universal Authority Viewer (callable from any module)
Status. NEW. Source: instruction of 6 September 2026. Extends Modules 21, 22, 34; surfaces L17.1.
Purpose. Make any statute section, article, rule or reported judgment openable in a side panel from wherever it is mentioned — a draft, a research result, a 
chronology, a WhatsApp reply, a cause list — without leaving the current screen.
Why it earns its place. Module 34 is a library the user visits. The instruction is that authority should come to the user: a clerk reading an order, an  
associate drafting, a partner reviewing a critique should each be one tap from the provision or the judgment, in the version that applies.
How the developer builds it
42.Reference detection: a shared parser (the citation and statute patterns of Module 33 extended with Article/Order/Rule forms) tags every recognised 
reference in any rendered text with a stable authority id.
43.Panel: a platform-level side panel (web, mobile; on WhatsApp a numbered 'open [1]' reply) that renders the authority from the corpus via Module 
10 with L17.1's as_at date taken from the current matter, showing version and treatment (21's getTreatment) inline.
44.Actions from the panel: insert into draft (12), add to compendium (20), pin to matter, open full text in 34, report a bad extraction to the corpus  
quarantine (Annex B).
45.Available in read-only and offline modes (42) for pinned authorities.
46.Absent authorities (not in the corpus) show 'not in library — verify manually' and offer manual upload to L3.5.
Key features
• One-tap authority anywhere text is shown.
• Version-aware (L17.1) and treatment-aware.
• Insert / pin / compile actions from the panel.
• WhatsApp-compatible numbered access.

L17.8 — Auto-Clerk Ambiguity Queue & Case-Number Follow-up
Status. NEW (formalises the instruction of 6 September 2026). Extends Modules 11, 17, L7.1, L3.7.
Purpose. Give the Auto-Clerk its own explicit queue for documents it cannot confidently route, and its own tracked follow-up loop for case numbers not 
yet allotted by the registry.
Why it earns its place. L7.1 routes or asks the last associate; Module 11 splits and classifies. Neither specifies what happens to the residue — the  
documents that match two matters, none, or a client with no matter yet — or how a case-number chase is tracked to completion. The instruction is that  
ingestion, routing and follow-up are one module's responsibility with a visible queue.
How the developer builds it
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 22
=== PAGE 262 ===
47.Ambiguity queue: any routing decision below confidence 0.85, or with two candidate matters within 0.10 of each other, or with a party unresolved 
in L17.2, goes to the queue with the top-3 candidates and the reason. The queue is owned by the clerk role and visible in L4.10.
48.Resolution: one tap assigns; 'new matter' opens L7.2 pre-filled; 'not ours' archives to the loose-files vault (L4.5). Every resolution trains the router 
via a proposal to L17.5.
49.Case-number follow-up: on filing.recorded without a case number, a follow-up job (P2) is created with the court, branch and diary number; it  
reminds the clerk at configurable intervals, accepts the number by WhatsApp reply, validates the format per court, updates the matter (2) and the 
roster (35), and closes. Silence beyond threshold escalates to the instructing lawyer.
50.Bulk behaviour: L3.4/L9.1 uploads route in background; only the residue reaches the queue.
Key features
• Explicit ambiguity queue with reasons and candidates.
• One-tap resolution that teaches the router.
• Tracked case-number chase with court-format validation.
• Escalation on silence.
Representative functions

L17.9 — Court-Fee Calculator (reinstated) — per-type fee basis
Status. REINSTATED by Erratum E-3. Restores Module 44's calculateCourtFee and extends it.
Purpose. Compute the court fee payable on any document at filing under the Sindh Court Fees Act 2024 and the applicable schedules, from the document 
type and the value or nature of the relief, and produce the fee memo for the docket.
Why it earns its place. The instruction of 6 September lists the Court Fee Calculator among capabilities that must not be lost. Module 14 already carries a 
court-fee basis per document type; without the calculator that attribute has no consumer.
How the developer builds it
51.Fee rules table: per document type (14) → fee basis (fixed / ad valorem / per relief / exempt), schedule reference, valuation field required. Seeded 
for Sindh; jurisdiction-extensible.
52.Inputs: document type, court (courts seed table), valuation of relief or suit value, number of reliefs, exemptions claimed.
53.Output: fee amount, schedule and article relied on, and a fee memo paragraph inserted into the docket (L5.5) and shown in the per-type checklist 
(L5.4).
54.The rules table is master knowledge under L17.5 — amendments to the Act are applied as confirmed changes with effective dates (L17.1 applies).
55.Stamp duty remains excluded (E-4); the calculator does not compute it.
Key features
• Per-type fee basis, jurisdiction-extensible.
• Fee memo for the docket.
• Versioned rules with effective dates.

L17.10 — Legal Corpus Interface Contract (BYOD schema)
Status. NEW. Closes Errata E-7 and E-8. Defines what Module 10 maps to and what Modules 18, 21, 22, 34 and L17.1/L17.7 read. Full field tables in  
Annex A.
Purpose. State, once and precisely, the schema of the firm-controlled legal corpus that the application reads, so that any source — the firm's own external 
service, a licensed feed, an authenticated export — can be mapped to it and the application never depends on the internals of any one source.
Why it earns its place. The scraper prompt asserts its tables are 'identical' to the application's and need no mapping. Nothing in the set defines the target. 
A contract makes the BYOD model real: Module 10 maps to the contract; D5 reads only the contract.
How the developer builds it
56.Store: PostgreSQL 16, pgvector, pg_trgm; firm-owned; region per the data-residency decision (L17.13). Read-only role for the application; write 
role only for the corpus service (Annex B).
57.Entities: judgment, judgment_version (for corrigenda), citation (a judgment may have several reporter citations), treatment (followed /  
distinguished / overruled / per incuriam — from Authority Integrity, L17.11), statute, statute_section, statute_section_version (L17.1), instrument 
(SRO / notification / rule), court, judge, source_provenance (source, URL, fetched, hash, confidence, review status).
58.Every row carries provenance and a confidence score; the application never displays a record below the trust threshold without the 'unverified'  
marker; quarantine is a state, not a separate store.

L17.11 — Authority Integrity — Treatment Classification & Bench Competence
Status. CARRIED from the AWS lineage delta v5.1 ('Authority Integrity', built; no counterpart in v9). Extends Modules 21, 33; consumes L17.10.
Purpose. Record, for every judgment in the corpus, how later courts have treated it (followed, distinguished, doubted, overruled, per incuriam) and  
whether the bench that decided it was competent to bind — so that the citation gate checks currency and weight, not merely existence.
Why it earns its place. A correctly cited authority that has since been overruled, or a single-bench decision advanced as binding against a larger bench, is 
a live error in Pakistani practice. Module 21 'surfaces overrule flags' but nothing in the set produces them. Authority Integrity is the producer.
How the developer builds it
61.Treatment extraction: a nightly job over new judgments reads citations_cited and classifies the treatment of each cited case from the surrounding 
text via P1 (with the deterministic patterns first: 'overruled', 'distinguished', 'followed', 'per incuriam', 'not good law'). Output is a treatment row  
with confidence; below threshold it is queued for human review.
62.Bench competence: from the judge list and court, record bench size and type (single, division, full, larger); the gate flags any reliance on a smaller-
bench decision against a larger-bench one on the same point, and any High Court decision advanced as binding on another High Court.
63.Gate integration: Module 33 blocks approval where a relied-upon authority is overruled or doubted by a competent bench, and warns on smaller-
bench reliance; the reviewer (L11.1) shows the treating judgment.
64.Landmark scoring (citation_count) is a derived, recomputed field; it never substitutes for treatment.
Key features
• Treatment per cited authority, with provenance and confidence.
• Bench-size and competence recorded.
• Gate blocks on overruled authority; warns on weight.
• Human review queue for uncertain treatments.
Representative functions
Function Type Purpose
classifyTreatment scheduled Nightly treatment extraction
getTreatment GET Treatment history for a judgment (Module 21)
checkBenchCompetence internal Gate weight check
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 27
=== PAGE 267 ===
Function Type Purpose
reviewTreatment POST Human confirmation of a low-confidence classification
Wiring Detail
Reads from Corpus judgments and citations (L17.10).
Writes to treatment rows; gate findings (33).
Human control Low-confidence classifications are reviewed by an associate before the gate relies on them.
Depends on L17.10; Modules 21, 33; L11.1; P1.
Desired output. An authority the firm cites is one the firm knows is still good law and knows how much it weighs.
L17.12 — External Corpus Service Governance (the standalone scraper, outside the app)
Status.  GOVERNING/CARRIED.  Records  how  the  firm's  external  corpus  service  (the  31-file  Python  service  specified  in  
FINAL_SCRAPER_PROMPT_1) relates to the app after Erratum E-6. Corrections to the prompt itself are in Annex B.
Purpose. Keep the corpus service outside the application, lawful in its sources, and compliant with the interface contract — so that the application's 'no 
scraper ships in the product' decision remains true in fact and not only in wording.
Why it earns its place. The service exists and is the firm's chosen way to populate its corpus. It must be governed like any other supplier of data the firm 
relies on in court: lawful sources, provenance, quarantine, and a schema the app can trust.
How the developer builds it
65.Deployment boundary: separate repository, separate cloud project, separate credentials; the application holds only a read-only connection string to 
the corpus (Module 10).
66.Source policy (from E-6): public government portals (Supreme Court, Sindh High Court and other High Courts, Federal Shariat Court,  
pakistancode.gov.pk, National Assembly, provincial assemblies, Gazette) — full ingestion with robots.txt respect and polite rate limits; open non-
governmental sites — ingestion with provenance and 'review' trust until verified; subscription databases behind login — ingestion only by licensed 
API or authenticated manual export; automated login rotation is disabled by configuration.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 28
=== PAGE 268 ===
67.Quarantine as state: the service's quarantine_queue is the review workflow; the application shows quarantined records only to reviewers, never in 
research results.
68.Schema compliance: the service writes to the contract (Annex A) directly; the 'pg_dump into SIKANDER AI' path is withdrawn — the corpus is 
the firm's store, and the app connects to it (E-7).
69.Seed data ownership: the service does not seed limitation periods or court fee rules; those are master knowledge owned by Module 44 / L17.9  
under L17.5 (the service's limitation_periods table is dropped — Annex B).
70.Embedding model: one model, recorded in corpus metadata; the application reads it from testConnection.
Key features
• Hard deployment boundary between corpus service and app.
• Source policy aligned with the 21 August ruling.
• Quarantine visible only to reviewers.
• Writes to the contract; no dump/restore path.
• Legal seed data owned by the app, not the service.
Wiring Detail
Reads from Public portals; licensed feeds; manual exports.
Writes to Corpus (contract tables) only.
Human control Quarantine review daily for the first fortnight of each new source, weekly thereafter; source additions are partner-approved.
Depends on L17.10; Annex A; Annex B; Module 10.
Desired output. A corpus the firm owns, populated lawfully, read by the app through one door.

=== PAGE 279 ===
Annex A — Legal Corpus Interface Contract (field level)
This is the shape Module 10 maps to and Modules 18, 21, 22, 34, L17.1, L17.7 and L17.11 read. Types are PostgreSQL. Every table carries id (uuid),  
created_at, updated_at, source_id (→ source_provenance), confidence (0–1) and review_status (promoted | quarantined | rejected). Only promoted rows 
are visible to research; quarantined rows are visible to reviewers only.
A.1 judgment
Field Type Meaning
case_title text Parties as reported
court_id uuid → court Deciding court
bench_size / bench_type int / enum single · division · full · larger — for L17.11
judge_ids uuid[] → judge Bench
decision_date date Date of judgment
full_text text Complete text, HTML stripped
headnotes / summary text As reported / first 500 words
statutes_cited jsonb [{statute_id, section_number, as_at}] — resolved where possible
citations_cited uuid[] → citation Resolved cross-references
is_landmark / citation_count bool / int Derived, recomputed nightly; never substitutes for treatment
classification enum Always 'public' for reported/portal judgments; L3.5 uploads may be higher
embedding vector(d) d recorded in corpus_metadata.embedding_model
A.2 citation
Field Type Meaning
judgment_id uuid → judgment A judgment may carry several reporter citations (PLD and SCMR)
SIKANDER AI — Layer 17 · Reconciliation, Completion & Amendment No. 1 · 7 September 2026 · Page 40
=== PAGE 280 ===
Field Type Meaning
citation_text text UNIQUE Normalised, e.g. 'PLD 2019 SC 456', '2021 SCMR 1234'
reporter / year / volume_or_court / page text / int / text / int Parsed components
A.3 treatment (L17.11)
Field Type Meaning
treating_judgment_id / treated_judgment_id uuid → judgment Which case treated which
treatment enum followed · applied · distinguished · doubted · overruled · per_incuriam · referred
passage_anchor text Paragraph / page in the treating judgment
treating_bench_type enum For competence comparison
confidence / review_status float / enum Below threshold → reviewer queue
A.4 statute · statute_section · statute_section_version (L17.1)
Field Type Meaning
statute: name / short_name / year_enacted / 
jurisdiction / status
text / text / int / enum / enum jurisdiction: Federal · Sindh · Punjab · KP · Balochistan · ICT · GB · AJK; status: in force · 
repealed · lapsed
statute_section: statute_id / 
section_number / chapter / current_title
uuid / text / text / text Stable identity of a section across versions

version_no / effective_from / effective_to /

Annex B — Amendment to FINAL_SCRAPER_PROMPT_1 (external corpus service)
The prompt is a specification for a service outside the application. It is not part of the architecture set and is not bound into it; but because it populates the 
corpus the application relies on in court, its conflicts with the set are corrected here. Each item names the prompt section and gives the replacement  
instruction verbatim, so the prompt file can be updated by direct substitution.
No. Prompt section Defect Replacement instruction (verbatim)
B-1 Section 1, Source 1 
(PakistanLawSite); Section 5 
(dual-credential failover); Section 
6 (PakistanLawSite discovery)
Automated login with two rotating credential slots against 
a subscription database — the conduct excluded by the 21 
August ruling and by decision #1 as amended (E-6).
'Source 1 is ingested ONLY by (a) a licensed API where the publisher 
offers one, or (b) authenticated manual export files placed in the 
/imports directory by a human. The service does not log in to Source 
1. Section 5 (CredentialManager, scrape_with_auth) is retained in the 
codebase for licensed-API token handling only; the login-form 
discovery, credential-slot rotation and failover logic are disabled by 
configuration (ALLOW_LOGIN_SCRAPING=false, non-overridable 
in production) and their tests are marked skipped. The dashboard 
credential card is replaced by an API-key card and an import-folder 
status card.'
B-2 Section 1, Sources 4 and 5 (SHC, 
Supreme Court) — trust level 
'review'
Consistent with E-6 (public government portals may be 
ingested in full); trust level stands.
No change to scope. Add: 'Provincial High Court portals (Lahore, 
Peshawar, Balochistan, Islamabad) and the Federal Shariat Court are 
added as Sources 7–11 with identical Playwright/PDF handling and 
review trust. Provincial assembly legislation portals and the Gazette 
of Pakistan are added as statute sources with trusted trust.'
