=== PAGE 287 ===
0. Rulings
0.1 Status of the three documents. The Cruel Critic Audit (8 September) is accepted as an engineering review, not as an architecture authority: its valid 
findings are adopted in §1, its errors are recorded in §2. The v11 Master Implementation Prompt and the v11.1 Corrected Specification are absorbed into 
this layer and superseded as standalone documents. Their central contribution — regrouping the build into 23 services with namespaced routes, defined 
`internal` semantics, idempotency, pagination, rate limits, feature-flagged amendment layers and gates as scripts — is adopted in full, with the defects in 
§2 corrected.
0.2 Precedence. Layer 17 (Document 25) governs what the system is. Layer 18 (this document) governs how it is built. On any conflict between the two, 
Layer 17 governs on scope and rules and Layer 18 governs on implementation form. v11.1's self-declared precedence ('v11.1 > v11 > Spec v10 + L17') is 
withdrawn: the set has one lineage and changes travel as numbered layers under the Layer 17 §1.4 protocol. This is the same ruling made on 7 September 
against rev-17, applied again.
0.3 Court-fee calculator. The instruction of 8 September 2026 recorded in v11 §5 and v11.1 §1 removes the court-fee calculator. It is the later instruction 
and governs over the 6 September reinstatement. Erratum E-15 (§13) records the reversal: L17.9 is withdrawn; `calculateCourtFee`, `getFeeRule`,  
`insertFeeMemo` and the `courtFeeBasis` type attribute are deleted; Module 44 keeps limitation only; the docket (L5.5) is built without a fee memo.  
Valuation fields on the matter are retained as data. CI fails on the string `calculateCourtFee`.
0.4 'Zero feature loss' is a claim to be proved, not asserted. v11.1 asserts it; §12 demonstrates it by mapping every one of the 44 modules, 5 platform 
services, D7, and the 63 layer components to a service and an owner. Where the v11.1 map silently dropped something (Ghost Archive, Internal Precedent 
Discovery, Client Reporting, the Part D generators, L3.5–L3.7, L9.2–L9.5, the L12 suite, L13.3, L14.1, L15.2, L16.4), §12 restores it.
0.5 What Layer 18 does not do. It does not reopen any decision in the exclusion register, does not re-specify any module's purpose, and does not change 
the corpus contract (Layer 17 Annex A) except to add the platform tables and the corrected embedding definition in §5.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 18 · Implementation Consolidation · 9 September 2026 · Page 3

1. Disposition of the Audit's Twenty Findings
# Finding Disposition What Layer 18 does
1 TOC fragment / duplicate L14.1 Accepted Build Guide defect; the spec was clean. L14.1 is one component (User Personalization); 
the fragment is deleted from the Build Guide index.
2 'NXR' undefined Accepted Module 13 is renamed Annexure Numbering & Referencing Wizard (ANR). No other 
rename of module numbers.
3 159 types without catalogue Accepted with 
correction
The catalogue is seeded from the firm's Drafting DNA (decision #6), not invented by a 
vendor. Annex C in v11.1 is a schema for the seed file, not the catalogue; the catalogue 
itself is a Phase 1 deliverable from the firm (§13, O-7).
4 Module 40 before Module 1; random order Accepted as presentation Module numbers are identifiers, not build order. The 23-service view (§3) is the build 
order; module numbers stay as stable references.
5 Duplicate IDs / two dashboards Accepted L4.8 and L9.4 are one Command Dashboard owned by the Web Channel with role-
based views.
6 14 duplicate function names Accepted with 
correction
Namespacing alone is not the fix — one owner per function is. §3.2 assigns a sole 
owner to every duplicated name; other services call it. v11.1 itself left 
`recordCaseNumber` in three services.
7 `internal` undefined Accepted Defined in §4.1 as a P2 Cloud Task with idempotency key, retry policy, dead-letter 
queue and Opik trace; synchronous in-process calls are a separate type, `helper`.
8 No pagination Accepted §4.3.
9 No idempotency Accepted §4.2.
10 No rate limiting Accepted §4.4.
11 Wiring missing for 60% of modules Rejected as stated; 
requirement accepted
Wiring exists for all 44 modules in the Interconnection Matrix (Part V). The auditor did 
not read it. The requirement that each service carry its own wiring block is nonetheless 
adopted (§3).
12 Firestore/Postgres split unclear Accepted §5.4 defines the system of record per entity and forbids cross-store joins; the matter's 
relevant_law_dates are passed as parameters to corpus queries.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 18 · Implementation Consolidation · 9 September 2026 · Page 4
=== PAGE 289 ===
# Finding Disposition What Layer 18 does
13 Loose vault security hole Accepted with 
correction
v11.1's fix (clerk + partner, clearance ≥ confidential) is adopted, with one change: auto-
expiry does not delete — it escalates to L4.10 at 30 days and blocks new uploads to the 
vault until filed. Deleting a client's document because a clerk was slow is not acceptable.
14 MFA without recovery Accepted disableMfa (partner approval + recovery code), recoveryCodes, enforceMfaForRole, 
break-glass — §3 P5.
15 Privilege leak in translation and OCR Accepted Every external call — Vision, Whisper, ElevenLabs, translation — goes through P1 
with the aggregate classification check (L17.3). This was the intent of N2; it is now 
explicit per function.
16 Annex A missing; embedding lock theatre Rejected in part; 
accepted in part
Annex A exists (Layer 17). The missing pieces — corpus_metadata read by the app, 
getCorpusMetadata, reembedCorpus — are added in §5.1 and §3.
17 WhatsApp parser fantasy Accepted §8.
18 Temporal binding missing field / jurisdiction Accepted relevant_law_dates in Phase 0; getSectionAsAt(sectionId, asAtDate, jurisdiction); the 
approval router (L4.1) declares Module 33 as a hard dependency.
19 Entity resolution impossible Accepted with 
correction
§7 — but the 0.95 / 0.70 bands remain provisional until the firm's evaluation set is run; 
the audit's 'arbitrary' criticism is fair and the fix is a dataset, not a different arbitrary 
number.
20 Master writer lie Rejected as to the spec; 
accepted as to the Build 
Guide
The consolidated spec already rewrote Modules 15, 16, 30, L4.6 and L14.2 as proposers 
(Layer 17 E-9). The Build Guide had stale wiring. CI now fails on any master write 
outside P6.
Two audit findings are recorded as valid but outside Layer 18's scope: backup, soft delete and retention (finding 6, fourth bullet) become open item O-8 
with a six-year retention default pending the firm's confirmation of the applicable rule; and the cost model (finding 15) is adopted as provisional in §11.

=== PAGE 290 ===
2. Register of What Was Wrong — in the Audit and in v11.1
Each row names the error, why it matters, and the correction adopted. These are not matters of taste; each would have failed at build or in court.
# Error Why it matters Correction adopted
W-1 v11.1 §4.2: `embedding vector(3072)` with 
`CREATE INDEX … USING ivfflat`.
pgvector's ivfflat and hnsw indexes cap at 2,000 
dimensions. The Phase 0 DDL described as 'frozen' 
fails on its own index statement.
Embedding model text-embedding-3-small at 1,536 
dimensions with an HNSW cosine index (§5.1). If a 3,072-
dimension model is ever chosen, the column must be 
`halfvec(3072)`. corpus_metadata records model and 
dimension; the app refuses a mismatch.
W-2 v11.1 §4.2 trigger: `IF NEW.classification < 
OLD.classification` on TEXT labels.
String comparison: 'privileged' < 'public' 
alphabetically. The trigger would reject an upgrade 
to privileged and permit a downgrade to public — 
the exact inverse of L17.3.
A classification_rank table (public 0 … privileged-restricted 
4) and a trigger that compares ranks (§5.2). The Firestore 
rule uses the same rank map.
W-3 v11.1 §4.1 Firestore rule for /matters tests 
`resource.data.matterId == null`.
A matter has no matterId field; the rule is 
malformed and would not deploy or would deny 
everything.
Rules rewritten per collection (§5.3): matters by 
membership; documents with matterId == null by role and 
clearance.
W-4 v11.1 §4.2 courts seed: five courts, slash-only 
regexes.
Real Sindh filings read 'C.P. No. D-1234 of 2025', 
'Const. Petition No. D-…', 'Cr. Bail Appln. No. … 
of 2026', 'J.M. No. 7 of 2024', 'Suit No. 812 of 
2025'. The seed regexes reject all of them, so every 
genuine filing would enter the follow-up queue as 
malformed.
Normalise-then-match (§6): strip 'No.', dots and spaces, map 
'of' to '/', then match a canonical pattern per court. Seed 
covers the Supreme Court, all five High Courts and their 
benches, the Federal Shariat Court, Karachi's district courts, 
the Banking Court, NAB Accountability Court and the Sindh 
Labour Court.
W-5 v11.1 places `recordCaseNumber` and 
`openCaseNumberFollowUp` in D1-2, D3-2 and 
D6-1; `syncBodyReferences` in D3-2 and D4-1; 
`reportExtraction` in D3-3 and D5-1.
It commits the duplication sin it claims to cure; 
namespacing hides the collision rather than 
resolving ownership. Three services owning one 
write path is three implementations of one rule.
One owner per function (§3.2). Others call the owner.
W-6 v11.1 N1 puts supremecourt.gov.pk and 
sindhhighcourt.gov.pk in the in-app allowlist and 
bans Playwright by CI.
Contradicts Layer 17 E-6: the app fetches official 
legislation only; judgments come through the 
external corpus service, which legitimately uses 
Playwright. Banning Playwright repo-wide would 
forbid the corpus service if it shares the repo.
In-app allowlist is legislation only (L4.9). The corpus service 
is a separate repository (Layer 17 L17.12); the CI rule 
applies to the app repository.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 18 · Implementation Consolidation · 9 September 2026 · Page 6
=== PAGE 291 ===
# Error Why it matters Correction adopted
W-7 Audit §8: 'Annex A not included'. It is Layer 17 Annex A. The v11.1 re-creation 
dropped the instrument and judge tables and the 
review_status column on every table — the 
quarantine-as-state model — and gave treatment 
three judgment foreign keys where two are needed.
Layer 17 Annex A stands. §5.1 adds only corpus_metadata 
(as the app reads it), corpus_embeddings, and the platform 
tables.
W-8 v11.1 'zero feature loss' with no mapping. Fourteen components are absent from the 23-
service map (§0.4).
§12 maps all 113 units.
W-9 Audit §5: 'wiring missing for 60% of modules'. Wiring for all 44 modules is the Interconnection 
Matrix (Part V).
Requirement kept; assertion corrected.
W-10 PBC 'Rule 85' (conflicts) and 'Rule 134' 
(solicitation) cited as settled in code comments and 
tests.
Rule numbering under the Pakistan Legal 
Practitioners and Bar Councils Rules 1976 must be 
verified before it is written into software or shown 
to a client.
Both references carry [REQUIRES VERIFICATION] in §3 
and §8 until the firm confirms the rule numbers.
W-11 v11.1 §10 cost model presented as 'viable'. Several figures are unsupported (WhatsApp 
Business conversation pricing; Cloud SQL sizing 
for pgvector over a six-figure judgment corpus; 
Opik storage at 10 GB). A wrong cost model is 
worse than none because it becomes a promise.
Adopted as provisional (§11), replaced by metered numbers 
at G-1.
W-12 Audit §16 doubts whether the Sindh Court Fees Act 
2024 is notified.
Irrelevant after the removal, but the doubt itself is 
unverified.
Recorded; no action — the calculator is removed.
W-13 v11.1 §4.1 loose-vault auto-expiry 'to force filing' 
deletes at 30 days.
Destroying a client's document to enforce clerical 
discipline is not acceptable in a law firm.
Expiry escalates and blocks further uploads to the vault; it 
never deletes (§3 D3-1).
W-14 Audit §2.2: L-numbers 'chaos', 'no legend'. The legend is Index §3: Lx.y = Layer x, component 
y. L17.x are Layer 17 components, not amendments 
of a different kind.
No change; legend restated in §12.

4. Engineering Standards
4.1 Call types
Type Definition
GET / POST / PUT HTTP under /api/{domain}/{service}/{function}; headers Authorization (Bearer), X-Firm-Id; POST/PUT carry Idempotency-Key. Duplicate route 
registration fails the build (static check).
internal A P2 Cloud Task: { jobType, payload, classification, idempotencyKey, retryPolicy: exponential 1s/5s/25s, max 3, deadLetterQueue, opikTraceId }. Used 
for anything that calls an external provider, takes more than 2 seconds, or must survive a process crash: OCR, split, classify, embed, translate, transcribe, 
treatment classification, re-pointing after un-merge. Never a direct import.
helper A synchronous in-process function with no side effects on external systems (pure computation, validation, formatting). May be imported directly. Must 
not call P1 or any provider.
scheduled A P2 job with a cron expression stated in the service README, idempotent per run key (jobType + period), with a backfill command.
event Published on the event bus with { name, firmId, payload, classification, occurredAt, idempotencyKey }; subscribers are at-least-once and idempotent.
4.2 Idempotency
• Every POST/PUT requires Idempotency-Key (UUID v4). The server stores key → response for 24 hours per firm; a repeat returns the stored  
response with reused: true. Jobs carry the same key so a retried HTTP call does not enqueue a second job.
• Content-addressed dedup for uploads: SHA-256 of the file + firmId; a duplicate returns the existing docId.
4.3 Pagination
• Every list GET accepts limit (default 20, max 100) and cursor; returns { items, nextCursor, total? } where total is included only when cheap.  
Firestore cursors are opaque document snapshots; Postgres cursors are keyset (id, created_at).
4.4 Rate limits
• Per user: 60 requests/minute general; 10/minute for P1-backed operations (research, draft, translate, transcribe, conflict). Per firm: 100/day for  
conflict checks and 500/day for research from the WhatsApp channel. 429 with Retry-After. Limits are feature-flag configurable per firm (P5).
SIKANDER AI — Layer 18 · Implementation Consolidation · 9 September 2026 · Page 12
=== PAGE 297 ===
4.5 Permission check
checkPermission(userId, action, matterId?) in P5 reads the user's claims (firmId, role, clearanceLevel) and the matter membership record; returns  
allow/deny with reason; logs to auditPrivilegeAccess. It is the single authority for web, mobile and WhatsApp (R-1). A channel may pass additional  
restrictions to it (e.g. client self-service passes scope = own_matters) but may never pass elevations.
4.6 Classification ranks
Label Rank External-call policy (P1 provider table default)
public 0 Any configured provider.
internal 1 Any configured provider.
confidential 2 Providers with an executed DPA.
privileged 3 Providers with an executed DPA; content minimisation applied; trace PII-scrubbed.
privileged-restricted 4 In-region providers only per the firm's residency decision (L17.13a/h); otherwise deterministic-only or 
partner-authorised exception with recorded reason.

CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION enforce_monotonic_classification() RETURNS TRIGGER AS $$
DECLARE old_rank SMALLINT; new_rank SMALLINT;
BEGIN
  IF TG_OP = 'UPDATE' AND NEW.classification IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.classification THEN
    SELECT rank INTO old_rank FROM classification_rank WHERE label = OLD.classification;
    SELECT rank INTO new_rank FROM classification_rank WHERE label = NEW.classification;
SIKANDER AI — Layer 18 · Implementation Consolidation · 9 September 2026 · Page 15
=== PAGE 300 ===
    IF new_rank IS NULL THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'Unknown classification %', NEW.classification; END IF;
    IF new_rank < old_rank AND current_setting('app.declassify_reason', true) IS NULL THEN
      RAISE EXCEPTION 'Classification downgrade rejected: use declassify() with a partner reason';
    END IF;
  END IF;
  RETURN NEW;
END; $$ LANGUAGE plpgsql;

function sameFirm() { return request.auth.token.firmId == resource.data.firmId; }

  return exists(/databases/$(database)/documents/matter_members/$(matterId + '_' + request.auth.uid)); }

6. Courts Seed and Case-Number Normalisation (Corrected — W-4)
Pakistani case numbers are written inconsistently across cause lists, orders and filings: 'C.P. No. D-1234 of 2025', 'CP D-1234/2025', 'Const. Petition No. 
D-1234/2025', 'Suit No. 812 of 2025', 'Cr. Bail Appln. No. 456 of 2026', 'J.M. No. 7 of 2024', 'H.C.A. No. 22 of 2025', 'Criminal Appeal No. 89 of 2024'. A 
single regex per court cannot match them. The rule is normalise first, then match a canonical form.
6.1 Normalisation (helper, deterministic)
1. Upper-case; collapse whitespace.
2. Remove the tokens 'NO', 'NO.', 'NUMBER'.
3. Replace ' OF ' with '/'; replace '-' between a bench letter and digits with '-' retained (D-1234); remove dots inside abbreviations (C.P. → CP; J.M. → 
JM; H.C.A. → HCA).
4. Expand known synonyms to a canonical prefix: CONST PETITION | CONSTITUTION PETITION | CONSTITUTIONAL PETITION | CP →  
CP; CRIMINAL BAIL APPLICATION | CR BAIL APPLN | CRL BA → CRBA; CRIMINAL APPEAL | CRL A → CRA; CIVIL SUIT | SUIT → 
SUIT; GUARDIAN AND WARDS | G&W → GW; JUDICIAL MISCELLANEOUS | JM → JM; JCM → JCM; HIGH COURT APPEAL | HCA 
→ HCA; CIVIL PETITION | CPLA → CPLA (Supreme Court); CIVIL APPEAL → CA; REFERENCE → REF.
5. Result: PREFIX [BENCH-]NUMBER/YEAR, e.g. 'CP D-1234/2025', 'SUIT 812/2025', 'CRBA 456/2026', 'JM 7/2024'.
6.2 Canonical pattern and seed
Canonical (after synonym resolution): PREFIX [BENCH-]NUMBER/YEAR — the prefix is any token in the court's allowed_prefixes list, of any length 
(Erratum Set No. 1, W-15: the earlier {2,6} cap could not accept FAM or CIVILAPPEAL).
Per court (data/courts.json): allowed_prefixes[], bench_letters[] (SHC: D, S, R, L, M), synonyms{}, ambiguous_prefixes{}. Year is mandatory; a 
missing year is REJECTED and a follow-up opened.
Court Jurisdiction Allowed prefixes Example (as written → canonical)
Supreme Court of Pakistan Federal CP, CONSTP, CPLA, CA, CRA, CRP, HRC, SMC 'C.P.L.A. No. 123 of 2024' → CPLA 123/2024; 'Const. 
P. No. 5 of 2024' → CONSTP 5/2024; a bare 'C.P. No. 
123 of 2024' → CP 123/2024 flagged AMBIGUOUS 
— confirmed by the clerk against the matter's 
document type, never auto-mapped (Erratum Set No. 1, 
W-16)

prefix is a data change, not a code change.

=== PAGE 305 ===
7. Entity Resolution — Implementation
1. Deterministic keys first: CNIC (13 digits, normalised), NTN, SECP registration number, phone (E.164 with Pakistani mobile prefixes). Any exact 
key match is a confirmed link candidate at 0.99 and is auto-confirmed after 30 days unless disputed.
2. Transliteration: ICU transliteration (Arabic-Latin) plus a firm-maintained urdu_sindhi_roman mapping table for names — محمد → Muhammad | 
Mohammad | Mohd | M.; علی → Ali | Aly; حسین → Hussain | Hussein | Husain; احمد → Ahmed | Ahmad; شیخ → Shaikh | Sheikh | Shekh; and Sindhi 
honorifics and suffixes (Bhutto, Jokhio, Baloch, Memon, Soomro) treated as family tokens. Matching is token-set ratio over transliterated Roman 
forms.
3. Score = weighted: name token-set 0.45; father's/husband's name 0.20; address 0.15; date of birth 0.10; secondary phone/email 0.10. Bands: ≥ 0.95 
candidate-confirmed pending review (30-day review window); 0.70–0.95 candidate queued to the responsible associate; < 0.70 no link. The bands 
are provisional (§2, finding 19): the firm supplies an evaluation set of 200 known-same and 200 known-different pairs from its records at G-1 and 
the thresholds are tuned to zero false merges on that set.
4. Conflict Service reads confirmed links only. Result states: CLEAR, FLAG, POSSIBLE_CANDIDATE_REVIEW; every result carries  
identityBasis.
5. Un-merge saga: (1) merge_event exists and is immutable; (2) unmergeEntity writes reverted=true and enqueues one P2 job per referencing  
collection (matters, documents, evidence, propositions, correspondence) with idempotencyKey = mergeEventId + collection; (3) each job re-
points by the pre-merge snapshot stored in the event; (4) any failure dead-letters and alerts L4.10; partner approves the retry; (5) there is no half-
state visible to users because the entity remains marked 'unmerge in progress' until all jobs complete.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 18 · Implementation Consolidation · 9 September 2026 · Page 21
=== PAGE 306 ===
8. WhatsApp Command Grammar
Message      = Command / Reply / FreeText
Command      = "/" Name [SP Args]
Name         = "matters" / "hearings" / "clients" / "drafts" / "research" / "presence" / "intake" / "open" / "help"
Args         = CaseRef / Date / Number / Query
CaseRef      = normalised case number (see §6)  -> exactly one matter
Date         = "today" / "tomorrow" / ISO date / dd-mm-yyyy
Number       = 1*DIGIT                          -> selects from the last numbered list in this thread
Query        = 1*CHAR
Reply        = ("open" / "kholo" / "کھولیںSP Number     ; opens item N of the last list via L17.7
FreeText     = anything else -> classified by D1-3 (intake) or answered by the matter thread assistant
Command Urdu aliases (typed or voice) Behaviour
/matters [Query | CaseRef]    میرےکیس،مقدمات،کیس No argument: the caller's own matters (membership), paginated 10 per message. Query: 
search within those. CaseRef: the single matter thread.
/hearings [Date]  سماعت،پیشی Hearings the caller is marked on for the date (default today) from D6-1.
/clients [Query]  کلائنٹ،موکل Clients the caller has matter membership with.
/drafts [CaseRef]  مسودہ،ڈرافٹ Drafts on the matter awaiting the caller; approval actions only if role allows.
/research Query  تحقیق،ریسرچ D5-1 search over the corpus; returns a numbered list; 'open N' renders the authority.
/presence [in | out] حاضری D6-1 court presence log (geofence or manual).
/intake  نیاکیس Starts the D1-3 intake dialogue.
/help مدد Command list in the caller's language.
Every command calls P5 checkPermission(userId, action, matterId) before any data is read (R-1). Client self-service (L8.1) runs on a separate number  
scope: the client sees only own matters and hearings and cannot invoke /research or /drafts. Solicitation and advertising restrictions under the Legal  
Practitioners and Bar Councils Rules apply to every outbound client message — the specific rule number cited in v11.1 as 'Rule 134' is [REQUIRES  
VERIFICATION] before it appears in code or UI text. Inbound Urdu voice is transcribed by P1 (Whisper) and, where translation is needed, translated by 
P1 — both under the aggregate classification check; a privileged voice note never reaches a provider without a DPA. Rate limits per §4.4.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 18 · Implementation Consolidation · 9 September 2026 · Page 22
=== PAGE 307 ===
9. Amendment Layer Protocol (L18+)
Adopted from v11 §3 and v11.1 §7 with one correction: the protocol governs code; the Layer 17 §1.4 protocol governs the architecture document. A new 
feature needs both a layer folder and a numbered layer in the set.
9.1 Rules
1. Core is immutable after Gate G-1 for feature purposes: /platform, /services/23, the Phase 0 schema, the event catalogue. Bug fixes and security  
patches to core are permitted through the normal review path; features are not.
2. A new feature is a folder /layers/L{n}_{k}_{Name}/ containing manifest.json (id, name, version, depends_on, feature_flag, core_edits: false,  
events_subscribed, routes, ui), migration.sql (additive only: new tables, nullable columns with defaults), service.ts, routes.ts, ui.extension.tsx,  
health.ts (healthCheck(), disable()), README.md with the wiring block.
3. Coupling is through the event bus and the public service interfaces only; a layer never imports another service's internals.
4. Every layer is behind a feature flag with per-firm override and rollout percentage; disable() unsubscribes events, hides UI, leaves data intact; the 
core must boot and pass G-1 with every layer disabled.
5. A layer's learning proposes to P6; it never writes the master.
6. A layer that needs an external provider calls P1 with classification; it never calls a provider directly.
9.2 CI enforcement
Check Fails when
core-edit guard A PR touches /platform or /services and also adds or references a feature flag other than CORE.
duplicate-route guard Two registrations resolve to the same method + path.
master-write guard Any write to master tables or master_versions from a module other than P6.
provider guard Any import of an AI or OCR/STT/TTS provider SDK outside P1.
scraper guard (app repo) Any HTTP fetch to a host outside the L4.9 allowlist, or any Playwright/Puppeteer import in the app 
repository.
fee guard The string calculateCourtFee, getFeeRule, insertFeeMemo or courtFeeBasis appears anywhere.
idempotency guard A POST/PUT handler is registered without the idempotency middleware.

=== PAGE 308 ===

10. Gates as Scripts and the Forensic Test Matrix
Gate Script and what it proves
G-0 npm run gate:g0 — Postgres: extensions, Annex A tables and v_* views exist; corpus_reader can SELECT views and cannot SELECT base tables or INSERT; 
embedding column dimension equals corpus_metadata.embedding_dim and the HNSW index exists; classification_rank seeded; monotonic trigger attached to 
every artefact table and rejects a downgrade. Firestore: rules deploy; cross-firm read denied; non-member matter read denied; loose-vault read denied to intern. 
Courts: every seed example normalises and matches; a malformed example fails. Static: no duplicate routes, no fee strings, no provider imports outside P1, no 
scraper outside allowlist. Four-repository audit report present and signed.
G-1 npm run gate:g1 — the ten forensic tests below pass end to end with live provider keys and the WhatsApp webhook; Opik shows every call with classification 
and scrubbed PII.
G-2 npm run gate:g2 — two test firms seeded; every read function in D1-1, D1-2, D3-1, D5-1, D2-1 attempted cross-firm through web and WhatsApp returns deny; 
after the Postgres migration the same suite runs against RLS.
G-3 npm run gate:g3 — only P6 holds master write grants; a substantive proposal requires partner approval; applyChange writes a version and diff; rollbackMaster 
restores; every learner's write attempt outside proposeChange fails the master-write guard.
G-4 / G-5 Phase-close scripts: coverage of the completeness map for the phase's services (every owned function reachable and tested); disable-every-layer boot test.
10.1 Forensic tests (G-1)
# Test
1 Same PDF uploaded via web, team portal and bulk with three different Idempotency-Keys → one document, reused: true on the second and third, one OCR job.
2 WhatsApp /matters as a clerk with two matter memberships → exactly two matters; the same call as a client → own matters only; /research as a client → denied.
3 Two clients 'Muhammad Ali' with the same CNIC and different phones → confirmed-candidate link; a conflict check naming one → 
POSSIBLE_CANDIDATE_REVIEW with identityBasis, never CLEAR.
4 Draft citing a judgment recorded in treatment as overruled by a larger bench → gate result OVERRULED; approval routing blocked; partner reason required to override.
5 Draft pleading a PPC section for an offence dated 2015 where the section was amended in 2018 → getSectionAsAt returns the 2015 version; using current text → 
TEMPORAL-MISMATCH block with side-by-side view.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 18 · Implementation Consolidation · 9 September 2026 · Page 25
=== PAGE 310 ===
# Test
6 New matter without engagement confirmed → no intimation_register row, no accounts notification, status engagement_unconfirmed; after confirmation → accounts 
notified; no reply in 24 hours → L4.10 escalation.
7 Privileged document (classification privileged) → WhatsApp translation request → P1 blocks the provider without a DPA (ai.call.blocked) or requires a partner reason; 
the trace shows the classification.
8 Attempt to UPDATE an artefact's classification from privileged to internal without app.declassify_reason → trigger exception; with a partner's declassify() → succeeds 
and is audit-logged; derived artefacts keep their higher level.
9 Case-number entry 'C.P. No. D-1234 of 2025' on an SHC matter → normalised to CP D-1234/2025 and recorded; 'CP 1234' → rejected and follow-up opened.
10 corpus_metadata.embedding_model changed to a different model while embeddings exist → D5-1 refuses to search and D3-3 testConnection reports the mismatch; 
reembedCorpus job completes and the old vectors are retained until confirmation.

11. Cost Model — Provisional
v11.1's figures are adopted as a starting estimate only. Each line is replaced by a metered figure from Opik and the provider dashboards during Phase 1, 
and the model is re-issued at G-1. The purpose is to size budget alerts (P1 setBudgetAlerts at 70 and 90 percent), not to promise a price.
Item (10 lawyers, 20 filings/day, 50 pages/filing) v11.1 estimate / month Layer 18 note
Firestore reads/writes ~$10 Plausible; depends on dashboard polling — make dashboards event-driven.
Cloudflare R2 storage and egress ~$5 Plausible; egress is free within Cloudflare, so verify the assumption.
Postgres 16 + pgvector (managed) ~$50 Understated for HNSW over a six-figure judgment corpus with 1,536-dimension 
vectors; expect $100–200 for a suitably sized instance. Measure at G-1.
OCR (Vision) with Tesseract fallback ~$1.50 Plausible for printed text; Nastaliq/handwriting vendor (L17.13e) is unpriced.
Whisper STT / ElevenLabs TTS ~$4.50 Plausible at stated volumes.
LLM calls through P1 (deterministic-first) $30–50 Unverified; the 60% saving is a target, not a measurement. Budget alerts protect 
against error.
WhatsApp Business API ~$20 Unverified; conversation-based pricing varies by category and country. Measure.
Opik (self-hosted) ~$10 Storage grows with trace retention; set a retention policy (90 days full, 1 year 
summarised) or this line grows without bound.
Total $130–150 Treat as $200–350 until metered. Still viable for the firm; the point is to know, 
not to guess.

Annex C — The 159 Document Types (approved catalogue, 10 September 2026)
Source. The number 159 first appeared in the v4.1.1 architecture of 8 March 2026 as a category-count table with no names. The only named registry is  
document_types.py in the April 2026 codebase, which names 138 types across 12 categories. One of those — the Court Fee Calculation Sheet — is  
removed under Erratum E-15. The remaining 137, plus 22 additions proposed on 9 September to fill the shortfalls against the March category table, were 
approved by the partner on 10 September 2026 and together form the final catalogue of 159. The full per-type rules (courtIds, requiredFields,  
temporalBindingRule, annexureRule, checklist, templateId) are in the seed file; this annex lists the identity and the pleading-date rule of each type. Court 
fee and stamp duty appear in checklists as manual items only.

=== PAGE 338 ===
ES1-1. Register of Corrections (W-15 to W-22)
# Error Why it matters Correction adopted
W-15 Layer 18 §6.2 canonical pattern caps the prefix at 
six letters ([A-Z&]{2,6}).
FAMILYSUIT and CIVILAPPEAL cannot 
match; Family Court filings would be rejected.
Prefix is any token in the court's allowed_prefixes list; prefixes 
are data in data/courts.json; Family Court prefix FAM with 
synonyms FAMILY SUIT, FAMILYSUIT, FMS, F.S.
W-16 Layer 18 §6.2 example maps a Supreme Court 'C.P. 
No. 123 of 2024' to CPLA; the Correction Pack 
maps it to the Art. 184(3) track instead.
Both are wrong as automatic rules. At the 
Supreme Court 'C.P.' is the customary 
abbreviation for a Civil Petition (for leave to 
appeal), while Art. 184(3) matters are 'Const. 
P.'; but registries and practitioners are not 
uniform, and a wrong track binds the pleading 
date (L17.1).
Bare 'C.P.' at SC_PK normalises to CP and is flagged 
AMBIGUOUS; the clerk confirms the track against the 
matter's document type (cpla_185_3 or 
constitutional_petition_184_3). 'C.P.L.A.' and 'Civil Petition' 
→ CPLA; 'Const. P.' → CONSTP. Fixture-guarded.
W-17 Seed v1.0 contracts generated by category stencil: 6 
checklists, 11 requiredFields; corporate block 
carried 'exact land description'; the will and heirship 
affidavit carried plaintiff/valuation fields and 
vakalatnama; the s.497 first-ground rule was cloned 
onto appeals, 561-A, 22-A, FIA and 489-F 
complaints.
A clerk following the checklist would put the 
wrong first ground on an acquittal appeal and 
demand a land description for an NDA.
Seed v1.1: posture column (11 postures); firstGroundRule per 
posture with type overrides; requiredFields, annexureRule and 
checklist by posture and type; 36 distinct checklists, 88 distinct 
requiredFields; the antecedents ground on s.497 and s.498 
only; 'exact land description' on property rows only; removed 
type on its own sheet; arrays as arrays; ids unchanged. seed-
clone-guard enforces all of this.
W-18 Layer 18 §12 gave D6-1 Court Operations 
ownership of the limitation engine.
Limitation is computed on the statute text in 
force at accrual and consumed first by the 
drafting checklist; court operations only need 
the alerts.
checkLimitation is owned by D4-2 Drafting Intelligence; D1-2 
stores relevant_law_dates; D6-1 raises the 90/30/7 alerts and 
reads diary dates.
W-19 Layer 18 §12 carried L17.8 as one row with two 
owners.
Two owners for one row contradicts the one-
owner rule.
L17.8a Auto-Clerk ambiguity queue → D3-2; L17.8b case-
number follow-up → D6-1.
W-20 Layer 18 §12 heading said '109 units' while §0.4 
said 113 and the table had its own count.
A count that is asserted three ways is not a 
count.
One counted line: units mapped = the row total; removed = 1; 
unmapped = 0. The two figures are withdrawn.
W-21 v6 Module 36 specifies 31 WhatsApp commands; 
Layer 18 §8 specifies 8; the 23 were silently 
dropped.
A silent drop is a feature loss the completeness 
map is meant to prevent.
Feature flag WA_31_CUT (default off, Phase 4) records the 
deferred commands; the §12 row for Module 36 says so.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 18 · Erratum Set No. 1 · 10 September 2026 · Page 3
=== PAGE 339 ===
# Error Why it matters Correction adopted
W-22 Layer 18 §5.3 wrote Firestore rules for two 
collections and G-0 claimed the practice graph was 
covered.
Every uncovered collection is default-deny in 
Firestore, which blocks the application, or 
default-allow in a careless deploy, which leaks 
it.
firestore.rules now has a block for every Phase 0 collection 
with the Correction Pack's minimum policy (master_versions 
client-write denied; audit_log, custody_events, versions 
append-only; merge_events revert-only; default deny). Guard 
[10] checks coverage.

LAYER 19 — RESEARCH NOTES, CITATION & STATUTE VIEWERS, NATURAL-LANGUAGE RESEARCH AND 
ADVISORY MODE
Additive layer under the §1.4 Amendment Layer Protocol · specification, data model, ownership, wiring, tests · forensic audit and corrections bound in Part C
Prepared for Advocate Shamail Sikander · NHS Legal, Karachi · 11 September 2026
Status: GOVERNING for Phase 3 research and viewer work. Precedence: Layer 18 Erratum Set No. 1 > Layer 18 > Layer 17 > this layer where they conflict; this layer 
governs everything it introduces. It adds; it does not amend earlier layers except where Part C says so.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 1
=== PAGE 344 ===
Part A — What this layer is and the rulings it rests on
L19.0 Scope
Layer 19 delivers the research working environment the partner described on 10 and 11 September 2026: a shelf of research notes, one per clarified legal 
proposition, kept alive by the corpus; a Citation Viewer and a Statute Viewer with a real statute tree; research by plain English, spoken or typed, for cases 
and for law; and an advisory mode. It absorbs and supersedes Modules 18 (Research Engine — search portion only), 20 (Compendium — as a note  
export), 21 (Citation Viewer), 22 (Statutes Viewer) and L17.11 (Authority Viewer) as their governing specification. Their ids survive as routes; their  
behaviour is defined here.
L19.0.1 Rulings
1. A research note is matter work product. It is created, stored and classified in the application, never in the corpus service. The corpus service never 
learns what the firm is working on (D4).
2. The house note template is master knowledge: it is written by P6 only, on a proposal the partner confirms, versioned and rollbackable. Every note 
renders from the template in force at its last edit; re-rendering to a new template is a proposal, not an automatic rewrite.
3. The system proposes; the lawyer decides. No citation is ever removed from a note, no note is ever created from a system-noticed proposition, and 
no swap is ever applied, without a human tap. The ranking chooses what to propose.
4. Nothing is deleted. A replaced citation moves to the note's Removed list with its order, date, reason and replacement, and is restorable. Notes are 
versioned; every version is readable.
5. Every research request runs through P1 with the matter's classification. Party names, CNICs and contentions are masked before any provider sees 
them; the corpus receives an anonymised query only. Privileged and privileged-restricted matters require a provider with a DPA or the  
deterministic path.
6. The Citation Viewer and Statute Viewer are features of the application (D5-2 Library), reading the corpus through the read-only connector. The 
corpus service keeps only an inspection screen for the clerk — counts, and open one record to confirm it arrived correctly. The corpus receives an 
as-at date and anonymised queries; never a matter, party or contention.
7. The proposition is clarified before any search runs. A note has one proposition, in one or two sentences, accepted by a lawyer. Research is an  
answer to a stated question.
8. The WhatsApp grammar stays at eight commands (Layer 18 §8). Research on WhatsApp uses the existing /research command with arguments; no 
ninth command is added.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 2
=== PAGE 345 ===
Part B — Specification
L19.1 The research note
A note is a document with a fixed structure and a house-styled rendering. The structure is data; the rendering is the template.
Part Field Content
1 Proposition proposition One or two sentences stating the law point as a question or a statement to be tested. Accepted by a lawyer. 
Immutable after acceptance; a changed proposition is a new note linked to the old.
area · forum · 
asAtDate
Area of law (from the 12 categories plus sub-area), the forum the note is written for, and the date the law is 
pleaded as at (taken from the linked matters' relevant_law_dates; the earliest where several).
matters[] Matters the note feeds. Adding a matter never changes the proposition.
2 Reasoning settledPosition What the law is, in the firm's voice, with pinpoint references to the numbered citations.
split · qualifications Where benches or High Courts differ; conditions, exceptions, procedural traps.
leadingAuthority The one citation the note would rely on first, and why.
3 Citations in force citations[] Numbered from 1. Each: canonical citation, parties, court, bench size, date, ratio in one line, treatment status 
(from corpus), pinpoint pages, score, role (leading / supporting / contrary), corpus id.
4 Statutes engaged statutes[] Section references with the edition in force at asAtDate, corpus statute_section_version id, and one line on what 
the section does for the proposition.
5 Removed removed[] Numbered in order of removal. Each: the full citation entry as it stood, removedAt, removedBy, reason 
(replaced / overruled / doubted / declined by lawyer / duplicate), replacedByCitationNo, restorable=true.
6 History versions[] Every saved version with author, timestamp, what changed.
Metadata classification · 
createdBy · origin · 
templateVersion
Classification = the highest of the linked matters (monotonic, L17.3). origin  {asked, noticed, drafting}. ∈
templateVersion = master template version used to render.
Default house template (in force until the partner replaces it through the template manager): Title = proposition; block 'Position' (settledPosition); block 
'Where courts differ' (split) if any; block 'Leading authority'; block 'Authorities relied on' as a numbered list with ratio and treatment; block 'Statutory  
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 3
=== PAGE 346 ===
provisions' with edition dates; block 'Removed authorities' as a numbered list with reasons; footer with note id, version, as-at date and classification. Times 
New Roman 12/14, 1.5 spacing, A4. The partner's own format, when dictated, replaces this by proposal.
L19.2 How a note begins — three origins
Origin Behaviour
A asked The lawyer speaks or types a contention. L19.5 extracts the proposition and shows it back: 'I read this as: …'. The lawyer corrects or accepts. On 
acceptance the note is created and the first research run starts.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 4
=== PAGE 347 ===
Origin Behaviour
B noticed Nightly, the Matter Intelligence job (owner D4-2) reads each active matter's pleadings, orders, sections charged and relief sought, and extracts 
candidate propositions. A candidate whose semantic match to an existing note's proposition is ≥ 0.85 links the matter to that note instead. New 
candidates are placed in the shelf's 'Suggested' section with the matter that produced them. Nothing runs until a lawyer accepts the title. Suggestions 
not acted on in 30 days are archived, not deleted.
C drafting When the Drafting Factory meets a point of law for which no note exists (no note with match ≥ 0.85 to the proposition it is about to plead), it opens a 
note in Suggested with origin=drafting and pauses the outline for the lawyer to accept or dismiss it. The draft may continue without a note; the gate 
still checks each citation.
L19.3 The first research run
1. Query build: the proposition, the sections it names, the area, the forum and the asAtDate become a corpus query through the connector — semantic 
(embedding of the proposition) plus citation and section matches, filtered to material decided on or before asAtDate unless the lawyer widens it.
2. Scoring: every hit is scored by L19.4. The top N in force (default 12, research_config.maxInForce) become citations 1…N in score order; the rest 
with score 50–69 are listed under 'also consider' inside the note, not numbered.
3. Reasoning: P1 drafts settledPosition, split, qualifications and leadingAuthority from the ratios of the in-force citations, in the firm's voice, with the 
deterministic-first rule (clause library and prior notes on the same area are offered before a provider is called).
4. Rendering: the note is rendered from the master template in force. The lawyer edits in the embedded editor; edits are versioned.
5. The whole run is one P2 job with idempotency key note_id:run_no; a re-run never duplicates a citation (matched on corpus id).
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 5
=== PAGE 348 ===
L19.4 Ranking and replacement
Score (0–100) = 35·propositionMatch + 20·bindingForForum + 15·benchAndCourt + 10·recency + 10·treatmentHealth + 10·treatsExisting. Definitions: 
propositionMatch = cosine similarity of the judgment's headnote and ratio embedding to the proposition embedding, rescaled 0–1, plus 0.15 if the  
judgment cites a section the note names (capped at 1). bindingForForum = 1 if the deciding court binds the note's forum (Supreme Court everywhere; the 
same High Court for its subordinate courts; larger bench over smaller), 0.6 for persuasive superior-court authority, 0.3 otherwise. benchAndCourt = 1 for 
Supreme Court, scaled by bench size (5+ = 1, 3 = 0.85, 2 = 0.7, 1 = 0.55); High Court 0.8 scaled the same; others 0.4. recency = (year − 1980)/(currentYear 
− 1980) clipped to [0,1]. treatmentHealth = 1 followed/undisturbed, 0.6 distinguished, 0 doubted or overruled. treatsExisting = 1 if the judgment follows, 
distinguishes or overrules a citation already in the note. All six weights live in research_config.ranking and are changed only by proposal.
Outcome Rule
Ignored score < 50. Logged to note.runLog; not shown.
Also consider 50 ≤ score < 70. Shown in the note's 'also consider' list; never numbered.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 6
=== PAGE 349 ===
Outcome Rule
Proposed replacement score ≥ 70 and score > (lowest in-force citation's score + 8). The proposal names the citation it would replace. Shown as a shelf 
badge and inside the note. Lawyer: Accept (old citation → Removed, reason 'replaced', replacedBy set), Decline (proposal 
suppressed for this note; the judgment stays in 'also consider'), Keep both (new citation appended as N+1 if N < maxInForce; 
else declined).
Treatment alert A new judgment with treatmentHealth 0 that hits an in-force citation (overrules or doubts it) is never proposed in force; it raises 
an alert on that citation: 'doubted/overruled by …'. The lawyer moves the citation to Removed (reason 'overruled'/'doubted') or 
records a reason to keep it. The citation gate reads the same corpus treatment table, so a draft citing it will block regardless.
Never automatic No path applies a change without a human tap. Every decision is logged with user, time and reason; the note version increments.
L19.5 Natural-language research — cases and law
1. Input: speech (Whisper via P1, Urdu/English) or text, from the research workspace, either viewer, a matter, a draft, or WhatsApp /research <text>.
2. Mask and classify: P1 masks party names, CNICs, phone numbers and addresses and carries the matter's classification; the provider is chosen by 
the L17.3 policy table. The masked text is what leaves the application.
3. Extract: P1 returns a structured reading — proposition (one sentence), material facts (list), area, forum, asAtDate (from the matter if attached, else 
today), mode  {cases, law, both}, and the sections it recognises.∈
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 7
=== PAGE 350 ===
4. Show the reading: the lawyer sees 'I read this as: …' with the facts and mode; corrects inline or accepts. This is the same clarification step as L19.2 
and is never skipped.
5. Search: mode cases → corpus semantic + citation + section search with as-at filter; mode law → provision search over statute_node and  
statute_section_version (title, text, definitions) returning the section as it stands today and as at the date; mode both → both.
6. Score and explain: cases scored by L19.4; each result carries one line, generated by P1 from the ratio, on why it matters to the stated facts. Law  
results carry the section text, its edition dates, and a plain explanation of what it does and does not do for the situation.
7. Save: → New note (proposition pre-filled) or → into an open note (citation appended through the citation gate) or → pin to the matter. On  
WhatsApp the results come back as a numbered list; 'open n' opens the authority.
L19.6 The research shelf
Element Specification
List One row per note: proposition (title), area, forum, in-force count, removed count, matters fed, last change, badge 'n proposed 
changes' / 'treatment alert'. Sort by last change; filter by area, matter, forum, origin, alerts. Search across propositions.
Suggested A section above the list holding system-noticed and drafting-origin propositions awaiting acceptance, each with the matter that 
produced it, Accept / Edit title / Dismiss.
Open note Full note (L19.1) in the embedded editor; side rail with proposals and alerts; Removed list; History; Export (PDF/DOCX in house 
format = the compendium of Module 20); Share to matter team; Cite into draft (through the gate).
Counts line Notes in force · citations in force · removed · proposals pending · alerts pending, for the firm and per user.
Permissions A note is visible to members of any of its matters and to partners; a note with no matter is visible to its creator and partners. 
Classification rules apply to export and share.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 8
=== PAGE 351 ===
L19.7 Citation Viewer and Statute Viewer (application features, corpus-fed)
Screen Specification
Citation Viewer (application, D5-2) Browse by reporter → year → court → page; by subject (corpus subject index); by court and date. Open a judgment: header (citation, 
parties, court, bench with size, date), headnote, full text with paragraph anchors, statutes cited (linked to the tree), cases cited, cited-by with 
treatment, provenance and extraction confidence, 'Report extraction' (writes to corpus quarantine). Full-text and semantic search boxes. 
The as-at date comes from the matter; treatment as at that date is shown first.
Statute Viewer (application, D5-2) Jurisdiction → Act → Part → Chapter → Section → sub-section tree (statute_node). Open a section: text of the version in force at the 
passed date, all versions with effective_from/to, the amending instrument per version, definitions referenced, cases citing the section (via 
corpus). Diff between any two versions. Search by section number, title words, or text.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 9
=== PAGE 352 ===
Screen Specification
Statute tree New corpus table statute_node (id, statute_id, parent_id, kind  {act, part, chapter, section, subsection, clause, schedule}, label, title, ∈
ordinal, path) built by the corpus service's statute parser from the Act's own structure; statute_section_version rows link to their section 
node. Additive to Annex A: no existing column changes. The connector's mapping step exposes it.
Opening from anywhere Both viewers open as the side panel of L17.11 from any citation or section reference in a draft, note, search result or WhatsApp list ('open 
n'). Actions: Insert (through the citation gate), Pin to matter, Add to note, Report. The application passes the as-at date only; never a matter, 
party or contention.
Corpus inspection screen (corpus 
dashboard only)
Not a viewer: counts of judgments, statute sections and versions per source and per reporter-year; open any one record to confirm 
extraction, treatment and versions arrived correctly; Report/Reject to quarantine. No research, no natural-language search, no matter 
context. Everything a lawyer uses is in the application.
L19.8 Advisory mode — 'find me a way'
From a note or a matter, the lawyer asks for a way through. P1 produces, in this order and under these constraints: (1) what the law permits and what it  
forbids, each with numbered authority from the note or the corpus; (2) the strongest argument each side would make, with its best authority; (3) the  
procedural routes available and their limitation, from the Limitation Engine's dates; (4) the opening the system sees, stated as a view to be tested, with the 
authorities it rests on and the counter it expects. Every authority must resolve to a corpus id or a note citation; anything else is refused by the output  
validator. The result is saved as a note section 'Advisory (date)', classified with the matter, and is never sent to a client without a partner's approval. The 
mode is available to associates and partners; interns receive it read-only.
L19.9 Data model
Store Additions
Firestore — research_notes note as L19.1; firm_id; matter_ids[]; classification; origin; status  {suggested, active, archived}; template_version; proposals[] ∈
(judgment corpus id, score, targetCitationNo, status); alerts[]; run_log[]
Firestore — research_note_versions append-only versions (note_id, version, author, changed_fields, rendered_html, classification)
Firestore — research_config one document per firm: ranking weights, thresholds (ignore 50, consider 70, margin 8), maxInForce 12, suggestionMatch 0.85, 
suggestionArchiveDays 30; changed only by P6 on a confirmed proposal
Master knowledge (Postgres platform, P6) note_template (versioned) — the house format

L19.11 Privilege and data protection
1. Classification of a note = max(classification of linked matters); a note with no matter = confidential. Derived artefacts (renderings, exports,  
advisory output, WhatsApp replies) inherit it (L17.3, rank comparison).
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 11
=== PAGE 354 ===
2. Outbound to any provider: masked text only; the outbound-mask test of Erratum Set No. 1 Job F applies to every L19 call. Privileged and  
privileged-restricted: DPA provider or deterministic path, else ai.call.blocked.
3. Corpus queries carry: proposition embedding, section references, area, forum, as-at date. They never carry matter id, party names, note text or  
contentions. The connector strips anything else and logs a violation if it sees it.
4. Export and share obey the classification; a client-facing export of a note requires partner approval.
L19.12 Gates and tests (added to the forensic matrix as Tests 11–16)
# Test
11 Contention with a CNIC and two party names → outbound provider JSON contains neither; corpus query contains no names.
12 Proposition accepted → first run creates ≤ maxInForce numbered citations, each resolving to a corpus id; re-run with same key creates no duplicates.
13 New judgment scoring 78 against a note whose lowest in-force score is 66 → proposal raised, not applied; Accept → old citation in Removed with replacedBy set; 
Restore → back in force at N+1 and Removed entry marked restored.
14 Corpus treatment marks citation 3 overruled → alert on citation 3; a draft citing it is blocked by Gate 33 with reason OVERRULED.
15 Matter with s.12(2) CPC pleadings and no note → nightly job creates a Suggested note; nothing runs until accepted; after 30 days unaccepted → archived, not 
deleted.
16 Statute Viewer asked for s.489-F PPC as at 2015 → returns the 2015 version text; as at today → current; diff shows the 2018 amendment and its instrument.
Gate G-2 (Ingestion and Research) may not pass while any of Tests 11–16 fails. The seed-clone guard is extended: research_config must exist per firm  
with all six weights summing to 100.

=== PAGE 355 ===
Part C — Forensic audit of this layer, and the corrections applied
Method: the draft of Parts A–B was audited against Layer 17, Layer 18 and Erratum Set No. 1 for (i) contradiction of a locked decision, (ii) a function with 
two owners, (iii) a hidden placeholder or undefined value, (iv) a privacy or privilege leak, (v) a data-model conflict with Annex A, (vi) an untestable claim. 
Each finding below was corrected in the text above before this document was issued; the finding is recorded so the reader can verify the correction.
# Finding in the draft Correction applied
F-1 The draft put the research shelf 'in the corpus dashboard', which would have 
given the corpus service matter names and contentions, breaching D4 and 
L17.12.
Shelf, notes, advisory mode moved to the application (D5-1); corpus keeps only the 
two public viewers. L19.0.1(1), L19.7, L19.11 rewritten; connector strips and logs 
anything but anonymised query fields.
F-2 'Notes are refreshed and citations replaced automatically' — contradicted the 
partner's instruction that removed citations are kept and the L17.5 principle 
that learners propose.
L19.4: replacement is a proposal with Accept/Decline/Keep both; nothing applies 
without a tap; Removed list with restore; Test 13.
F-3 The note template was described as 'the partner's format' with no format 
defined — a placeholder by another name.
A concrete default house template is specified in L19.1 and is master knowledge 
written by P6; the partner's dictated format replaces it by proposal.
F-4 Ranking said 'relevance score' without a formula; thresholds absent — 
untestable.
Six weighted components with definitions, three thresholds and a margin, all as data 
in research_config; Test 13 asserts the numbers.
F-5 extractProposition was assigned to both D5-1 and D4-2 (matter intelligence) 
— a duplicate owner, the exact defect Layer 18 §3.2 removed.
D5-1 owns extraction and research; D4-2 owns only the nightly matter-notice job, 
which writes suggested notes and never runs research (L19.10).
F-6 A ninth WhatsApp command '/note' was added, breaking the eight-command 
grammar of Layer 18 §8.
Removed; research on WhatsApp uses /research <text>, /research notes, and the 
existing 'open n' reply (L19.0.1(8), L19.10).
F-7 statute structure was described as 'stored with the section' — would have 
required altering Annex A's statute_section table.
Additive statute_node table with a link from statute_section_version; no existing 
column changes; exposed through the connector mapping (L19.7, L19.9).
F-8 Advisory mode allowed 'general legal knowledge' authorities — an 
unverifiable citation path around Gate 33.
Output validator: every authority must resolve to a corpus id or a note citation; 
otherwise refused (L19.8).
F-9 Suggested notes from the nightly job were 'deleted if ignored for a month' — 
contradicts the no-deletion rule.
Archived, never deleted; Test 15.
F-10 The first research run had no idempotency key; a retried job would duplicate 
citations.
Key note_id:run_no; citations matched on corpus id; Test 12.
SIKANDER AI — Layer 19 · Research Notes and Viewers · 11 September 2026 · Page 13
=== PAGE 356 ===
# Finding in the draft Correction applied
F-11 Classification of a note was undefined for notes without a matter. Defaults to confidential; max of linked matters otherwise (L19.11).
F-13 The draft placed the full Citation and Statute Viewers on the corpus side, 
contrary to the founding decision that the scraper is only a database the 
application reads (Layer 17 D-1, Module 10).
Viewers moved to the application (D5-2); the corpus keeps an inspection screen only 
(L19.0.1(6), L19.7, L19.10).
F-12 'The system notices what the firm is working on' had no definition of what it 
reads or how it decides sameness.
Reads pleadings, orders, sections charged, relief sought; sameness = semantic match 
≥ 0.85 to an existing proposition, else a new suggestion (L19.2 B).
Residual items that only the firm can close: the partner's own note format (replaces the default by proposal); confirmation of the six ranking weights after 
the first month of use (a proposal with the run logs as evidence); whether interns may see the research shelf at all (default: read-only on notes of their  
matters).
End of Layer 19.
